这些文字背后是激动人心、引人入胜的故事——关于神与人，关于英雄壮举与悲剧，关于坚强与脆弱，关于世界的创造与城市的毁灭，所有这一切都由人类历史上最伟大的想象力之一所塑造。

Behind these words are exciting and engrossing tales—of gods and men, of heroic exploits and tragedy, of strength and frailty, of worlds created and cities destroyed, all given shape by one of the greatest flowerings of the imagination humanity has ever known.

阅读《神话中的词语》就像踏上一段令人眼花缭乱的发现之旅，去探索古希腊的辉煌——以及语言永恒的奇妙和魔力。

To read Words from the Myths is to enter into a dazzling voyage of discovery of the glory that was Greece—and the enduring wonder and magic of language.

“学术性强……但读起来却很轻松。求知欲强的人必读之作”—— 《共同体》

“Scholarly … yet reads easily. A boon for the curious mind” — Commonweal

ISAAC ASIMOV is generally regarded as one of this country’s leading writers of science and science fiction. He obtained his Ph.D. in chemistry from Columbia University and was Associate Professor of Biochemistry at Boston University School of Medicine. He is the author of over two hundred books, including The Chemicals of Life, The Genetic Code, The Human Body, The Human Brain, The Wellsprings of Life, and Words of Science, all available in Mentor editions.

介绍

INTRODUCTION

如果人类不对周围的世界感到好奇，那就称不上是人类了。几千年前，当人类还处于原始阶段时，他们一定曾躲在洞穴里向外张望，思考着眼前的一切。闪电是如何产生的？风从何而来？为什么冬天会很快到来，为什么所有的绿色植物都会枯萎？而到了来年春天，它们又为何会重新焕发生机？

HUMAN BEINGS wouldn’t be human if they didn’t wonder about the world about them. Many thousands of years ago, when mankind was still primitive, men must have looked out of caves and wondered about what they saw. What made the lightning flash? Where did the wind come from? Why would winter start soon and why would all the green things die? And then why did they all come back to life the next spring?

人类也曾思考过自身。为什么人类有时会生病？为什么所有人类最终都会衰老死亡？是谁首先教会了人类使用火和织布？

Man wondered about himself, too. Why did men get sick sometimes? Why did all men get old and die eventually? Who first taught men how to use fire and how to weave cloth?

当时有很多问题，但却没有答案。那是科学出现之前的时代；人类还没有学会通过实验来探究宇宙运行的规律和原因。

There were any number of questions but there were no answers. These were the days before science; before men had learned to experiment in order to determine the hows and whys of the universe.

早期人类所能做的，就是编造出看似最合乎逻辑的答案。狂风如同愤怒之人咆哮。然而，这风远比任何普通人的呼吸都强劲得多，而且自人类有记忆以来就一直吹拂着。因此，这风必定是由一位体型庞大、力量无比强大的人所创造，此人永生不死。这样一位超人的存在，便是“神”或“魔”。

What early man had to do was to invent what seemed to be the most logical answers. The raging wind was like the blowing of an angry man. The wind, however, was much stronger than the breath of any ordinary man and it had been blowing ever since man could remember. Therefore, the wind must be created by a tremendously huge and powerful man, one who never died. Such a superhuman being was a “god” or “demon.”

那闪电或许像是另一位神祇射出的威力巨大的致命长矛。既然箭矢能杀人，那么疾病也可能是另一位神祇射出的无形之箭所致。

The lightning seemed, perhaps, the huge, deadly spear of another god. Then, since arrows killed men, disease could be the result of invisible arrows fired by still another god.

既然男女结婚生子，那么世间万物或许就是天神（一位神）与大地女神（一位女神）的子女。滋润万物的雨水，便是他们之间的结合。

Since men and women married and had children, perhaps the green plants of the world were the children of the sky (a god) and the earth (a goddess). The gentle rain which made the plants grow was the marriage between them.

或许是一位掌管世间植物的女神，因某些不幸而发怒。她可能拒绝让植物生长，直到一切恢复正常。因此，绿色植物凋零，寒冬来临；也正因如此，当寒冬过后，春天到来，世界才再次焕发生机。

Perhaps a goddess was in charge of the plants of the world and grew angry because of some misfortune. She might have refused to let plants grow until things had been straightened out. That was why the green things died and winter came and that’s why the world grew green again when winter was over and spring came.

人类的各个族群都创作过这类故事；有些族群在这方面比其他族群更有趣、更巧妙。其中最杰出的当属古希腊人。他们生机勃勃、想象力丰富，拥有卓越的文学才能，创作出了许多引人入胜的此类故事。他们称这类故事为“mythos”，这是一个希腊词，意思就是“故事”或“传说”。如今，我们用“myth”一词来指代一种特定类型的故事：它包含奇幻或超自然的情节，旨在解释自然现象；或者讲述早期人类创造的神灵和恶魔的故事。

Every group of human beings made up such stories; and some groups were more interesting and clever at it than others. Foremost in excellence were the ancient Greeks. They were a lively, imaginative people with great literary talents, and they made up some of the most fascinating tales of this sort. They called such stories “mythos” which is a Greek word that simply means “tale” or “story.” We use the word “myth” nowadays to mean a particular kind of story; one which contains fanciful or supernatural incidents intended to explain nature, or one which deals with the gods and demons that were invented by early man.

希腊人非常重视神话。由于神祇掌控着自然之力，因此谨慎对待他们是明智之举。人们需要贿赂神祇，才能在需要时降雨，并恳求他们不要降下疾病或灾祸。正因如此，人们会向神祇献祭牲畜，为他们建造精美的神庙，并创作歌曲来赞美他们。就这样，一种围绕神话的宗教逐渐形成。

The Greeks took their myths very seriously. Since the gods controlled natural forces, it was wise to treat them with careful consideration. They had to be bribed to send rain when it was needed, and pleaded with not to send disease or misfortune. For that reason, animals were sacrificed to them, beautiful temples were built for them, songs were composed to praise them. Thus, a religion grew up about the myths.

一千多年来，古代世界的人们（我们的文明正是源于他们）一直信仰这种宗教。他们创作的伟大文学作品中充满了这种宗教的元素。他们以神话人物的名字命名星辰和行星。他们讲述着记忆模糊的祖先，并将他们描绘成诸神之子。他们以这些神裔“英雄”的名字为自己的孩子命名。

For over a thousand years, the people of the ancient world (from whom we inherit our civilization) believed in this religion. The great literature which they produced was filled with it. They named the stars and planets after characters in the myths. They told of dimly remembered ancestors whom they made the sons of the various gods and goddesses. They named their children after these god-descended “heroes.”

基督教建立之后，旧宗教逐渐消亡，欧洲人不再信仰古希腊罗马诸神。然而，这些神祇及其神话传说却在人们的记忆中流传了下来。古代文学也并未消亡，它们太过伟大，不容被遗忘。时至今日，我们依然阅读荷马的 《伊利亚特》和《奥德赛》，阅读希腊剧作家创作的伟大戏剧，阅读伊索寓言以及希腊罗马的历史和哲学著作。所有这些作品中都充满了神祇和神话传说。

After Christianity was established, the old religion died out and Europeans no longer believed in the old Greek and Roman gods. Those gods and the myths about them lived on in memory, however. The old literature did not die; it was too great to be allowed to die. We read Homer’s Iliad and Odyssey even today. We read the great plays written by the Greek dramatists. We read Aesop’s fables and the historical and philosophical works of the Greeks and Romans. All are full of the gods and the myths.

事实上，古希腊神话故事如此引人入胜，以至于即使在基督教传入之后，人们也只有了解这些故事才算真正受过教育。受过教育的人会将神话中的词汇融入日常用语，其中一些词汇甚至流传至今。正因如此，如今在包括英语在内的每一种欧洲语言中，都能找到希腊神话的痕迹。

In fact, the old Greek stories were so fascinating that even after Christianity was established, men did not consider themselves really educated unless and until they had learned those stories. Educated men introduced words from the myths into their speaking and some of those words remained in the language. For that reason, traces of the Greek myths are to be found today in every European tongue, including English.

例如，警车的信号灯是警笛，海牛是海牛。马戏团的管风琴是汽笛风琴；水母是水母；澳洲食蚁兽是针鼹。我们用洪亮的声音呼喊；聆听和蔼的导师或长着胡子的巢穴主人的教诲；鄙视专横跋扈的恶霸。

For instance, a police car’s signal is a siren and a sea cow is a sirenian. A circus organ is a calliope; a jelly-fish is a medusa; and an Australian anteater is an echidna. We call out in a stentorian voice; listen to a kindly mentor or a bearded nestor; despise a hectoring bully.

在上述所有例子中，我们都借鉴了希腊神话，其中塞壬是死亡陷阱，卡利俄珀是女神，美杜莎和厄喀德娜是可怕的怪物，而斯滕托尔、门托尔、涅斯托尔和赫克托尔是人。

In every case mentioned, we are drawing upon the Greek stories, where a Siren was a death trap, Calliope a goddess, Medusa and Echidna horrible monsters, and Stentor, Mentor, Nestor, and Hector were men.

科学家们尤其借鉴了古老的神话。直到不久前，拉丁语和希腊语还是各国学者的通用语言。当需要为某种新的动物、行星、化学物质或现象命名时，如果各国科学家都使用各自语言中的名称，那将非常不便。因此，人们逐渐养成了使用拉丁语或希腊语名称的习惯，以便所有国家都能使用。

Scientists, particularly, drew upon the old myths. Until quite recently, Latin and Greek were the common languages of learned men of all nations. When some new animal or planet or chemical or phenomenon needed to be named, it would have been inconvenient if scientists of each nationality used names drawn from their own language. The habit arose of giving a Latin or Greek name which all nationalities could use.

由于希腊神话广为人知，因此在需要时借用神话中的词语也就顺理成章了。例如，二战期间，人们首次利用核裂变技术分解铀时，在致命的放射性高温中发现了一种新元素。这种新元素被命名为“普罗米修斯”（Promethium），以纪念希腊神话中的普罗米修斯——他冒着太阳致命的放射性高温，为人类带来了火种。

Since the Greek myths are so well known it was natural to take words from those myths whenever they seemed to fit the situation. For instance, when uranium was first being broken down by fission during World War II, a new element was found amid the deadly radioactive heat. It was named “promethium” after Prometheus, a character in the Greek myths who dared the deadly radioactive heat of the sun to bring fire to mankind.

我们大多数人在童年时期就接触过希腊神话，但通常认为它们只是有趣的童话故事和冒险故事。然而，正如你所看到的，它们远不止于此。它们是我们文化的一部分，我们的许多语言，特别是科学语言，都源于它们。

Most of us are introduced to Greek myths in childhood but are taught to think of them only as interesting fairy tales and adventure stories. However, as you see, they are more than that. They are part of our culture, and much of our language, particularly our scientific language, is derived from them.

因此，在这本书中，我想再次回顾一些希腊神话，看看它们是如何演变成我们今天使用的词汇和表达方式的。我想展现这些流传了三千年的古老故事，是如何至今仍然渗透到我们日常生活中的。通过理解神话，我们将更好地了解自己。

What I would like to do, then, in this book, is to go over some of the Greek myths once again and see how they gave rise to words and expressions we use today. I want to show the manner in which these old, old stories, which have been living now for three thousand years, still penetrate our daily lives. By understanding the myths, we will understand ourselves better.

例如，让我们像希腊人那样，从头开始。

For instance, let’s begin, as the Greeks did, with the beginning.

1 开端

1 THE BEGINNING

希腊人认为，宇宙最初是由完全混合、混乱的物质构成的。万物没有任何特定的形状或形态。宇宙仅仅是一种尚未被创造出来的原材料。希腊人称这种原材料为混沌。

THE GREEKS imagined that, to begin with, the universe was composed of completely mixed and confused matter. Nothing had any particular shape or form. The universe was merely a raw material out of which nothing had yet been made. The Greeks called this raw material Chaos.

“混沌”一词在希腊语中意为“深渊”。“深渊”（chasm）一词也意为“深渊”，且与“混沌”同源。因此，最初的混沌可以被描绘成类似外太空的景象，但那时恒星和行星都尚未形成。一切都仅仅以某种旋转的透明蒸汽的形式存在。那时，似乎只有虚空，只有一片广袤无垠的深渊。（事实上，现代科学家认为，这才是宇宙最初的真正面貌。）

The word “chaos” means, in Greek, an open gulf. The word “chasm,” which means that also, comes from the same root as does chaos. The original chaos can be pictured, then, as something like outer space, but with none of the stars and planets formed yet. Everything existed merely as a kind of swirling and transparent vapor. Only emptiness, only a vast open gulf, would then seem to exist. (Actually, modern scientists think that is how the universe really did exist to begin with.)

我们今天仍然用“混乱”一词来表达任何处于绝望的混乱和无序状态的事物，即使它只是一个物品散落一地的房间。

We still use the word “chaos” today to express anything that is in hopeless confusion and disorder, even if it’s just a room with its contents scattered all about.

这个词以另一种更熟悉的形式流传至今，但这种形式几乎从未被人们所认识。

The word has come down to us in another and more familiar form, but one which is practically never recognized.

大约在1600年，一位名叫扬·巴普蒂斯塔·范·海尔蒙特的佛兰德化学家正在研究木炭燃烧时产生的蒸汽。他对果汁静置时形成的类似气泡的物质也很感兴趣。

About 1600, a Flemish chemist named Jan Baptista van Helmont was studying the vapors that were produced when charcoal was burned. He was interested also in the airlike bubbles formed when fruit juice was allowed to stand.

这些蒸汽和各种“气体”与化学家们通常处理的液体和固体截然不同。蒸汽本身没有固定的形状。当它被装在容器里时，容器看起来就像是空的。这种无形无态的物质正是混沌的体现。范·海尔蒙特决定用一个与“混沌”一词相关的词语来命名它。他的母语是弗拉芒语（荷兰语的一种方言），他根据弗拉芒语的发音将“混沌”（chaos）拼写为“gas”。

These vapors and different kinds of “airs” were not like the ordinary liquids and solids that chemists deal with. A vapor has no shape of its own. When it is inside a container, the container seems empty. Such a substance, without shape or form, is an example of chaos. Van Helmont decided to use a name for it that was suggested by the word. His language was Flemish (a dialect of Dutch) and he spelled “chaos” according to the Flemish pronunciation—“gas.”

如今汽车使用的燃料在被泵入油箱时是液态的。然而，在发动机内，它会汽化成气体。只有气体才能与空气混合，驱动活塞运动，从而带动发动机运转。由于这种液体极易汽化，因此被命名为“汽油”。美国人通常简称其为“gas”。

Now the fuel that is used in automobiles today is liquid as it is pumped into the fuel tank. In the engine, however, it vaporizes and turns into a gas. It is only a gas that can combine with air to drive the pistons and run the engine. Because the liquid turns into gas so easily, it is named “gasoline.” Americans usually call it “gas” for short.

所以，当我们“踩下油门”时，我们使用的这个词可以追溯到希腊语中表示宇宙最初状态的词。而当早高峰时段，太多司机同时踩下油门时，结果的确是混乱。

So when we “step on the gas” we are using a word that traces all the way back to the Greek word for the original state of the universe. And when, during a morning rush hour, too many motorists are stepping on the gas at the same time, the result is chaos indeed.

当有形有状的事物从混沌中诞生时，便形成了宇宙（Cosmos）。“宇宙”一词源于希腊语，意为“秩序”和“良好的安排”，因此它与混沌（Chaos）截然相反。如今，人们常常用“宇宙”（cosmos）来指代整个宇宙。此外，这个词在其他方面也发挥着作用。

When things with form and shape were created out of Chaos, the result was Cosmos. This is a Greek word meaning “order” and “good arrangement” and is therefore just the opposite of Chaos. It is quite common, nowadays, to refer to the universe as the “cosmos.” The word comes into play in other ways, too.

例如，1911年，人们发现了一种新型辐射，它似乎从四面八方轰击着地球。事实上，这种辐射似乎来自宇宙的各个角落，来自整个宇宙。因此，美国物理学家罗伯特·A·密立根在1925年建议将其称为“宇宙射线”，而这正是我们现在所熟知的名称。“宇宙”一词也意味着浩瀚无垠、至关重要，正如宇宙本身一样。“世界公民”指的是那些认为自己是整个世界的一部分，而不仅仅是其中一小部分的人。另一方面，像蚁穴这样的小世界就是一个“微观世界”，“微观”一词本身就意味着“小”。

For instance, in 1911, a new type of radiation was discovered, which seemed to be bombarding the earth from all sides. The radiation seemed to be coming from everywhere in the universe, from the whole cosmos, in fact. Therefore, the American physicist Robert A. Millikan suggested in 1925 that they be called “cosmic rays” and that is what they are now known as. “Cosmic” also means vast and all-important, like the universe itself and a “cosmopolitan” is one who considers himself to be part of the whole world rather than part of but one small section of it. On the other hand, a small world, such as an anthill is a “microcosm,” “micro” meaning “small.”

“宇宙”一词还有一种更为人熟知的含义。由于“宇宙”意味着美好的秩序，粉底、腮红、口红、睫毛膏以及其他使面容更加精致的化妆品都属于“化妆品”的范畴。的确，看到一些美容师的工作场景，你会觉得他们仿佛在用化妆品将混沌化作宇宙。

There is an even better-known trace of “cosmos.” Since cosmos means good arrangement, the powder, rouge, lipstick, mascara and other means of putting a face into better arrangement are “cosmetics.” And, sure enough, to see some beauticians at work does indeed make it look as though they are using makeup to form a Cosmos out of a Chaos.

在大多数神话体系中，最初从混沌中诞生的并非人类，而是神祇。神祇通常被描绘成拥有人类的外貌，尽管有时他们也可能部分或完全具有动物的特征。他们与人类的区别在于他们拥有远超人类的力量。他们可以掌控自然之力，例如操控太阳、释放闪电、掀起飓风、使植物生长或降下瘟疫。通常，他们被描绘成永生的神祇。

In most systems of myths, the first beings to be formed out of the original chaos are not men, but gods. Gods are usually pictured as having the appearance of men, though sometimes they are partly or entirely animal in appearance. They differ from men in that they are much more powerful. They can control the forces of nature. They can direct the sun, or hurl lightning, or lash the sea with hurricanes, or make the plants grow, or let loose plague. Usually, they are pictured as immortal.

在最广为人知的希腊神话版本中，最早从混沌中诞生的神灵是盖亚和乌拉诺斯。“盖亚”是希腊语中“大地”的意思，“乌拉诺斯”是希腊语中“天空”的意思，所以这就好比说，大地和天空是从最初的混沌中形成的。

In the most familiar form of the Greek myths, the first beings to come out of chaos were Gaia and Ouranos. Now “gaia” is the Greek word for “earth,” and “ouranos” is the Greek word for “sky,” so this is like saying that out of the original Chaos, Earth and Sky were formed.

希腊人将大地描绘成女性（我们自己也称大地母亲），因此盖亚不仅是物质世界的地球本身，也是一位象征大地的女神。她是“大地女神”。同样，乌拉诺斯是“天空之神”。

The Greeks pictured the earth as female (we ourselves speak of “mother Earth”) and so Gaia was not only the physical earth itself, but also a female god (a “goddess”) that symbolized the earth. She was the “goddess of the earth.” In the same way, Ouranos was the “god of the sky.”

最终，当讲拉丁语的罗马人征服希腊后，他们被希腊人的生活方式深深吸引，并尽可能地加以吸收。例如，他们对希腊的诸神非常感兴趣。然而，在书写这些神祇的名字时，他们却采用了自己的一套拼写系统。拉丁字母与希腊字母不同，为了保持正确的发音，他们不得不改变字母的组合。

Eventually, when the Latin-speaking Romans conquered Greece, they were fascinated by the Greek way of life and adopted as much of it as they could. They were very interested in the Greek gods and goddesses, for instance. However, in writing of these gods and goddesses, they spelled the names according to a system of their own. The Latin alphabet was different from that of the Greeks and to keep the correct pronunciation, they had to change the letter combinations.

例如，拉丁字母中没有字母“k”，所以希腊人使用“k”的地方，如“kosmos”，罗马人则用“c”代替，如“cosmos”。希腊人使用“ou”和“ai”的地方，罗马人通常用“u”和“ae”代替。希腊人的名字经常以“os”结尾，但罗马人几乎总是将其改为“us”。

For instance, there is no “k” in the Latin alphabet so wherever the Greeks used a “k,” as in “kosmos,” the Romans used a “c” as in “cosmos.” Where the Greeks used “ou” and “ai,” the Romans usually substituted “u” and “ae.” The Greeks often ended names with “os,” but the Romans almost always changed that to “us.”

因此，希腊大地女神的罗马拼写是盖亚（Gaea），而希腊天空之神的名字则是乌拉诺斯（Uranus）。

Thus the Roman spelling of the name of the Greek goddess of the earth is Gaea, and for the Greek god of the sky, Uranus.

现代英语名字通常遵循罗马字母而非希腊字母，因为我们的字母表与拉丁字母几乎相同。然而，我们常常会简化名字，例如去掉词尾的“us”，或者将词尾的“ius”改为“y”。本书中，我将使用拉丁字母的英文拼写。

Our modern English names generally follow the Roman spelling rather than the Greek, because our alphabet is almost the same as the Latin alphabet. Often, however, we simplify the names by dropping the final “us,” or changing a final “ius” to a final “y.” In this book, I will use the English versions of the Latin spellings.

然而，我们对字母组合的发音与罗马人的发音并不相同。例如，希腊语中的“Gaia”和拉丁语中的“Gaea”都发音为“Gay'yuh”，而我们则发音为“Jee'uh”。同样，希腊语中的“Ouranos”和拉丁语中的“Uranus”都发音为“Oo'-ruh-nus”，而我们则发音为“You'ruh-nus”。本书中，我会在必要时给出这些名称的英语发音，但我们必须记住，这并非古人对这些词的发音。

However, our pronunciation of letter combinations is not the same as the Roman pronunciation. For instance, “Gaia” in Greek and “Gaea” in Latin are both pronounced “Gay’yuh.” We, however, pronounce it “Jee’uh.” Again the Greek “Ouranos” and the Latin “Uranus” are both pronounced “Oo’-ruh-nus” but we pronounce it “You’ruh-nus.” In this book, I will give the English pronunciation of the names when that seems necessary, but we must remember that this is not always the way the ancients pronounced the word.

罗马人进一步将自己的神祇与希腊的神祇等同起来。也就是说，他们会假定自己的一位神祇与某个希腊神祇是同一位，然后用自己的名字替换希腊名字。由于许多现代欧洲语言都源于拉丁语，因此拉丁语名称最终比希腊语名称更为人熟知。

The Romans further identified their own gods and goddesses with those of the Greeks. That is, they would suppose one of their gods or goddesses was the same as a particular Greek god or goddess. Then they would substitute their own name for the Greek one. Since many modern European languages developed out of Latin, the Latin names ended up more familiar to us than the Greek names.

例如，罗马大地女神盖亚有两个名字：泰拉（Terra）和特鲁斯（Tellus）。这两个名字都与盖亚（Gaea）相对应，而且在英语中，这两个名字的使用频率都高于盖亚（Gaea）。因此，在科幻小说中，人类可能会被称为“地球人”（Earthling）、“地球人”（Terrestrial）或“地球人”（Tellurian），但绝不会被称为“盖亚人”（Gaean）。同样，来自其他星球的生物几乎总是被称为“外星人”（extra-terrestrial），其中“extra”是拉丁语中“外部”的意思。

For instance, the Roman goddess of the earth had two names, Terra and Tellus. Both of these were identified with Gaea, and both are more often used in English than Gaea is. Thus, in science-fiction stories, a human being may be called an “Earthling,” a “Terrestrial” or a “Tellurian,” but never a “Gaean.” Again, a being from another planet is almost always called an “extra-terrestrial,” where “extra” is the Latin word for “outside.”

Terra一词也出现在一些更熟悉的词语中。隆起的土坡是“梯田”，我们周围的地貌形状是“地形”。一个国家存在的土地是它的“领土”。

Terra appears in more familiar words, too. A raised bank of earth is a “terrace” and the shape of the landscape about us is the “terrain.” The tract of land over which a nation exists is its “territory.”

地球留下的痕迹并不多，但其中一个重要的痕迹却在化学领域。1798年，一位名叫马丁·海因里希·克拉普罗特的德国化学家为一种十五年前发现的新元素提出了一个名称。他之前已经用天空命名了一种元素，因此他觉得也应该用类似的词语来象征地球。他选择了“Tellus”来代表地球，并将这种元素命名为“碲”（tellurium）。

Tellus has not left as many traces, but one important one is to be found in chemistry. In 1798, a German chemist named Martin Heinrich Klaproth suggested a name for a new element that had been discovered fifteen years earlier. He had already named an element for the sky, and he felt he ought to give the earth similar credit. He chose Tellus to symbolize the earth and he named the element “tellurium.”

然而，盖亚并没有被完全遗忘。许多英语单词都带有前缀“geo-”，以提醒我们这位古老的女神。

However, Gaea is not entirely forgotten. There are a number of English words that have the prefix “geo-” to remind us of that ancient goddess.

其中最广为人知的包括“地理学”（“地球的描述”）、“地质学”（“地球科学”）和“几何学”（“地球的测量”）。事实上，几何学的最初用途就是勘测或测量地球，以便确定农场的边界。

The most familiar of these are “geography” (“description of the earth”), “geology” (“science of the earth”), and “geometry” (“measurement of the earth”). The first use of geometry was, in fact, the surveying or measuring of the earth, in order that the boundaries of farms might be determined.

再次强调，古希腊人认为地球是宇宙的中心，太阳、月亮和行星都围绕地球运转的理论被称为“地心说”。

Again, the Greek theory that the earth was at the center of the universe and that the sun, moon and planets revolve about it is called the “geocentric theory.”

研究地球表面和内部的物理过程，例如地球的热量、磁场、海洋中的洋流运动或空气中的风，就是“地球物理学”。因此，从1957年7月1日至1958年12月31日的“国际地球物理年”（也是第一批人造卫星发射升空期间）就提到了盖亚。

The study of the physical processes on and within the earth, such as its heat or magnetism or the movement of currents in the sea or winds in the air, is “geophysics.” Thus, the “International Geophysical Year” which ran from July 1, 1957, to December 31, 1958, and during which the first man-made satellites were launched, contains a reference to Gaea.

类似的词语都与天王星有关。“天王星学”是对天空星座的描述，“天王星学”是研究天空的科学（不过，更常用的词是“天文学”）。

Similar words are based on Uranus. “Uranography” is the description of the constellations of the sky and “uranology” is the science of the sky (for which the more common word, however, is “astronomy”).

然而，天王星在现代科学中享有的盛名，是任何一位地球女神都无法比拟的。1781年，一位出生于德国的英国天文学家威廉·赫歇尔发现了一颗比之前已知的任何行星都更遥远的新行星。在此之前，所有已知的行星都是天空中明亮的天体，很容易被观测到，而且早在史前时代就已被人们所认识。然而，这颗新发现的行星却非常暗淡，几乎肉眼无法辨认。

Uranus, however, gained a fame in modern science that none of the Earth goddesses could match. In 1781, a German-born English astronomer, William Herschel, discovered a new planet more distant than any that were known before. Until that date, all the planets that were known were bright objects in the sky that were easily seen and that had been recognized in prehistoric times. The new planet was a very faint object, however, barely visible to the naked eye.

赫歇尔原本想将这颗新行星命名为“乔治之星”（Georgium Sidus），以纪念当时的英国国王乔治三世。其他天文学家则建议将其命名为“赫歇尔星”，以纪念发现者赫歇尔。

Herschel wanted to call the new planet “Georgium Sidus” (which is Latin for “George’s star”) after George III, who was then King of England. Other astronomers suggested it be named “Herschel” after the discoverer.

这两个提议都没有被采纳。其他行星都以古代神祇的名字命名，这一惯例得以延续。这颗新行星最终被命名为天王星，这是德国天文学家约翰·波德的建议。

Neither suggestion was adopted. All the other planets had been named after ancient gods and goddesses and the custom was kept. The new planet was named Uranus at the suggestion of a German astronomer named Johann Bode.

新行星的发现引起了轰动，天王星也因此声名鹊起，许多从未听说过或已经遗忘希腊神话的人都开始谈论他的名字。然而，在天王星被发现几年后，发生了一件事，最终使这位神祇的名字更加引人注目。

The discovery of the new planet created quite a sensation and Uranus thus gained a new fame that brought his name to the lips of many men who had never heard, or who had forgotten, the Greek myth. However, something happened a few years after the discovery of Uranus which, eventually, made the god’s name even more sensational.

1789年，克拉普罗特（我前面提到的那位化学家）发现了一种新金属。中世纪的化学家们有个古老的习惯，就是用天上的行星来命名金属。克拉普罗特觉得他应该用这颗新发现的行星来命名他的新金属，所以他把这种金属命名为“铀”（后来为了平衡，他又把另一种元素命名为碲，正如我前面提到的）。

In 1789, Klaproth (the chemist I mentioned a bit earlier) discovered a new metal. Now it had been an old-fashioned habit of the chemists of the Middle Ages to name metals after the planets in the sky. Klaproth felt he ought to name his new metal after the new planet, so he called the metal “uranium” (and later named another element tellurium, to balance matters, as I said above).

如今，由于原子弹的研制成功，铀这个词可谓家喻户晓。希腊神话中最古老的神祇，如今依然与这种最新、最可怕的科学武器紧密相连。

Nowadays, as the result of the development of the atomic bomb, the word uranium is famous indeed. The oldest of the Greek gods still lives in a word connected with the newest and most dreadful of scientific weapons.

古希腊人将天王星和盖亚视为夫妻，他们孕育子女。想象一下，雨水从天而降（天王星），落到大地（盖亚）上，滋养万物生长，你就能明白，希腊人在谈论婚姻和子女时，正是以此为象征。

Uranus and Gaea were considered by the ancient Greeks as man and wife, giving birth to children. Picture the rain falling from the sky (Uranus) to the earth (Gaea) and bringing about the growth of plants, and you can see that the Greeks were symbolizing this when they talked of marriage and children.

这对古代夫妇的许多孩子都是体型巨大、力量惊人的凶猛生物，被称为巨人（Gigantes），或者用英语来说就是“巨人”。这些可能代表了自然界的破坏力量。

Many of the children of this ancient couple were ferocious beings of tremendous size and power, called Gigantes, or, in English, “giants.” These probably represented the destructive forces of nature.

我们从这些巨人那里衍生出了“巨大”一词，用来形容任何体积非常大的事物。医生称，因激素紊乱而长到异常高大的人患有“巨人症”。马戏团里的巨人通常都患有这种疾病。

From these giants, we get the word “gigantic” for anything of very large size. A person who grows to unusual heights because of hormone troubles is said, by doctors, to suffer from “gigantism.” Circus giants are usually sufferers of this disease.

许多巨人不仅体型庞大，而且面目狰狞。有些巨人被描绘成拥有百臂，有些巨人则额头正中长着一只炯炯有神的眼睛。这些巨人被称为独眼巨人，在希腊语中意为“圆眼”。据说他们在火山的熔炉中工作，那里常常传来神秘的隆隆声，熔岩和火山灰偶尔会从中喷涌而出——希腊人认为这是由于熔炉的火焰过高造成的。独眼巨人或许象征着整个火山，因为火山顶部有一个火山口，就像一只独眼凝视着苍穹。

Many of the giants were monstrous as well as large. Some were pictured as having a hundred arms. Others possessed a single, staring eye in the middle of the forehead. These were called Cyclops, which in Greek means “round-eyed.” They were supposed to work in forges in volcanoes, from whence mysterious rumblings could often be heard and from which molten rock and ashes occasionally shot out when, the Greeks supposed, the fires of the forge grew too high. The Cyclops might well symbolize volcanoes altogether, since the volcano has a crater at the top, like a single eye, staring at heaven.

独眼巨人也被认为能够制造闪电，因此他们最终也象征着这种毁灭性的力量。事实上，由天王星和盖亚所生的最初三个独眼巨人分别名为布朗特斯（Brontes）、斯特罗佩斯（Steropes）和阿尔格斯（Arges），这三个词在希腊语中分别意为“雷霆”、“闪电”和“光明”。这说明，当雨水从天王星降临盖亚时，有时会伴随雷霆和闪电，并带来光明。布朗特斯有时也指铁匠。

The Cyclops were also supposed to manufacture the lightning bolts, so that they eventually came to symbolize that form of destructive power as well. In fact, the first three Cyclops born of Uranus and Gaea, were named Brontes, Steropes, and Arges, which are the Greek words for “thunder,” “lightning,” and “brightness.” This is one way of saying that when rain descends from Uranus to Gaea, thunder and lightning are sometimes born of the process and a brightness results. Brontes is sometimes used as a term for a blacksmith.

据说独眼巨人建造了迈锡尼和梯林斯的城墙，这两座城市在希腊早期是最强大的城市。然而，当希腊鼎盛时期，这两座城市却只剩下残垣断壁。希腊人不禁疑惑，为何城墙如此巨大，由巨大的岩石砌成，且未使用任何砂浆。他们最终认定，只有像独眼巨人那样的巨人才能堆砌出如此雄伟的城墙。

The Cyclops were supposed to have built the walls of Mycenae and Tiryns, two cities that were the most powerful of all in the very early days of Greece. When Greece was at its height, however, those two cities were merely ruined villages and the Greeks then wondered how they came to have such huge walls made of tremendous rocks fitted together without mortar. They decided that only giant beings, like the Cyclops, could have piled those rocks.

因此，即使在今天，用大石头砌成的、没有砂浆的墙仍然被称为“独眼巨人式的”。事实上，这个词是“巨大的”的同义词，只是不太常用。然而，“独眼巨人”这个名字却用来指一种体长不超过20毫米的小型水蚤。当然，它得名并非因为体型小，而是因为它的头部中央似乎长着一只眼睛。（实际上，它的眼睛是双眼。）

As a result, even today, walls made of large rocks without mortar are called “cyclopean.” In fact, the word is a less familiar synonym of “gigantic.” Yet Cyclops is a name given to a tiny water flea not more than a twentieth of an inch long. The name isn’t given it because of its size of course, but because it seems to have a single eye in the middle of its head. (Actually, though, the eye is double.)

2 泰坦

2 THE TITANS

乌拉诺斯和盖亚的众多巨型后代中，最重要的是一群被称为泰坦和泰坦女神的生物，他们既有男性也有女性。由于希腊人通常（但不总是）将这些生物描绘成体型庞大的巨人，因此“泰坦”一词逐渐成为“巨人”的同义词。形容为“巨大的”事物也可以被形容为“泰坦般的”。

THE MOST IMPORTANT of the gigantic offspring of Uranus and Gaea were a group of creatures, both male and female, who were called Titans and Titanesses. Since the Greeks presented these usually (but not always) as tremendous giants, the word “titan” has become synonymous with “giant.” Something which is described as “gigantic” may also be described as “titanic.”

1911年，一艘当时最大的巨轮下水。为了彰显其庞大的体型，它被命名为“泰坦尼克号”。人们认为它如此巨大、如此先进，以至于永不沉没。事实上，建造者和船东对它的永不沉没深信不疑，以至于他们根本不在乎救生艇、救生衣、应急演习等等。

In 1911, a giant ship, the largest in existence at that time, was launched. To express its size, it was named the Titanic. It was thought to be so large and so advanced as to be unsinkable. In fact, so certain were the builders and owners of its unsinkability that they didn’t bother very much with such things as lifeboats, life preservers, emergency drills and so on.

1912年4月14日，这艘号称永不沉没的巨轮在首航途中撞上冰山，不到三个小时便沉没。船上2206名乘客中，超过1500人溺水身亡。这是历史上最惨重的海难。

On April 14, 1912, on its very first voyage, it struck an iceberg. The supposedly unsinkable ship sank in less than three hours. Of the 2206 passengers, over 1500 drowned. It was the greatest ship disaster in all history.

或许，如果庄园主人了解神话，更加迷信一些，他们就不会取这样狂妄的名字了。正如我将在本书后面解释的那样，希腊人认为这种傲慢必将招致毁灭。此外，神话中泰坦巨人最终也难逃灭亡，所以迷信的人或许会认为，使用他们的名字是不祥之兆。

Perhaps if the owners had known mythology and been more superstitious they might have avoided such a boastful name. That was the kind of pride which the Greeks believed was sure to be followed by destruction, as I shall explain later in the book. Besides, the Titans themselves underwent complete destruction in the myths, so to use their name may have been a bad omen, superstitious people might think.

克拉普罗特再次涉足化学领域，他曾将天王星和泰鲁斯（罗马神话中的盖亚）的名字引入元素周期表，如今又将它们的儿子——泰坦神——的名字也纳入其中。1791年，一位名叫威廉·格雷戈尔的英国牧师发现了一种新金属。克拉普罗特建议将其命名为“钛”，这一提议被采纳。

Turning to chemistry again, Klaproth, who had introduced the names of Uranus and of Tellus (the Roman Gaea) into the list of elements, did the same for their sons, the Titans. In 1791, a new metal had been discovered by an English clergyman named William Gregor. Klaproth’s suggestion that it be named “titanium” was accepted.

事实上，这个名字纯属巧合，却非常贴切。钛在未提纯时质地较脆，几乎毫无用处。然而，近年来，化学家们成功制备出了纯度极高的钛，而这种钛正是已知最坚硬的金属。钛这个名字恰如其分地体现了它钢铁般的强度。

As a matter of fact, that name turns out, quite by coincidence, to be a good one. When titanium is impure it is rather brittle and useless. Recently, however, chemists have succeeded in preparing titanium in quite pure form, and it then turns out to be the strongest metal known. The name titanium fits its titanic strength.

一些体型巨大的史前动物也以神话中的巨人命名。例如，有一种恐龙被称为“泰坦龙”（titanosaurus，意为“泰坦巨蜥”），还有一种叫做“巨蜥”（gigantosaurus，意为“巨蜥”），以及一种叫做“雷龙”（brontosaurus，意为“雷霆巨蜥”）的恐龙，后者让人联想到我之前提到的独眼巨人之一——布朗特斯。此外，还有一种古代巨型犀牛被称为“泰坦巨兽”（titanotherium，意为“泰坦巨兽”）。

Some prehistoric animals of great size are also named for these giants of mythology. There is a type of dinosaur called “titanosaurus” (“titanic lizard”) and, for that matter, one called “gigantosaurus” (“giant lizard”) and another called “brontosaurus” (“thunder lizard”) which recalls Brontes, one of the Cyclops I mentioned earlier. There is also an ancient species of giant rhinoceros called “titanotherium” (“titanic beast”).

泰坦中最强大的当属克洛诺斯。他领导泰坦们反抗他们的父亲乌拉诺斯。盖亚厌倦了不断生育巨人和怪物，于是她给了克洛诺斯一把镰刀，克洛诺斯用这把镰刀攻击乌拉诺斯，并将他驱逐。

The most powerful of the Titans was Cronus. He led a revolt of the Titans against their father, Uranus. Gaea had grown tired of giving birth to giants and monsters. Consequently, she armed Cronus with a scythe and with this he attacked Uranus and drove him away.

希腊人或许以此象征他们相信天地原本紧密相连。克洛诺斯和他的兄弟泰坦象征着太阳、月亮和行星，它们将天地分开，占据了其间广袤的空间。宇宙由此最终形成了它现在的形态。

In this manner, the Greeks may have symbolized their belief that originally earth and sky were close together. Cronus and his brother Titans symbolized the sun, moon and planets which drove earth and sky apart and took over the vast space between. In this way, the universe was put into its final shape.

“克洛诺斯”一词并非源自希腊语，很可能是希腊人到来之前居住在希腊的居民遗留下来的词汇。人们很容易认为这个陌生的词“克洛诺斯”实际上是熟悉的希腊语单词“克洛诺斯”，意思是“时间”。

The word Cronus is not Greek in its origin and is probably a hangover from those who inhabited Greece before the Greeks themselves arrived. It was easy to suppose that the unfamiliar word, Cronus, was actually the familiar Greek word “Chronos,” meaning “time.”

因此，克洛诺斯经常被认为是“时间之神”。即使在今天，“时间之父”通常被描绘成一位手持镰刀的老人，镰刀正是克洛诺斯用来击败乌拉诺斯的武器。这是克洛诺斯在现代最常见的形象，但这种形象是错误的。（老妇人有时被称为“crone”，但这与克洛诺斯无关。它源自一个古老的丹麦语词汇，两者相似纯属巧合。）

For that reason, Cronus was frequently considered to be the “god of time.” Even today, “Father Time” is usually pictured as an old man carrying a scythe, the weapon with which Cronus defeated Uranus. That is the most familiar way in which Cronus lingers on into modern times and it is a wrong one. (An old woman is sometimes called a “crone” but this has no connection with Cronus. It comes from an old Danish word and the similarity is a coincidence.)

在希腊人之前的民族眼中，克洛诺斯很可能是一位农业之神。据说他用来攻击父亲乌拉诺斯的镰刀，最初只是收割谷物的工具，并无其他致命之处。

To the people who preceded the Greeks, Cronus was probably a god of agriculture. The scythe with which he was supposed to have attacked his father, Uranus, was originally a tool for harvesting grain, nothing more deadly than that.

罗马人信奉农业之神萨图恩，并将他与克洛诺斯等同起来。每年12月19日至26日，罗马人会举行为期一周的庆祝活动来祭祀萨图恩，这个节日被称为“农神节”（Saturnalia）。节日期间，人们欢聚一堂，享用丰盛的宴席，并互赠礼物。如今，我们仍然会在同一时间庆祝圣诞节，而这种节日氛围也保留了部分下来。

The Romans had a god of agriculture called Saturn, and they identified him with Cronus. The Romans honored Saturn during a week-long celebration from the 19th to the 26th of December. This celebration was called the “Saturnalia.” There was great jollity, much feasting, and an exchange of presents. Some of the atmosphere lingers on into our modern celebration of Christmas at the same time of year.

然而，庆祝活动中出现了太多的狂野和醉酒行为，以至于如今“saturnalia”一词就指狂野、醉酒的派对。

However, so much wildness and drunkenness developed during the celebration that nowadays the word “saturnalia” means a wild, drunken party.

希腊人将克洛诺斯与第六颗行星（如果将地球也算作行星之一，即第三颗）联系起来。这可能是因为在希腊人所知的行星中，第六颗行星距离地球最远，因此也最接近天空的边缘。或许是因为它似乎在攻击天空之神（天王星），所以才被恰如其分地命名为克洛诺斯？

The Greeks associated Cronus with the sixth planet (counting our earth as one of the planets, the third). This may be because, of the planets known to the Greeks, the sixth planet was farthest from the earth and therefore the nearest to the outer limits of the sky. Could it be that it seemed to be leading the attack against the god of the sky (Uranus) and was therefore rightly named Cronus?

另一种可能性是这样的。第六行星也是希腊人所知的所有行星中距离太阳最远的行星。在这个距离上，太阳的引力很弱，第六行星的运行速度也比希腊人所知的任何其他行星都要慢。它的运行速度如此之慢，以至于在星空背景下绕天一周需要29年半的时间。如此缓慢而庄严的运行，或许会让希腊人觉得它应该由一位古老的神祇来象征。克洛诺斯似乎是顺理成章的选择。

Another possibility is this. The sixth planet is also the farthest planet from the sun of all the planets known to the Greeks. The sun’s gravitation is weak at that distance and the sixth planet moves more slowly than any other planet the Greeks knew. It moves so slowly that it takes 29 1/2 years to make a complete circuit of the sky against the background of the stars. With such a slow and majestic movement, it might have seemed to the Greeks that it ought to be symbolized by an old, old god. Cronus seemed the natural choice.

罗马人自然而然地将这颗行星称为“土星”，这个名字也一直沿用至今。

The Romans naturally called the planet “Saturn” and that is the name that has come down to us.

土星赤道附近有一圈细细的光环。当然，古人对此一无所知，因为这些光环肉眼不可见，必须借助望远镜才能观测到。它们于1655年被发现，同年，土星的九颗卫星中的第一颗也被发现。这颗卫星是第六颗（从土星向外数），被命名为“泰坦”（Titan）。这个名字其实不太合适，因为它并非某个神的名字，而是一群神的名字。

Saturn has a thin set of rings encircling it about its equator. The ancients didn’t know this, of course, since the rings are invisible without a telescope. They were discovered in 1655, and in that same year the first of Saturn’s nine satellites was discovered. This satellite was number six (counting outward from Saturn) and was named “Titan.” This is a poor name, really, because it isn’t the name of a god, but of a whole group of gods.

土星其余的大部分卫星都以泰坦神和泰坦女神的名字命名，因为将它们与天上的土星联系起来似乎很合适。因此，第七颗和第八颗卫星分别以泰坦神许珀里翁和伊阿珀托斯命名。第三、四、五和第九颗卫星则分别以泰坦女神忒提斯、狄俄涅、瑞亚和福柏命名。1905年，曾有报道称发现了一颗卫星，并以一位后来被认为是正义女神的泰坦女神忒弥斯命名。但后来证明这一发现是错误的，据我们所知，并不存在这样一颗卫星。

Most of the remaining satellites of Saturn were named after individual Titans and Titanesses, for it seemed fitting to associate them with Saturn in the heavens. Thus, satellites seven and eight were named Hyperion and Iapetus after Titans. Satellites three, four, five and nine were named Tethys, Dione, Rhea, and Phoebe after Titanesses. In 1905, a satellite was reported as discovered and was named Themis, after a Titaness who was later considered the goddess of justice. The discovery proved to be a mistake and, as far as we know, no such satellite exists.

在土星卫星上所有被纪念的泰坦女神中，瑞亚最为重要。她是克洛诺斯的妻子，最初也与农业相关，因此她也是大地女神的另一种化身。正如我稍后将解释的，她是希腊人崇拜的主要神祇的母亲，因此被称为“众神之母”，备受尊崇。（不知何故，她的名字被用来指代一群类似鸵鸟的鸟类，即“美洲鸵”，它们生活在南美洲。）

Of all the Titanesses memorialized among Saturn’s satellites, Rhea was the most important. She was the wife of Cronus and since she was also associated with agriculture originally, she is another version of the earth goddess. She was the mother, as I shall explain later, of the chief gods and goddesses actually worshiped by the Greeks, so she was called “The Mother of the Gods” and was held in high honor. (For some reason, her name has been given to a group of ostrichlike birds, the “rheas,” who are to be found in South America.)

大约公元前200年，罗马人接纳了库柏勒女神，她最初在小亚细亚被奉为农业女神。罗马人将她与瑞亚女神等同起来，并尊称她为“Magna Mater”（伟大的母亲）或“Alma Mater”（养育之母）。如今，“Alma Mater”一词用来指代男性的大学，象征着大学如同养育之母一般，给予他们滋养和庇护。

About 200 B.C. the Romans took over a goddess named Cybele who was originally worshiped in Asia Minor as a goddess of agriculture. The Romans identified her with Rhea and worshiped her as “Magna Mater” (“Great Mother”) or “Alma Mater” (“Fostering Mother”). That phrase “alma mater” is now applied to a man’s college, which has been a fostering mother, one that feeds and protects, to his mind.

并非所有泰坦神和泰坦女神都被铭刻在土星的卫星上。泰坦女神忒提斯肯定在那里，但她的丈夫俄刻阿诺斯却不在那里。

Not all the Titans and Titanesses are memorialized among the satellites of Saturn. Tethys, the Titaness, is there, to be sure, but her husband, Oceanus, is not.

俄刻阿诺斯是泰坦神中最古老的一位，象征着环绕世界陆地的水域。当然，希腊人并不确定水域是否环绕了所有陆地。他们只熟悉地中海及其周边地区，也就是南欧、北非和西亚。

Oceanus was the oldest of the Titans and symbolized the water that encircled the land of the world. The Greeks weren’t sure that water encircled all the land, of course. They were only familiar with the Mediterranean Sea and the land about it. That means southern Europe, northern Africa and western Asia.

在四个方向中的三个方向，他们所见的只有连绵不断的陆地（这就是为什么欧洲、亚洲和非洲，以及任何大片陆地都被称为“大陆”）。然而，地中海向西延伸，直至我们今天所称的直布罗陀海峡。在那里，欧洲和非洲几乎相连，但并未完全相接。海峡之外，是一片浩瀚的开阔水域。

In three of the four directions, they knew only of continuous land (which is why Europe, Asia and Africa, and any large stretch of land is called a “continent”). However, the Mediterranean Sea stretched off westward until it came to what we call today the Strait of Gibraltar. There, Europe and Africa almost meet, but not quite. And beyond it is a vast stretch of open water.

这条水似乎向南北流淌很远，在希腊人看来，将其安排成一条巨大的环形河流，占据一个饼状世界的边缘，似乎是一个简洁而令人满意的安排。

This water seemed to run far north and south and to the Greeks it seemed to be a neat and satisfactory arrangement to have it in the form of a tremendous circular river occupying the rim of a pie-plate world.

然而，由于他们所知的世界之河只有一部分位于遥远的西方，他们便想象俄刻阿诺斯和忒提斯居住在那里。事实上，古希腊人将他们的大部分奇观都安置在了遥远的西方。

However, since the only part of this world-river they actually knew about lay in the far west, they imagined Oceanus and Tethys to live out there. In fact, the ancient Greeks put most of their wonders in the far west.

当然，随着人类对世界的了解加深，人们发现那条“世界之河”根本不是河流，而是一片广袤的咸水海，类似于地中海，但面积要大得多。然而，这位泰坦神的名字却流传了下来，因为我们自然而然地将如此广阔的水域称为“海洋”。

Of course as men grew to know the world better, it was found that the world-river was not a river at all, but a vast stretch of open salt water like the Mediterranean but much larger. The name of the Titan lingers on, however, for of course we call such a large stretch of water an “ocean.”

希腊人关于世界之河的说法在某一方面被证明是正确的。水的确环绕着欧洲、亚洲和非洲，但除此之外还有其他大片陆地。有北美洲和南美洲、南极洲、澳大利亚以及众多较小的岛屿。这些陆地分割了连续的咸水区域，因此我们现在所说的五大洋才有了。

The Greeks turned out to be right in one respect about the world-river. Water does indeed extend all around Europe, Asia and Africa, but there are other large tracts of land besides. There is North and South America, Antarctica, Australia, and a host of smaller islands. These break up the continuous stretch of salt water, so that now we speak of five different oceans.

由于新几内亚和数千个较小的岛屿被最大的海洋——太平洋所环绕，它们被统称为“大洋洲”。因此，这片希腊人从未见过的土地，以他们想象中的泰坦巨人的名字命名。

Because New Guinea and thousands of smaller islands are surrounded by the largest of the oceans, the Pacific Ocean, they are lumped together under the name of “Oceania.” Thus, land the Greeks never saw is named after the Titan of their imagination.

至于俄刻阿诺斯的妻子忒提斯，除了是土星的卫星之外，她还以一种不太寻常的方式被纪念。科学家们追溯地球的过去历史发现，陆地和海洋的格局并非一直如此。数亿年前，曾有一片广阔的水域，横跨亚洲，从大西洋延伸到太平洋。地中海正是这片古老水域的现代遗迹。奥地利地质学家爱德华·苏斯将其命名为“忒提斯海”。

As for Tethys, the wife of Oceanus, she has received her memorial (aside from being a satellite of Saturn) in a less common way. Scientists, tracing the past history of the earth, have learned that the pattern of land and sea was not always the same. Some hundreds of millions of years ago, there was a large stretch of water, running from the Atlantic to the Pacific Ocean across Asia. The Mediterranean Sea is a modern remnant of that ancient stretch of water. The Austrian geologist Eduard Suess named it the “Tethys Sea.”

在所有泰坦神中，最著名的或许要数阿特拉斯了。他是否是第二代泰坦神尚无定论。一些古希腊作家称他是乌拉诺斯的儿子，克洛诺斯、俄刻阿诺斯、伊阿珀托斯和其他泰坦神的兄弟；另一些作家则称他是伊阿珀托斯的儿子，乌拉诺斯的孙子，也就是说他是克洛诺斯和俄刻阿诺斯的侄子。

Perhaps the best known of all the Titans is Atlas. He may or may not have been a second-generation Titan. That is, some of the ancient Greek writers describe him as a son of Uranus and a brother of Cronus, Oceanus, Iapetus and the other Titans. Others describe him as the son of Iapetus and the grandson of Uranus, so that he would be the nephew of Cronus and Oceanus.

这本身并不重要，但它表明神话中没有什么是绝对确定的。每个希腊作家都自由地用自己的方式讲述神话，因此你所获得的信息取决于你所阅读的版本。

This is not very important except that it shows there is nothing certain in mythology. Each Greek writer felt free to tell the myths in his own way and the information you get depends on the one read.

总之，阿特拉斯加入了其他泰坦的行列，对抗更年轻、更强大的神祇。他甚至可能是泰坦的统帅。他和泰坦们战败了，作为惩罚，阿特拉斯被判处肩负苍穹。（事实上，“阿特拉斯”这个名字就源于一个希腊词，意思是“支撑”。）

In any case, Atlas joined the other Titans in a war against younger and more powerful gods. He may even have been the Titan general. He and the other Titans were defeated and, as punishment, Atlas was condemned to support the heavens on his shoulders. (In fact, the name “Atlas” comes from a Greek word meaning “to support.”)

希腊人将阿特拉斯山描绘在遥远的西方，靠近直布罗陀海峡，那是他们通常所见的充满奇观的迷雾之地。当西方地区被进一步探索后，他们自然没有在那里找到阿特拉斯山，但却发现了一片山脉，于是他们推断，在过去的某个时期，阿特拉斯山已经变成了石头。摩洛哥和阿尔及利亚境内的这片山脉至今仍被称为“阿特拉斯山脉”。

The Greeks pictured Atlas as standing in the far west near the straits of Gibraltar, their usual misty region of wonders. When the western region was better explored, they naturally found no Atlas there, but they did find a region of mountains, so they decided that at some time in the past, Atlas had been turned to stone. The range of mountains in Morocco and Algeria are still called the “Atlas Mountains.”

据说阿特拉斯是众多年轻女神的父亲，她们被称为亚特兰蒂斯女神。她们也被称为赫斯珀里得斯女神，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“西方”，因为她们和她们的父亲一样，被描绘成居住在遥远的西方。

Atlas was supposed to have been the father of various groups of young goddesses called the Atlantides. They were also called the Hesperides from a Greek word meaning “west” because, like their father, they were pictured as dwelling in the far west.

顺便一提，在古希腊人眼中，任何被描绘成年轻女孩的次要女神都被称为宁芙（nymph），“宁芙”正是希腊语中“年轻女孩”的意思。在动物学中，某些昆虫的幼虫也被称为“宁芙”（nymph），原因就在于此。

To the ancient Greeks, by the way, any minor goddess pictured as a young girl was a nymph, which was the Greek word for “young girl.” In zoology, the young forms of certain insects are called “nymphs,” for this reason.

希腊人将宁芙描绘成自然界各种事物的象征。有的宁芙居住在树上，有的居住在岩石和山脉中，有的居住在湖泊和河流中。她们可以被视为特定树木或特定溪流的“精灵”。

The Greeks pictured the nymphs as symbolizing the various objects of nature. There were nymphs living in trees, in rocks and mountains, in lakes and rivers. They were what we might call the “spirit” of a particular tree or a particular brook.

亚特兰蒂斯女神是与海洋相关的宁芙仙女。其他类似的宁芙仙女还有俄刻阿尼得斯女神，她们是俄刻阿诺斯的女儿，当然还有涅瑞伊得斯女神，她们是古海神涅柔斯的女儿。

The Atlantides were nymphs associated with the sea. Other such nymphs were the Oceanids, the daughters of Oceanus, of course, and the Nereids, the daughters of an old god of the sea named Nereus.

亚特兰蒂斯与俄刻阿诺斯的极西海域相连，因此这片海域既被称为“大西洋”，也被称为“海洋”，而今天我们称之为“大西洋”。由此可见，阿特拉斯和俄刻阿诺斯都在第二大洋的名称中留下了痕迹，更不用说像佐治亚州亚特兰大、新泽西州大西洋城和海洋城这样的城市了。

The Atlantides were associated with the far western waters of Oceanus, so that those waters were called “Atlantic” as well as “Ocean” and today we call it “Atlantic Ocean.” Thus both Atlas and Oceanus leave their traces on the name of the second largest ocean, to say nothing of such cities as Atlanta, Georgia, and Atlantic City and Ocean City in New Jersey.

公元前355年，希腊哲学家柏拉图虚构了一个关于西洋中一块巨大陆地的故事，这块陆地在地震后沉入海底，他称之为“亚特兰蒂斯”。这当然纯属虚构，但自那以后，一直有人坚称亚特兰蒂斯真实存在。

In 355 B.C., when the Greek philosopher Plato made up a story about a great land in the western Ocean which sank beneath the waters after an earthquake, he called it “Atlantis.” It was pure fiction, of course, but there have been people ever since who have insisted that Atlantis really existed.

随着时间的推移，人们越来越难以想象阿特拉斯托起天空的形象。希腊人对天文学有了更深入的了解，并得出结论：天空距离地球至少有数百万英里之遥。此外，他们认为，天空没有坠落的趋势，因此无需托起。

As time went on, it became difficult to picture Atlas as holding up the sky. The Greeks learned more about astronomy and decided that the sky had to be at least millions of miles from the earth. Besides, they decided, the sky has no tendency to fall and need not be held up.

于是，阿特拉斯支撑大地而非天空的形象便应运而生。事实上，如今人们描绘的阿特拉斯形象是一位身材魁梧、疲惫不堪的人物，他肩上扛着沉重的地球，用一只粗壮的手臂稳住它。

So the notion arose of Atlas supporting the earth rather than the sky. In fact, when Atlas is pictured these days it is as a gigantic, weary figure with the great globe of the earth weighing down on one shoulder while he steadies it with one tremendous arm.

人体内有一块骨头似乎承担着寰椎的功能。头部的球状部分支撑在脊柱上，脊柱由一系列沿背部延伸的骨头组成。支撑头部的最上方那块骨头，自然而然地被称为“寰椎”。

There is a bone in the human body which seems to do the job of Atlas. The globe of the head rests on the spine, which is made up of a series of bones running down the length of the back. The topmost one of these bones, which supports the head, is naturally called the “atlas.”

早期地理学家绘制地球各区域地图集时，通常会在封面上印上阿特拉斯举起地球的图案。16世纪，佛兰德地理学家格哈杜斯·墨卡托绘制了第一张现代地图，并根据封面上的图案将他的地图集命名为“阿特拉斯”（Atlas）。

Now when the early geographers began to make books of maps of various regions of the earth, they used to put on the cover a picture of Atlas holding up the earth. In the 1500’s, the Flemish geographer Gerhardus Mercator drew up the first modern maps, and he called his book of such maps an “atlas” from the picture on the cover.

从那时起，地图集——实际上，任何用图画或图片来说明某个主题（例如人体解剖学）的书籍——都被称为地图集。

Ever since, a book of maps—and, indeed, any book of drawings or pictures illustrating some subject, such as the anatomy of a human being—is called an atlas.

在动物学中，“Atlas”一词与“Titan”一样，可以用来表示体型巨大。一种据称是体型最大的远古恐龙，从头尖到长尾末端可达百英尺，被称为“亚特兰蒂斯龙”（Atlantosaurus，意为“类似阿特拉斯的蜥蜴”）。同样，“Atlas beetle”指的是一种体型巨大的甲虫，“Atlas moth”指的是一种体型巨大的蛾子。

In zoology, Atlas, like Titan, can be used to indicate tremendous size. An ancient dinosaur that is supposed to have been the largest of all, stretching a hundred feet from the tip of its head to the end of its long tail, is called “Atlantosaurus” (the “Atlas-like lizard”). Similarly, the “Atlas beetle” is a very large beetle and the “Atlas moth” a very large moth.

而非常现代的例子是美国正在建造的两款威力强大的弹道导弹——“阿特拉斯”和“泰坦”。威力更大的导弹是“土星”。

And very modern examples are two powerful ballistic missiles being built by the United States, the “Atlas” and the “Titan.” An even more powerful missile is the “Saturn.”

土卫六许珀里翁是土星的卫星之一，最初被认为是太阳神。然而，随着时间的推移，太阳的职责逐渐转移到了许珀里翁的儿子赫利俄斯身上。

The Titan Hyperion, who is included as one of the satellites of Saturn, was originally considered the god of the sun. However, as time went on, this became the function of Hyperion’s son, Helios.

现代英语中关于太阳神赫利俄斯最重要的线索或许要追溯到1868年发生在印度的一次日食。当时，人们让太阳大气层的光芒穿过一种叫做光谱仪的仪器。光线被分解成一系列不同颜色的明亮谱线。每条谱线都代表着太阳大气层中的一种特定元素，当地球上已知的元素被加热到炽热的白光时，这些谱线都能在太阳光中重现。但有一个例外。太阳光中的一条谱线与地球上任何已知元素产生的谱线都不相同。

Perhaps the most important trace of Helios in modern English is the result of an eclipse of the sun that took place in India in 1868. The light of the sun’s glowing atmosphere was allowed to pass through an instrument called a spectroscope. In doing so, the light was split up into a series of bright lines of different colors. Each line was the mark of a particular element in the sun’s atmosphere and could be duplicated in the light of elements known on earth when these were heated to a white-hot glow. There was but one exception. One line in the sun’s light was not like the line produced by any known element on the earth.

英国天文学家诺曼·洛克耶认为，太阳光必定是由一种只存在于太阳中的元素产生的。他将这种元素命名为“氦”。

The British astronomer Norman Lockyer decided it must be produced by an element that occurred only in the sun. He named that element “helium.”

二十七年后，地球上也发现了这种元素，但那时它的名字已经固定下来了。这种元素至今仍被称为氦，也就是“太阳的元素”。

Twenty-seven years later, that element was discovered on the earth, too, but by then the name was firmly stuck. The element is still called helium, the “element of the sun.”

前缀“helio-”用于许多与太阳相关的词语中。例如，现代对太阳系的描述是一系列行星围绕太阳这个中心运行，这就是“日心说”。

The prefix “helio-” is used in a number of words having to do with the sun. For instance, the modern picture of the Solar system is that of a series of planets circling the sun as a center and this is the “heliocentric theory.”

一个更常见的例子是那种不断向着太阳转动的花。这类花最常见的名字，用通俗易懂的英语来说，就是“向日葵”。一个不太常见的例子是“天芥菜”，它在希腊语中的意思就是“向着太阳转动”。

A more common example involves a flower which turns constantly toward the sun. The most common flower of this sort is called, in straightforward English, the “sunflower.” A less common example is the “heliotrope,” which, in Greek, means “turn toward the sun.”

赫利俄斯的父亲许珀里翁也被描绘成有两个女儿，塞勒涅和厄俄斯。前者是月亮女神，后者是黎明女神。因此，许珀里翁的三个孩子象征着地球上三大光源：太阳、月亮以及日出前洒满大地的黎明之光。（当然，黎明之光是由太阳光照射到高层大气后才到达地面形成的，但神话的创造者们要么对此一无所知，要么根本不在乎。）

Hyperion, the father of Helios, was also pictured as having two daughters, Selene and Eos. The first was goddess of the moon, the second, goddess of the dawn. Thus, the three children of Hyperion symbolized the three important sources of light on the earth: the sun, the moon, and the light of dawn that spreads over the earth before the sun rises. (Of course, the light of dawn is caused by the sun shining on the upper atmosphere before its light reaches the ground itself, but the mythmakers either didn’t know this or didn’t care.)

如同太阳神赫利俄斯一样，塞勒涅也在元素周期表中留下了痕迹。1818年，瑞典化学家永斯·雅各布·贝采利乌斯发现了一种与碲非常相似的元素，可以说是碲的孪生姐妹。由于碲是以大地女神的名字命名的（正如我在本书前面解释的那样），贝采利乌斯决定以月亮女神的名字来命名这种新元素，以求平衡。他选择了塞勒涅，并将这种新元素命名为“硒”。

Like Helios, Selene leaves traces in the table of chemical elements. In 1818, a Swedish chemist, Jöns Jacob Berzelius, discovered an element very like tellurium; a kind of twin sister, so to speak. Since tellurium had been named after a goddess of the earth (as I explained earlier in the book), Berzelius decided to balance matters by naming the new element after a goddess of the moon. He chose Selene and named the new element “selenium.”

光明之源厄俄斯，作为第三种光源，虽然没有被列入元素周期表，但她并未被遗忘。对希腊人来说，厄俄斯不仅象征着黎明，也象征着黎明出现的方向。英语中表示这个方向的词是“east”（东），正如你所见，两者含义相近。

Eos, the third source of light, does not enter the list of elements, but she is not forgotten. Eos symbolized not only the dawn to the Greeks, but also the direction in which it appeared. The English word for that direction is “east” which, as you see, is similar.

前缀“eo-”用于表示事物开始之初、黎明之时的词语中。例如，恐龙灭绝后，地球历史上进入了一个以鸟类和哺乳动物为主的时期。这个时期被称为“新生代”（Cenozoic），源自希腊语，意为“新的动物”。

The prefix “eo-” is used in words meaning anything which occurs at the very beginning, the dawn, of something. For instance, after the dinosaurs died out, a period in earth’s history began in which the birds and mammals were most important. This is the “Cenozoic” period from Greek words meaning “new animals.”

新生代的第一个时期被称为“始新世”（“新时代的曙光”）。

Well, the first part of the Cenozoic period is called the “Eocene” (“dawn of the new”).

此外，科学家们还研究了非常古老的马科动物的骨骼。他们发现的现代马的最古老祖先是一种体型与狐狸相仿的动物，每只脚有四个脚趾。这种动物被称为“始祖马”（Eohippus，意为“黎明之马”）。

Then, again, scientists have studied the bones of very ancient horselike animals. The oldest ancestor of the modern horse which they could find was an animal no larger than a fox, with four toes on each foot. This is called “Eohippus” (“dawn horse”).

最后，人们在地下发现了数十万年前远古人类遗留下来的原始工具。其中最古老的工具极其原始，科学家们甚至无法确定它们究竟是人类制造的，还是仅仅由于风和水的偶然作用而形成了粗糙的工具状。这些最原始的工具被称为“风石”（或“黎明石”）。

Finally, primitive tools are found in the ground which were left behind by ancient men hundreds of thousands of years ago. The oldest of all are so primitive, scientists can’t be quite sure whether they were formed by men or whether they just took on crude tool-like shapes by the accidental action of wind and water. Those most primitive tools are called “eoliths” (“dawn stones”).

罗马人有他们自己的光明之神和女神。他们将太阳神索尔等同于赫利俄斯，将月神卢娜等同于塞勒涅，将曙光女神奥罗拉等同于厄俄斯。在英语中，罗马名字更为人熟知。

The Romans had their own gods and goddesses of these various forms of light. They identified their god Sol with Helios, their goddess Luna with Selene, and their goddess Aurora with Eos. In each case, it is the Roman name that is better known in English.

“solar”一词在英语中是一个常用的形容词，用来描述与太阳有关的事物，“lunar”则用来描述与月亮有关的事物。

The word “solar” is a common adjective in English to describe anything pertaining to the sun, and “lunar” serves the same purpose in connection with the moon.

例如，太阳及其周围的行星被称为“太阳系”。地球绕太阳公转一周的时间称为“太阳年”。另一方面，月球绕地球公转一周的时间称为“朔望月”。十二个朔望月构成“朔望年”，比太阳年短十一天。

For instance, the sun, and the planets circling it, are called the “solar system.” The period of time in which the earth circles the sun just once is the “solar year.” On the other hand, the period of time in which the moon circles the earth just once is a “lunar month.” Twelve lunar months is a “lunar year,” which is eleven days shorter than a solar year.

还有其他一些痕迹。例如，阳光充足的房间被称为“日光浴房”（solarium）。小型遮阳伞被称为“阳伞”（parasol），这个词源于意大利语，意思是“遮挡阳光”。至于月亮，它最引人注目的形状当然是新月形，而“lunate”的意思就是说它是新月形的。

There are other traces too. A room open to the sun for sun-bathing purposes is a “solarium.” A small sunshade is a “parasol,” a word which comes from Italian words meaning “to guard against the sun.” As for the moon, its most dramatic shape is, of course, the crescent, and to say something is “lunate” is to say it is crescent-shaped.

罗马女神奥罗拉的名字与一种非常美丽的自然现象联系在一起。太阳喷射出的微小粒子——电子——由于受到地球磁场的吸引，聚集在地球周围的空间中。这些电子会缓慢地泄漏到地球大气层的极北和极南区域。在这些区域，地球磁场最为强烈。

The Roman goddess Aurora leaves her name on a very beautiful phenomenon indeed. Tiny particles called electrons are fired out from the sun and collect in the space around the earth because they are trapped there by the earth’s magnetism. There is always a slow leakage of these electrons down into the earth’s atmosphere in the far north and far south. There, the earth’s magnetism is strongest.

当电子撞击高层大气中的原子时，会使原子发出微弱的光芒。从地球表面观测，天空中会出现彩色的薄片和条纹。这种现象在极地地区非常常见，但在伦敦或纽约等南部地区却很少见到。

When the electrons strike the atoms of the upper atmosphere, they cause them to glow in faint colors. As seen from the surface of the earth, colored sheets and streamers appear in the sky. This is very common in the polar regions, but is only occasionally seen as far south as London or New York.

当这种情况发生时，人们总是惊叹于如同黎明之光般的光芒竟然出现在北方而非东方。我们用通俗易懂的语言称这种现象为“北极光”。法国天文学家皮埃尔·伽桑狄在1621年将其称为“北极光”（Aurora Borealis，意为“北方的黎明”）。随后，在1773年，英国探险家詹姆斯·库克航行到足够南的地方，看到了“南极光”，他将其称为“南极光”（Aurora Australis，意为“南方的黎明”）。

When it is, there is always amazement that light like the light of dawn should appear in the north rather than in the east. We call this phenomenon the “Northern Lights” in plain English. The French astronomer, Pierre Gassendi, called it the “Aurora Borealis” (“northern dawn”) in 1621. Then, in 1773, the English explorer, James Cook, sailed far enough south to see the “Southern Lights” and these he called “Aurora Australis” (“southern dawn”).

乌拉诺斯和盖亚的女儿中，有些既非巨人也非泰坦。然而，她们中的一些人所代表的恐怖，远比单纯的怪异体型和外形更加可怕。例如，复仇女神厄里倪厄斯三姐妹。这三位姐妹惩罚那些犯下滔天罪行的人。她们追捕他们，让他们不得安宁，最终将他们逼疯。她们或许象征着良知和悔恨，这种内心的情感确实会让人因自己所犯下的罪行而终生痛苦。希腊人常常称这三位姐妹为“欧墨尼得斯”（Eumenides），意为“仁慈的女神”，以此来安抚她们，告诉她们她们是仁慈的。（这种用悦耳的名称来称呼不愉快的事物以避免不愉快的习惯被称为“委婉语”，源自希腊语，意为“说好话”。）

There were daughters of Uranus and Gaea who were neither giants nor Titans. Some of them, however, represented terrors that were more frightful than mere monstrous size and shape could be. For instance, there were the three Erinnyes. These sisters punished those who were guilty of particularly horrible crimes. They pursued them, gave them no rest and drove them mad. Probably they symbolized conscience and remorse, those feelings inside a man which can indeed bring him lifelong misery over a wrong he regrets having committed. The Greeks often called these sisters the Eumenides, meaning the “kindly ones,” in order to keep them kindly by telling them that was what they were. (This habit of calling something unpleasant by a pleasant name in order to avoid unpleasantness is called “euphemism” from Greek words meaning “to speak well.”)

罗马人称这些复仇女神为Furiae（英语中的“Furies”），而“fury”（愤怒）一词也由此而来。最初，这个词指的是一种类似复仇女神带来的狂暴疯狂，在这种状态下，人会丧失对自己行为的责任感。尤其是女性，如果表现出这种行为，就被称为Fury（愤怒的化身）。随着时间的推移，这个词的含义逐渐减弱，如今“furious”仅指非常愤怒。

The Romans called these avenging women Furiae (“Furies” in English) and we inherit the word “fury” as a result. Originally, this meant a kind of raging madness of the sort brought on by the Furies, one in which a person was not responsible for his deeds. A woman, in particular, behaving in this fashion, is called a Fury. With time, the word weakened, and now to be “furious” means only to be very angry.

另一组古代三姐妹是命运三女神（摩伊拉）。她们可能是天王星的女儿，也可能是他的侄女。她们掌控着宇宙的运行轨迹。希腊人认为宇宙的运行轨迹在最初就已注定，无法改变。即使是众神也无力干预。

Another set of three ancient sisters are the Moirae. These may be the daughters, or the nieces of Uranus. They controlled the course of the universe. The Greeks felt that the entire course was laid out at the beginning and could not be altered. Even the gods were helpless to interfere.

三位命运女神分别名为克洛托、拉刻西斯和阿特洛波斯。

The three Moirae were named Clotho, Lachesis, and Atropos.

克洛托被描绘成纺织所有象征生命之线的女神，这些生命之线为所有活着的人带来生机。事实上，她的名字在希腊语中意为“纺纱者”。在日常生活中，纺出的线通常会被织成“布”，而布最常用来制作“衣服”。这些英语词汇源自盎格鲁-撒克逊语，但它们必然与克洛托有关。

Clotho is pictured as spinning all the threads that represent life for all the individuals alive. In fact the name means “the spinner” in Greek. In ordinary life, threads that are spun are usually woven into “cloth” out of which, most often, “clothes” are made. These English words come from the Anglo-Saxon, but they must be related to Clotho.

“拉刻西斯”（Lachesis）一词在希腊语中意为“签”。他们想象每个孩子出生时都会抽取一张签，决定他的一生。拉刻西斯通过引导和测量克洛托纺出的线的长度来掌控这签的性质。

The word “lachesis” means “lot” in Greek. They imagined each child as drawing some lot when he was born which decided what his life would be like. Lachesis controls the nature of this lot by guiding and measuring the length of thread spun by Clotho.

当然，拉刻西斯的出现并非偶然。一些希腊人认为，一个人的命运取决于他应得的，或者说是他“应得的”，而“应得”（merit）一词与“命运女神”（Moirae）相关。人既可以因善而得，也可以因恶而得，但人人都渴望因善而得，因此“应得”一词的含义也倾向于此。说某人或某事“有应得”（has merit）意味着它是好的或有价值的。

Of course, Lachesis doesn’t work by chance. Some Greeks thought that a person’s lot was what he deserved, or what he “merited” and the word “merit” is related to “Moirae.” It is possible to merit either good or evil but naturally everyone wants to merit good and the word has been slanted in that direction. To say someone or something “has merit” implies that it is good or worthwhile.

最后，在拉刻西斯所指之处，通常手持剪刀的阿特洛波斯剪断了绳索。这象征着死亡。“阿特洛波斯”在希腊语中意为“不回头”。任何人和事都无法动摇阿特洛波斯的决心。没有人能够阻止她。

Finally, at the point indicated by Lachesis, Atropos, who is usually pictured with a pair of shears, cuts the cord. That meant death. “Atropos” in Greek means “not turning.” Atropos was not to be turned away from her purpose by anyone or anything. There was no way of stopping her.

阿特洛波斯在现代化学中留下了痕迹。有一种名为“颠茄”（意大利语意为“美丽的女士”）的植物，其汁液可用作眼药水。这种眼药水能使瞳孔放大。使用这种眼药水的女性感觉眼睛变得深邃美丽，因此得名“颠茄”。

Atropos leaves a trace of herself in modern chemistry. There is a plant called “belladonna” (Italian for “beautiful lady”) from which a juice can be obtained for use as eyedrops. Such eyedrops cause the pupils of the eye to enlarge. Women using these drops felt that this gave their eyes a beautiful dark appearance, which is why the plant received its name.

然而，颠茄汁液如果吞食则剧毒。18世纪末，瑞典植物学家卡尔·林奈在对植物进行分类时，将颠茄类植物统称为“阿托帕属”（Atropa）。换句话说，颠茄植物如同阿托波斯（Atropos）一样，能够斩断生命之线。1831年，人们在颠茄汁液中发现了这种有毒化学物质，并将其命名为“阿托品”。

The belladonna juice, however, is very poisonous, if swallowed. When the Swedish botanist, Carolus Linnaeus, was classifying plants in the late 1700’s, he gave the belladonna group the general name “Atropa.” The belladonna plant, in other words, could cut the thread of life as surely as Atropos ever could. Then, in 1831, the poisonous chemical in belladonna juice was located and named “atropine.”

罗马人也认为三位姐妹决定着男人的命运。他们称她们为“帕尔卡”（Parcae），这个词源于拉丁语，意为“带来”，因为这三位姐妹的行为预示着未来。罗马人（以及希腊人）相信，他们不必总是等到最后才能看到未来。他们认为神明可以提前揭示未来。揭示未来的体系被称为“神谕”（oracles），这个词源于拉丁语，意为“说话”，因为接受神谕的常见方式是让女祭司进入恍惚状态，并念诵据称是神明的旨意。

The Romans also visualized three sisters determining the lot of men. They called them the Parcae from a Latin word meaning “to bring forth” because the actions of these sisters brought forth the future. The Romans (and the Greeks, too) believed that they didn’t always have to wait till the end to see what future would bring forth. They felt the future could be revealed in advance by the gods. Systems for revealing the future were called “oracles,” from a Latin word meaning “to speak,” because a common way of receiving an oracle was to have a priestess go into a trance and speak what were supposedly the words of the god.

女祭司们通常谨慎地宣读神谕，这些神谕不会直接预言未来，而是至少可以有两种不同的解读。因此，她们采取了稳妥的做法。正因如此，“神谕”指的是那些含义模糊、可以有两种解释的语句。最著名的神谕出现在希腊的德尔斐城，因此这种令人费解的语句也可以被称为“德尔斐式的”。

The priestesses were usually careful to pronounce oracles that didn’t predict the future too clearly but could be interpreted in at least two different ways. Thus, they played it safe. For that reason, an “oracular” statement is one which does not have a straightforward meaning but can be taken either way. The most famous oracle of all was at a Greek town named Delphi, so such a confusing statement can also be said to be “delphic.”

“神谕”的另一个词是“fatum”，源自另一个拉丁词，意思是“说话”。由于神谕关乎未来，而未来毕竟是由三位帕尔卡（或命运三女神）决定的，因此罗马人也将这三位女神称为“Fata”。

Another word for oracle was “fatum,” from another Latin word meaning “to speak.” Since oracles dealt with the future, which was, after all, decided by the three Parcae (or Moirae), the Romans called the three sisters “Fata” also.

因此，在英语中，我们说“命运三女神”，也说“命运”指的是无法改变的未来。认为未来一切都无法改变，一切都已预先决定的人，就是“宿命论者”。

In English, we therefore speak of the “Three Fates” and also of “fate” as meaning a future which cannot be changed. A person who believes that nothing in the future can be changed, that all is decided upon in advance, is a “fatalist.”

顺便一提，罗马人认为帕尔凯三女神中的第三位就是阿特洛波斯，她的名字是莫尔塔（Morta），源自拉丁语，意为“死亡”。她就是我们今天所说的“死亡天使”。

The third of the Parcae, by the way, whom the Romans identified with Atropos is Morta, from a Latin word meaning “death.” She is what we would call the “Angel of Death.”

我们经常使用这个词。人是“必死的”，因为他注定要死；神是“不朽的”（“不会死”）。如果一个词能带来死亡，那么它就是“必死的”，而“殡仪师”就是殡葬承办人。

We use the word often enough. Man is “mortal” because he is fated to die; the gods are “immortal” (“not dying”). A word is “mortal” if it brings death and a “mortician” is an undertaker.

希腊人认为死亡也有柔和的一面。漫长而疲惫的一生之后，死亡甚至可以带来安息。因此，他们认为死神塔纳托斯是睡神许普诺斯的兄弟。

The Greeks recognized a softer side to death. After a long and weary life, death might even come as a rest. For that reason, they considered Thanatos, the god of death, to be a brother of Hypnos, the god of sleep.

塔纳托斯并未留下任何广为人知的痕迹，但他却载入了美国文学史册。美国第一首伟大的诗歌是威廉·卡伦·布莱恩特于1817年创作的，当时他年仅23岁。尽管年纪尚轻，这首诗却聚焦于死亡的主题，并将其命名为《塔纳托普西斯》（Thanatopsis），在希腊语中意为“死亡的景象”。

Thanatos has not left any well-known traces, but he enters the history of American literature. The first great American poem was written in 1817 by William Cullen Bryant who was then twenty-three years old. Despite his age, it dwelt on the theme of death and he called it “Thanatopsis,” which in Greek means “the sight of death.”

Hypnos这个词可能让人感到熟悉。通过药物或暗示产生的睡眠状态被称为“催眠术”，而安眠药则被称为“催眠药”。

Hypnos probably strikes a familiar note. An artificial sleep brought on by drugs or by suggestion is “hypnosis,” and a sleeping pill is a “hypnotic.”

罗马的睡眠之神是索姆努斯（Somnus）。当我们感到困倦时，我们处于“昏昏欲睡”的状态。梦游是“somnambulism”，在拉丁语中的意思正是“梦游”。

The Roman god of sleep is Somnus. When we are sleepy, we are “somnolent.” Sleepwalking is “somnambulism” which in Latin means exactly that, “sleepwalking.”

索姆努斯的儿子是梦神摩耳甫斯。（毕竟，睡眠孕育着梦境。）“摩耳甫斯”一词源于拉丁语，而拉丁语又源于希腊语，意为“形式”或“形状”，因为梦境就是呈现在脑海中的各种形式和形状。

The son of Somnus is Morpheus, the god of dreams. (After all, sleep does give birth to dreams.) “Morpheus” goes back through Latin to the Greek word for “form” or “shape” because dreams are forms and shapes brought before the mind’s eye.

“形态学”是生物学的一个分支，研究生物的形态和结构。另一方面，加上前缀“a-”（这是希腊语中将一个词变成其反义词的方法），就得到了“amorphous”（无定形的）这个词，意思是“无形的”。

“Morphology” is that branch of biology that deals with the form and structure of living things. On the other hand, add the prefix “a-” (which is a Greek method for turning a word into its opposite) and you have the word “amorphous” meaning “shapeless.”

梦神在化学领域留下了有趣的印记。1803年，德国化学家F·W·瑟图纳从一种天然植物药中分离出了第一种纯化学物质。它是一种强效催眠剂，能缓解人们的剧痛，让他们安然入睡。仿佛梦神墨菲斯亲自降临，将他们拥入怀中——因此，这种化学物质被命名为“吗啡”。

The god of dreams leaves an interesting trace in chemistry. A German chemist, F. W. Sertürner, in 1803, isolated the first pure chemical to be obtained from a natural plant medicine. It was a powerful hypnotic. To people in agonizing pain, it brought relief and sleep. It was like Morpheus himself coming to enfold them—so the chemical was named “morphine.”

尽管希腊人坚信命运主宰人生，不可改变，但他们也承认偶然性的存在。命运女神堤喀（Tyche）被描述为俄刻阿诺斯（Oceanus）的女儿。这与我们熟知的罗马对应女神福尔图娜（Fortuna）相比，就显得逊色了。

But although the Greeks felt so strongly that Fate ruled life and could not be altered, they also recognized the existence of chance. The goddess of chance, described as a daughter of Oceanus, was named Tyche. This is not nearly as familiar to us as the Roman equivalent, Fortuna.

因此，我们用“命运”来形容一个人的“运势”，指的是他因命运的安排而注定的命运；而那些声称能够预测未来的人则被称为“算命先生”。自然，每个人都希望自己的未来幸福美满，而那些外出闯荡世界“寻觅财富”的年轻人则希望能够发家致富。因此，一大笔钱就被称为“财富”；一个幸福或幸运的人被称为“幸运儿”，而一个悲伤或不幸的人则被称为“不幸的人”。

Thus, we speak of a person’s “fortune” as signifying the fate that awaits him through the workings of chance and a person who pretends to predict the future is a “fortune-teller.” Naturally, everyone hopes his future will be a happy one and the young man who goes out into the world to “seek his fortune” hopes it will turn out to involve much money. Consequently a large sum of money is described as a “fortune”; a happy or lucky person is said to be “fortunate,” while a sad or unlucky one is “unfortunate.”

顺便一提，福尔图娜是其中一颗小行星的名字；它是第十九颗被发现的小行星。

Fortuna is the name of one of the planetoids, by the way; that of the nineteenth to be discovered.

3 奥林匹亚众神

3 THE OLYMPIANS

克洛诺斯统治下的泰坦神族并未长久。乌拉诺斯被克洛诺斯击败并驱逐后，这位古老的天空之神预言，克洛诺斯将会被他自己的一个儿子以同样的方式对待。因此，每当瑞亚生下孩子，克洛诺斯都会将其吞噬。

THE RULE OF THE TITANS under Cronus did not endure. After Uranus had been defeated and driven off by Cronus, the old god of the sky predicted that Cronus would be treated in the same way by one of his own sons. Therefore, every time Rhea bore a child, Cronus would swallow it.

克洛诺斯吞噬子女的这幅图画在天文学史上有着一段奇特的后续发展。意大利科学家伽利略·伽利莱是第一个使用望远镜观测星空的人。当他将望远镜对准遥远的土星（记住，土星是克洛诺斯的罗马名字）时，他勉强辨认出了土星环。

This picture of Cronus swallowing his children had an odd follow-up in the history of astronomy. The Italian scientist Galileo Galilei was the first to use a telescope to view the heavens. When he turned it on distant Saturn (the Roman name for Cronus, remember) he just barely made out the rings.

可惜的是，他的望远镜功率太小，无法让他看得清楚。他只能看到土星两侧似乎各有一个凸起。伽利略说，这就好比年迈的土星需要两个年轻的儿子，分别站在它的两侧，帮助它在太空中蹒跚前行。

Unfortunately, his telescope was too weak to let him make them out clearly. He could only see what looked like two bulges, one on each side of Saturn. Galileo said it was as though old Saturn needed two young sons, one on each side, to help his faltering steps through space.

然而，由于土星和地球绕太阳公转时位置不同，我们看到土星环的角度也会有所不同。有时，我们会看到土星环的侧面，此时它们非常薄，以至于看起来几乎消失不见。伽利略再次观测土星时，恰好遇到了这种情况，他发现土星环上的两个隆起消失了。他困惑又失望地惊呼：“什么！土星还在吞噬它的孩子吗？”此后，他再也没有观测过土星，直到大约半个世纪后，惠更斯才得以真正看到土星环的全貌。

However, the rings are seen at different angles, depending on the positions of Saturn and the earth in their journeys about the sun. Occasionally, we see the rings edge-on and they are so thin that at such a time they seem to disappear. When Galileo came to look at Saturn again, that happened to be the situation and he found the two bulges gone. Puzzled and disappointed he exclaimed, “What! Does Saturn still swallow his children?” He did not look at Saturn again and it was left to Huygens to see the rings as they actually were about half a century later.

然而，克洛诺斯并没有通过吞噬自己的孩子来保全性命。瑞亚对丈夫的这个恶习感到厌烦，于是将一块石头穿上婴儿的衣服，喂给了他，代替她第六个也是最后一个孩子。这个最后一个孩子就是宙斯。

Cronus did not, however, save himself by swallowing his children. Rhea, annoyed by this habit of her husband’s, dressed a stone in baby clothes and fed it to him in place of her sixth and last child. This last child was Zeus.

宙斯在希腊南部的克里特岛上秘密长大。在那里，他喝着一只名叫阿玛尔忒亚的山羊产的奶。长大成人后，他的母亲瑞亚帮助他骗克洛诺斯喝下一种饮料，导致这位泰坦神吐出了宙斯的五个兄弟姐妹。由于他们是永生的神，所以他们都安然无恙。

Zeus was secretly raised on the island of Crete, south of Greece. There he was fed on milk supplied by a goat named Amaltheia. When he had grown to manhood, his mother Rhea helped him trick Cronus into taking a drink that caused the Titan to throw up Zeus’s five brothers and sisters. Since they were immortal gods, they were all alive and well.

宙斯、他的兄弟姐妹及其后裔被称为“奥林匹斯诸神”，因为希腊人认为他们居住在奥林匹斯山上。这座山是希腊最高的山峰，海拔近万英尺，位于希腊北部边界附近。（当然，后来希腊人意识到奥林匹斯山上并没有任何神灵存在的迹象，于是他们认为真正的奥林匹斯山位于高高的天上。）

Zeus, his brothers and sisters and their descendants were called the “Olympians” because the Greeks pictured them as dwelling on Mount Olympus. This mountain, the highest mountain in Greece, nearly ten thousand feet high, stands near the northern boundary of the country. (Of course, later on, the Greeks came to realize that there weren’t really any signs of gods on Mount Olympus and they decided that the real Olympus was high in heaven.)

在希腊西南部的埃利斯地区，每四年都会举办一次特别的竞技会，以纪念宙斯。举办竞技会的山谷被称为“奥林匹亚”，以纪念奥林匹斯山。后来，希腊最伟大的雕塑家菲狄亚斯为竞技会场地雕刻了一尊“奥林匹亚宙斯”雕像。这尊雕像被认为是古代世界七大奇迹之一。

In the region of Elis, in southwestern Greece, there were special games every four years in honor of Zeus. The valley in which they were celebrated was called “Olympia” in honor of Mount Olympus, and in later years Phidias, Greece’s greatest sculptor, carved a statue of “Olympian Zeus” for the site of the games. This statue was considered one of the seven wonders of the ancient world.

有记载的第一届“奥林匹克运动会”于公元前776年举行。这些运动会是希腊世界的盛事。比赛的获胜者会获得无尽的荣誉，希腊人甚至以这些运动会来计算年份。每四年举办一次“奥林匹克运动会”，每次运动会都有编号。随着基督教的传入，这些运动会被视为异教节日而受到排斥。在举办了近300届奥林匹克运动会之后，罗马皇帝狄奥多西于公元394年将其废除。

The first of these “Olympian games” of which there is a record were conducted in 776 B.C. These games were the great event of the Greek world. Winners at the competitions were heaped with honors and the Greeks even counted the years by these games. Every four years was an “Olympiad” and each Olympiad was numbered. With the coming of Christianity, the games were frowned upon as a pagan festival. The Roman Emperor Theodosius put an end to them in A.D. 394 after nearly 300 Olympiads.

1896年，奥运会得以复兴，首届现代奥运会在希腊举行，当时被称为“奥林匹克运动会”（Olympics games），而非之前的“奥林匹克运动会”（Olympian games）。此后，奥运会在包括美国在内的多个国家举办。除了第一次世界大战和第二次世界大战期间的中断外，奥运会如同古代一样，每四年举办一次。因此，我们至今仍在尊崇奥林匹亚宙斯，至少在奥运会的名称中是如此。

In 1896, they were revived and the first of the modern version of the games, called the “Olympic games” rather than the Olympian games, was conducted in Greece. Since then, they have been held in various countries, including, several times, in the United States. Except for interruptions during World War I and World War II, they have been continued every four years, as in ancient times. And so we still honor Olympian Zeus, at least in the name we give these games.

回到神话故事——奥林匹斯诸神从克洛诺斯手中获救后，接受了宙斯的领导，并反抗泰坦巨人。泰坦巨人在蛮力方面更胜一筹。然而，宙斯却与之前被泰坦巨人囚禁的独眼巨人结盟。独眼巨人为宙斯打造了闪电，宙斯利用闪电击败了泰坦巨人。他将部分泰坦巨人囚禁于地下，并惩罚他们的首领阿特拉斯支撑天空。一些泰坦巨人，例如俄刻阿诺斯，由于保持中立甚至加入了奥林匹斯诸神，得以安享太平。

To return to the myths—The Olympians, having been rescued from Cronus, accepted the leadership of Zeus, and rebelled against the Titans. The Titans were the stronger as far as brute force was concerned. Zeus, however, took as allies the Cyclops whom, earlier, the Titans had imprisoned. The Cyclops forged lightning bolts for Zeus and by use of the lightning, Zeus defeated the Titans. He imprisoned some of them underground and condemned Atlas, their leader, to hold up the heavens. Some Titans, such as Oceanus, who had remained neutral or even joined the Olympians, were left in peace.

泰坦神与奥林匹斯诸神之间的这场战争，或许象征性地描述了第一批希腊人入侵如今被称为希腊的土地。他们带来了自己的神祇，包括宙斯，意图取代或同化希腊人之前的居民的神祇。

This battle between the Titans and the Olympians may have been a symbolic description of the invasion of the land now called Greece by the first Greeks. These brought their gods with them, including Zeus, to displace or absorb the gods of the pre-Greek inhabitants.

被征服的人民，在希腊异族的统治下，逐渐接受了希腊语言和希腊习俗。然而，他们想必对过去的生活怀有些许悲伤，因为一个夸张的传说流传开来：在克洛诺斯统治下，曾有过一个“黄金时代”。那时，人人幸福，吃着坚果水果，喝着牛奶。没有苦难，没有疾病，死亡也像他的兄弟——睡眠一样轻易到来。直到今天，我们仍然会把一个国家历史上特别强大或特别繁荣的时期称为“黄金时代”或“萨图恩时代”。

The conquered population, who found themselves under the rule of the Greek strangers, gradually adopted the Greek language and Greek ways. However, they must have remembered their old life with some sorrow, for the exaggerated legend grew up that there had been a “Golden Age” under the rule of Cronus. Then everyone had been happy, eating nuts and fruits and drinking milk. There had been no misery or sickness, and death came easily, like his brother, sleep. We still speak of any time in a nation’s history when it was particularly powerful, or particularly prosperous, as a “golden age” or a “Saturnian age.”

宙斯的两个兄弟是波塞冬和哈迪斯。战胜泰坦巨人后，众神一致同意宙斯继续统治奥林匹斯山，但兄弟二人抽签决定各自掌管宇宙的哪些部分。宙斯掌管天空，波塞冬掌管海洋，哈迪斯掌管冥界。

The two brothers of Zeus were Poseidon and Hades. After the victory over the Titans, it was agreed that Zeus was to remain the ruler of the Olympians, but the brothers cast lots to see what parts of the universe would be their special care. Zeus received the air, Poseidon the sea and Hades the underworld.

哈迪斯掌管冥界，因此被视为死神，因为冥界一直与死亡联系在一起。这或许是因为古希腊人习惯将死者埋葬于地下。（后来的希腊人开始火葬，但在神话最初形成之时，他们采用的是土葬。）因此，亡灵居住的地方便以哈迪斯的名字命名为“哈迪斯”。“哈迪斯”一词源于希腊语，意为“不可见的”，因为冥界是不可见的。

As Hades received the underworld, he was considered the god of the dead, for the underworld has always been associated with death. This is perhaps because of the habit of burying the dead underground. (The Greeks themselves in later times burned their dead, but in earlier times, when the myths were first made up, they buried their dead.) As a result, the place where the shades of the dead dwelt was called “Hades” after the god. The word “hades” comes from Greek words meaning “invisible” since the underground world cannot be seen.

因此，哈迪斯实际上并不是奥林匹斯神，因为他并不住在奥林匹斯山上。相反，他成为了“冥界之神”之一，也就是“冥界之神”。

Hades was thus not really an Olympian, for he did not live in Olympus. Rather he became one of the “chthonian gods”; that is, a “god of the underworld.”

希腊人还有其他冥界神祇，他们通常被描绘成可怕而怪诞的形象。人们认为他们起源于混沌，其血脉与天王星和盖亚无关。混沌孕育了夜之女神诺克斯和冥界之神厄瑞玻斯。

The Greeks had other chthonian gods and they were usually pictured as frightening and monstrous. They were thought of as having originated from Chaos by a line of birth that did not involve Uranus and Gaea. Chaos gave rise to Nox, the goddess of the darkness of night, and Erebus, the god of the darkness of the underground.

我们用“夜间的”来指代夜晚的事物，指的仍然是黑夜女神。同样，一首适合在夜晚演奏的乐曲被称为“夜曲”。至于厄瑞玻斯，南极洲确实有一座同名火山。如果你想象一下漫长而寒冷的南极之夜，以及通往冥界深处的阴森火山口，这个名字也确实很贴切。

We still are referring to the goddess of night when we refer to things of the night as “nocturnal.” Similarly, a musical composition meant to be played in the evening is a “nocturne.” As for Erebus, there is a volcano of that name in Antarctica. It is a good name, too, if you think of the long, frigid Antarctic night and the grim crater of the volcano leading down into the further darkness of the underworld.

厄瑞玻斯和奈特被认为是命运三女神（摩伊赖）的父母。他们也是塔尔塔罗斯的父母，塔尔塔罗斯很可能是希腊人入侵并引入奥林匹斯诸神之前冥界的统治者。哈迪斯取代了塔尔塔罗斯，正如宙斯取代了克洛诺斯一样。

Erebus and Night are considered to be the parents of the Moirae, or Fates. They are also the parents of Tartarus, who was probably the god of the underworld before the invasion of the Greeks brought in the Olympians. Hades displaced Tartarus as Zeus displaced Cronus.

然而，一些希腊神话的创造者保留了冥界和塔尔塔罗斯两个地方。冥界被认为是死者不会受到特别虐待的地方。但在冥界之下，更深处，是塔尔塔罗斯，那里是那些作恶多端的人和神祇受罚的地方。塔尔塔罗斯很可能启发了基督教中许多关于地狱的概念。

Some of the Greek mythmakers, however, kept both Hades and Tartarus. Hades was considered a place where the dead were not particularly mistreated. Beneath it, lower still, however, was Tartarus, where particularly evil men and gods were sent for punishment. It is Tartarus that probably gave rise to many of the Christian notions of Hell.

塔尔塔罗斯在历史上的一段悲惨篇章中留下了痕迹。13世纪，成吉思汗率领的蒙古部落从中亚故土向外扩张。他们入侵中国、波斯、俄罗斯和波兰，所到之处皆是杀戮和破坏。这或许是历史上最具灾难性的蛮族入侵。

Tartarus left its trace in a tragic episode in history. In the 1200’s, the Mongol tribes under Genghis Khan spread out of their central Asian homeland. They invaded China, Persia, Russia and Poland, killing and destroying. It was probably the most disastrous barbarian invasion in history.

蒙古人自称为“鞑靼人”。然而，他们的入侵如此可怕，仿佛将大地变成了塔尔塔罗斯，因此称他们为“鞑靼人”显然是个错误。

The Mongols called themselves “Tatars.” So dreadful was their invasion, though, that they seemed to convert the earth into Tartarus, and it was a natural mistake to call them “Tartars” instead.

至于冥界哈迪斯，希腊神话的创造者们对此着墨颇多。例如，哈迪斯被描述为以冥河斯堤克斯为界。任何与斯堤克斯河，也就是冥界相关的事物，都被称为“斯堤克斯的”。这个词尤其用于“斯堤克斯的黑暗”这一短语中，意指极度的黑暗，即冥界的黑暗。不知何故，斯堤克斯河在立誓时被认为具有特殊的神圣意义。在斯堤克斯河前立下的誓言，绝不能违背。

As for Hades, the Greek mythmakers had much to say of it. For instance, Hades was described as being bounded by the river Styx. Anything pertaining to the Styx, and therefore to the underworld, is said to be “Stygian.” This word is particularly used in the phrase “Stygian darkness” meaning a great darkness, the darkness of the underworld. For some reason the Styx was considered to be particularly holy in oath-making. An oath by the river Styx dare not be broken.

要想进入冥界，必须由一位名叫卡戎的阴沉老摆渡人摆渡过冥河。卡戎也是厄瑞玻斯和诺克斯的儿子。这个名字也可以用来戏谑地指代任何摆渡人。

To enter Hades, one had to be ferried across the Styx by a grim old ferryman named Charon, another son of Erebus and Nox. This name can be humorously applied to any ferryman.

冥河彼岸，冥界入口由一只名叫刻耳柏洛斯的三头怪犬守护。这个名字也常被用来戏谑地指代任何守卫。刻耳柏洛斯不允许任何人通过，除非他先向它献上一块面包作为赎金。因此，“给刻耳柏洛斯一块面包”这个短语就指为了办成某事而向官员行贿。

On the other side of the Styx, the entrance to Hades itself is guarded by a three-headed monstrous dog, named Cerberus. Again the name can be applied jokingly to any guardian. Cerberus allowed no one to pass who did not first throw him a ransom in the form of a piece of bread. For that reason, the phrase “to throw a sop to Cerberus” means to pay a bribe to some official in order to get something done.

亡灵一旦进入冥界，便会饮用勒忒河（Lethe）的河水，勒忒河在希腊语中意为“遗忘”。饮水之后，他们会遗忘生前的一切，变成无精打采、语无伦次的幽灵。我们至今仍用“勒忒的”（Lethean）来指代任何导致遗忘的事物。如果你感到困倦或行动迟缓，就容易健忘，这时我们就说你“昏昏欲睡”。而由于彻底的遗忘只伴随死亡，所以我们用“致命的”（lethal）来指代任何致命的事物。

Once within Hades, the spirits of the dead drink from a river named Lethe, a Greek word meaning “forgetfulness.” In drinking, they forget about their former life and become listless, gibbering ghosts. We still speak of anything which causes forgetfulness as “Lethean.” You are liable to be forgetful if you are sleepy or sluggish, and you are then said to be “lethargic.” And since complete forgetfulness comes only with death, we speak of anything deadly as “lethal.”

亡灵并非只能承受阴郁和恐怖。品德高尚的灵魂或许可以前往冥界一个特殊的区域，那里充满极乐与幸福。据说，这片区域并非由哈迪斯统治，而是由克洛诺斯掌管，这与黄金时代的传说遥相呼应。这片幸福之地被称为极乐世界（Elysium）或“爱丽舍田园”（Elysian Fields）。我们至今仍将极乐世界视为一个充满幸福的地方或时光，甚至将其作为“天堂”的代名词。法语中“爱丽舍田园”的说法是“香榭丽舍大街”（Champs Élysées），这也是巴黎一条美丽大道的名字。

The spirits of the dead need not undergo only gloom and horror. Worthy spirits might go to a special section of Hades where all was blissful and happy. That section was supposed to be ruled over by Cronus rather than Hades, an echo of the legend of the Golden Age.
