This happy place was called Elysium or the “Elysian Fields.” We still speak of Elysium as a place or time of great happiness and even use it as another word for “heaven.” The French words for Elysian Fields are “Champs Élysées” and that is the name given to a beautiful boulevard in Paris.

一些神话创造者认为极乐世界不可能位于冥界，于是将它们与其他奇景一起安置在遥远的西方。它们当时被称为“福岛”或“幸运岛”。

Some mythmakers felt that the Elysian Fields couldn’t be in Hades, and placed them in the far west along with other marvels. They were then called “Islands of the Blessed” or the “Fortunate Isles.”

当然，地下世界并非只是亡灵的居所，它也是金属，尤其是金银的来源。财富之神是普路托斯，我们今天所说的“富豪统治”（plutocracy）一词便源于此，指的是由富人掌控的政府。

Of course, the underground isn’t only an abode of the dead. It is also the source of metals, especially gold and silver. The god of wealth is Plutus, from which we get our word “plutocracy” to mean a government run by the wealthy.

作为掌管财富来源的地下世界的主宰，冥王哈迪斯也被赋予了与此相关的另一个名字，被称为普路托。

As a master of the underground regions from which wealth came, the god Hades was also given a form of that name, and was called Pluto.

罗马人将这两兄弟中的两位与他们自己的神祇联系起来。他们有一位天空之神名叫朱庇特，他们认为他就是宙斯；还有一位泉水和河流之神名叫尼普顿，他们认为他就是波塞冬。

The Romans identified two of these brothers with gods of their own. They had a god of the sky called Jupiter, whom they identified with Zeus; and a god of springs and rivers named Neptune, whom they identified with Poseidon.

两者都位列行星之列。希腊人以宙斯的名字命名了第五颗行星。这是一个合乎逻辑的选择，因为除了昏星和晨星之外，第五颗行星是最亮的。然而，昏星只在日落后几个小时出现，晨星只在黎明前几个小时出现，而第五颗行星却常常整夜闪耀。

Both ended among the planets. The Greeks named the fifth planet after Zeus. This was a logical choice because the fifth planet was the brightest except for the evening and morning star. The evening star, however, only appeared for a few hours after sunset and the morning star only for a few hours before dawn, while the fifth planet often shines the whole night through.

当然，罗马人使用了他们自己对这个名字的称呼，我们今天所熟知的这颗行星就是“木星”。

The Romans, of course, used their own version of the name and it is as “Jupiter” that the planet is known to us.

罗马人给朱庇特起了个别名，叫朱庇特（Jove）。除了在古老的感叹词“by Jove”（天哪）中偶尔出现外，朱庇特很少单独使用。不过，当需要形容词时，人们会用朱庇特（Jove）而不是朱庇特（Jupiter）。例如，人们从不说“木星卫星”（Jupiterian satellites），而总是说“木星卫星”（Jovian satellites）。

The Romans had a second name for Jupiter, and that was Jove. It is not used much by itself except in the old-fashioned exclamation, “by Jove.” However, when an adjective is needed, Jove rather than Jupiter is used. For instance, one never says the “Jupiterian satellites” but always the “Jovian satellites.”

海王星直到近代才获得类似的行星荣誉。19世纪初，天文学家仔细观测天王星，以确定其绕太阳的精确轨道。令他们惊讶的是，他们发现天王星的实际运行速度比计算结果略慢。一些人认为，天王星之外可能存在另一颗行星，其引力略微减缓了天王星的运行速度。

Neptune had to wait until modern times for a similar planetary honor. In the early 1800’s, astronomers carefully observed the planet Uranus in order to determine its exact orbit around the sun. They found, to their surprise, that its actual movement lagged a bit behind what their calculations were predicting. Some believed there might be another planet beyond Uranus whose gravitational pull was slowing it slightly.

英国天文学家约翰·库奇·亚当斯和法国天文学家于尔班·勒维耶素不相识，他们都计算出了这颗行星的位置，以解释天王星受到的影响。他们的计算结果一致。勒维耶率先公布了他的研究成果。1846年，柏林的一位天文学家观测了勒维耶指出的位置，果然发现了第八颗行星。

An English astronomer, John Couch Adams, and a French astronomer, Urbain J. J. Le Verrier, unknown to each other, both calculated where such a planet must be to account for the effect on Uranus. They both came out with the same answer. Le Verrier was the first to announce his results and in 1846 an astronomer in Berlin looked at the spot indicated by Le Verrier and, behold, there was the eighth planet.

它被命名为“海王星”，并没有什么特别的原因，而是因为海王星是一位重要的神祇，却没有自己的行星。

It was named “Neptune,” not for any particular reason, but because Neptune was one important god who lacked a planet.

即使考虑到海王星的影响，天王星的运动轨迹仍然略有滞后。因此，20世纪初，美国天文学家珀西瓦尔·洛厄尔开始寻找另一颗更遥远的行星。他未能成功，但他的后继者在1931年发现了第九颗行星。

Even allowing for Neptune, the motion of Uranus still lagged a tiny bit. The American astronomer Percival Lowell, in the first years of the twentieth century, therefore searched for another still more distant planet. He failed, but his successors found the ninth planet in 1931.

第九颗行星距离太阳光最远，深入到恒星之间的黑暗空间，因此被命名为“冥王星”。这个名字的前两个字母恰好是珀西瓦尔·洛厄尔名字的首字母。

The ninth planet moves farthest from the light of the sun and farthest into the darkness of the space between the stars, so it was named “Pluto.” The first two letters of the name happened to be the initials of Percival Lowell.

冥王星是唯一一颗拥有希腊名字而非罗马名字的重要行星。罗马人有一位冥界之神名叫狄斯（Dis），有时用“狄斯之父”（Dis pater）来指代哈迪斯（Hades），但不知为何，这个名字并没有像其他神祇的拉丁名字那样给西方世界留下深刻印象。

Pluto is the only important planet to have a Greek name, rather than a Roman one. The Romans had a god of the underworld named Dis and sometimes said “Dis pater” (“Father Dis”) when they meant Hades, but for some reason this did not make the impression on the Western World that the other Latin names of gods did.

海王星和冥王星的发现对二战期间的化学领域产生了深远的影响。1940年，铀是已知最复杂的元素。所有元素都按照复杂程度递增的顺序排列在一张名为“元素周期表”的图表中。元素编号从1到92，铀排在第92位。

The discovery of Neptune and Pluto had an echo in chemistry during World War II. Uranium was the most complicated element known in 1940. All the elements were listed in a chart called a “periodic table” in the order of increasing complexity. The elements went from number 1 to number 92 and uranium was number 92.

然而，1940年，加州大学的化学家们学会了如何少量制造出元素周期表中第93和94位的新元素。这些新元素位于元素周期表铀之后，因此以天王星以外的行星命名。由此，第93号元素被称为“镎”，第94号元素被称为“钚”。

In 1940, however, chemists at the University of California learned how to manufacture small quantities of new elements which fitted into the periodic table in places 93 and 94. These new elements were beyond uranium in the table and were therefore named for the planets beyond Uranus. Consequently, element number 93 is known as “neptunium” and element number 94 as “plutonium.”

木星，正如其以众神之王宙斯命名的行星一样，拥有太阳系中最大的卫星群，共有十二颗。其中四颗是伽利略于1610年发现的。它们是太阳系中最早被望远镜观测到的天体，而且都是大型卫星。两颗比月球还大，另外两颗也只比月球略小一些。每颗卫星都以神话中与宙斯密切相关的人物命名。

The planet Jupiter, as befits one named after the ruler of the gods, has the largest family of satellites in the Solar system. It has twelve. Four of these were discovered by Galileo in 1610. They were the first objects in the solar system to be discovered by the telescope and all are large satellites. Two are larger than our moon and the other two are only slightly smaller. Each of the satellites was named after some person closely connected with Zeus in the myths.

例如，宙斯曾爱上过一位名叫伊俄的河仙女。然而，宙斯已娶赫拉为妻，赫拉是克洛诺斯和瑞亚的女儿，而赫拉生性嫉妒。为了藏匿伊俄，宙斯将她变成了一头白牛，但赫拉却怀疑是白牛所为。她派巨人阿尔戈斯去监视伊俄。

For instance, Zeus was at one time in love with a river nymph named Io. However, Zeus was married to Hera, one of the daughters of Cronus and Rhea, and Hera was a jealous woman. Zeus turned Io into a white cow in order to hide her, but Hera suspected the cow. She sent a giant named Argus to watch her.

阿耳戈斯有一百只眼睛，其中一些眼睛始终睁着，时刻警醒。因此，任何警觉、清醒的人都被称为“阿耳戈斯之眼”。然而，宙斯施了魔法让阿耳戈斯沉睡，他的一百只眼睛也随之消失，然后宙斯杀死了他。赫拉将阿耳戈斯的眼睛移植到她最喜爱的鸟——孔雀的尾巴上。因此，一种与孔雀亲缘关系密切的鸟被称为“阿耳戈斯雉”。

Now Argus had a hundred eyes and some eyes were always open and watching. For that reason, any watchful, wakeful person is said to be “Argus-eyed.” Zeus, however, had Argus charmed to sleep, all hundred eyes of him, and then killed. Hera set the eyes of Argus in the tail of the peacock, which was her special bird. As a result, a bird closely related to the peacock is called the “Argus pheasant.”

但赫拉对伊俄的怀疑如今得到了证实，于是她派了一只螫刺的苍蝇驱赶伊俄，让她四处漂泊，不得安宁。神话的创造者们让伊俄游荡于已知世界的各个角落。有一次，她横渡了分隔欧亚大陆的海峡，也就是如今伊斯坦布尔所在的位置，这条海峡因此得名“博斯普鲁斯海峡”，意为“牛渡口”。（另一个英文对应的例子是“牛津”，一座非常著名的城市和大学就叫这个名字，但这与伊俄并无关联。）还有一次，她游过了分隔希腊和意大利南部的地中海，因此这片海域被命名为“爱奥尼亚海”。最终，她被允许在埃及安享晚年。

But Hera’s suspicions of Io were now confirmed, so she sent a stinging fly to drive her from place to place, never letting her rest. The myth-makers now had Io wander all over the known world. At one point, she crossed the straits separating Europe and Asia at the point where Istanbul is located and the straits received the name “Bosporus” meaning “cow crossing.” (Another English equivalent would be “oxford,” and a very famous town and University has that name though not through any connection with Io.) At another time she swam across the strip of the Mediterranean that separates Greece from southern Italy, and that was therefore named the “Ionian Sea.” Finally, she was allowed to find peace in Egypt.

关于仙女卡利斯托也有类似的故事。宙斯为了躲避赫拉，把她变成了一头熊。或者，也许是赫拉为了惩罚这位仙女而这么做的。

A similar story is told of the nymph Callisto. Zeus turned her into a bear to hide her from Hera. Or, perhaps, Hera did it to punish the nymph.

宙斯化身为动物的故事中，有一个女孩（并非仙女，而是人类）名叫欧罗巴。她居住在地中海亚洲沿岸的腓尼基。一天，欧罗巴和她的侍女们在海边时，宙斯化作一头白牛从海里游了出来。这头牛看起来如此美丽温顺，欧罗巴便爬上了它的背。这时，白牛抓住机会，猛地扎入海中，游到了克里特岛。根据神话传说，欧罗巴是第一个到达这片土地的人类，因此，希腊所在的这片大陆被命名为“欧洲”，以纪念她。

A case where it was Zeus himself that was turned into an animal involves a girl (not a nymph, but a human being) named Europa. She lived in Phoenicia on the Asiatic coast of the Mediterranean. Zeus changed himself into a white bull that swam out of the sea when she was with her maids at the seashore. The bull seemed so beautiful and tame that Europa climbed on its back. At that point the bull seized its opportunity, plunged into the sea and swam to the island of Crete. Europa was the first human being to arrive in that part of the world, according to the mythmakers, so the continent of which Greece is a part was named “Europe” in her honor.

他还牵涉到另一桩案件，此人名叫伽倪墨得斯，是一位英俊的年轻人，被宙斯化作雄鹰带到了奥林匹斯山。在那里，伽倪墨得斯担任侍酒官，在宴会上分发酒水。

It was a young man who was involved in still another case. This was Ganymede, a handsome young man who was carried off to Olympus by Zeus in the form of an eagle. There Ganymede served as cupbearer, passing out the drinks at the banquets.

由于这些故事，伽利略发现的四颗木星卫星被命名为“木卫一”（Io）、“木卫二”（Europa）、“木卫三”（Ganymede）和“木卫四”（Callisto），分别代表它们与木星的距离。第三颗卫星木卫三是太阳系中最大的卫星，直径达3200英里，质量甚至超过了土卫六（Titan）。

As a result of these stories, the four Jovian satellites discovered by Galileo were named “Io,” “Europa,” “Ganymede,” and “Callisto” in order of their distance from the planet. The third satellite, Ganymede, is the largest in the solar system. It is 3200 miles in diameter and more massive even than Titan.

直到1892年，美国天文学家爱德华·爱默生·巴纳德才发现了木星的第五颗卫星。这是一颗小型卫星，直径只有100英里，围绕木星运行的距离甚至比距离木星最近的大型卫星还要近。

It was not until 1892 that a fifth Jovian satellite was discovered, by an American astronomer, Edward Emerson Barnard. It was a small satellite, only 100 miles in diameter, circling Jupiter at a distance less than that of even the nearest of the large satellites.

这颗卫星有时被称为“巴纳德卫星”，以其发现者命名；有时被称为“木星五号”，因为它是第五颗被发现的木星卫星。然而，它的半官方名称是“阿玛尔忒亚”，取自哺育幼年宙斯的母山羊。宙斯童年的乳母在天上比他成年后的任何同伴都更接近他，这似乎合情合理。

This is sometimes called “Barnard’s satellite,” after its discoverer, and sometimes “Jupiter V” because it was the fifth Jovian satellite to be discovered. Its semi-official name, however, is “Amaltheia” after the goat that nursed the infant Zeus. It seems logical that the nurse of his childhood should be closer to him in the heavens than any of the companions of his adulthood.

20世纪初，人们又发现了七颗木星卫星。它们都非常小，而且距离木星相当远。目前为止，它们都还没有被命名。

In the 1900’s seven more Jovian satellites were discovered. All of them are very small and quite distant from Jupiter. None have as yet received any names.

接下来是海王星，它有两颗卫星，这两颗卫星在神话中都与它有关。

Passing on to the planet Neptune, it has two satellites and both are related to it in mythology.

因此，波塞冬有一个儿子名叫特里同，他是半人半鱼（这是希腊人描绘海神的一种常见方式）。波塞冬掀起风暴后，特里同的任务就是浮出水面，吹响用大贝壳制成的号角。号角一响，波涛便平静下来。

Thus Poseidon has a son named Triton, who is half man and half fish (a common Greek way of picturing the gods and goddesses of the sea). After Poseidon had brought on a storm, it was Triton’s job to come to the surface and blow a blast on a horn made out of a large sea shell. At the sound of Triton’s horn, the waves were calmed.

海王星被发现仅一个月后，英国天文学家威廉·拉塞尔就发现它有一颗大型卫星，其大小几乎与土卫六或木卫三一样。这颗卫星最终被命名为“海卫一”。

Only a month after the planet Neptune was discovered, a British astronomer, William Lassell, discovered it had a large satellite, one that was nearly as large as Titan or Ganymede. It was eventually named “Triton.”

（回到地球，有一种大型海螺也叫“特里同”，因为它的壳和传说中特里同使用的壳很像。）

(To come back to earth, there is a kind of large sea snail that is also called “Triton” because its shell is like that which Triton was supposed to have used.)

1950年，美国天文学家G.P.柯伊伯发现了海王星的第二颗卫星，它比海卫一小得多。他将其命名为“涅瑞伊得斯”，以纪念陪伴波塞冬航行于大海的五十位海仙女。

In 1950, the American astronomer G. P. Kuiper discovered a second satellite of Neptune’s, one that was much smaller than Triton. He named it “Nereid” after the fifty sea nymphs that attended Poseidon on his travels through the sea.

冥王星没有已知的卫星。而天王星则有五颗卫星。不过，天王星的卫星是太阳系中唯一名称并非源自希腊或罗马神话的卫星，因此无需赘述。

Pluto has no known satellites. Uranus, on the other hand, has five. However, the Uranian satellites are the only ones in the solar system that do not get their names from Greek or Roman mythology, so there is no need to mention them further.

除了行星之外，天空中还包含数千颗恒星。这些恒星被称为“恒星”，因为与行星不同，它们彼此之间的相对位置不会改变。它们构成不变的图案，日复一日，年复一年。

In addition to the planets, the heavens also contain thousands of stars. These are “fixed stars” since, unlike the planets, they do not change position with respect to one another. They form patterns that do not change, night after night and year after year.

史前时代的牧羊人和农民研究这些星象规律，因为它们可以作为历法。太阳在星空中移动，一年中不同时期夜晚可见的星星也各不相同。

Shepherds and farmers, in prehistoric days, studied these patterns because they served as a calendar. The sun moves against the background of the stars and different stars are visible at night at different times of the year.

处理这种情况最简单的办法是将星星分成几个方便的组，这些组现在被称为“星座”（源自拉丁语，意为“有星星的”）。这样，人们就可以说，当一个星座在日落时分从东方升起，就预示着收获的季节；另一个星座在日落时分升起，就预示着播种的季节，以此类推。

The easiest way to handle the situation was to divide the stars into convenient groups which are nowadays called “constellations” (from Latin words meaning “with stars”). Then one could say that when one constellation rose in the east at sunset, it was harvest; the rising of another at sunset would indicate planting time and so on.

就此而言，最重要的星座是太阳在天空中实际经过的星座。这样的星座共有十二个。之所以是十二个，是因为月相也被用来计时。月亮经历十二次盈亏变化，而太阳则在十二个星座中运行一次。换句话说，一年有十二个月，太阳在十二个星座中每个星座停留一个月。

The most important constellations in this connection would be those through which the sun actually passed on its path across the sky. There are twelve of these. The reason for the twelve is that the phases of the moon were also used to keep time. The moon went through its phases twelve times while the sun traveled through the constellations once. In other words, there are twelve months to the year and the sun spent one month in each of twelve constellations.

区分不同星座的自然方法是观察每个星座中星星的排列方式。随着时间的推移，人们自然而然地开始根据这些排列方式绘制复杂的图案。八颗星排列成“V”字形，可能看起来像一头带角的公牛的头。排列成“C”字形的星星，乍一看像弓，后来又像一个射手。

The natural way of telling one constellation from another is to notice the pattern of stars in each one. It was inevitable that as time went on, people would begin to make complicated pictures out of these patterns. Eight stars arranged in a “V” might seem to resemble the head of a bull with its horns. Stars arranged in a “C” might suggest first a bow, and then an archer.

因此，许多星座现在都被描绘成动物或人物。事实上，太阳运行轨迹所经过的十二星座组成的圆环包含了如此多的想象中的动物，以至于它被称为“黄道带”，这个词源于希腊语，意思是“动物之环”。

As a result, many of the constellations are now pictured as animals or people. In fact the circle of twelve constellations through which the sun passes in its travels contains so many imaginary animals that it is called the “zodiac” from Greek words meaning “circle of animals.”

希腊人从巴比伦人那里继承了大部分星座图。他们随后要么改变这些图以适应自己的神话，要么编造神话来解释这些图。

The Greeks inherited most of these constellation-pictures from the Babylonians. What they then did was either to change the pictures to fit their own myths, or else invent myths to explain the pictures.

例如，黄道十二宫中有一个星座，现在以其拉丁文名称摩羯座（Capricornus）而闻名，该名称源自拉丁语，意为“有角的山羊”。

For instance, there is a constellation in the zodiac that is now known by its Latin name of Capricornus from Latin words meaning “horned goat.”

希腊人认为，这只山羊实际上是阿玛尔忒亚，宙斯为了感谢她在他幼年时给予的养育，将她安置在天空中。如果真是如此，那么阿玛尔忒亚在地理上也留下了痕迹。

The Greeks stated that this goat was actually Amaltheia which was placed in the skies by Zeus out of gratitude for the nourishment she gave him as an infant. If this is so, then Amaltheia leaves her traces in geography as well.

12月21日，太阳运行到一年中最南端。此时，太阳的光芒直射一条穿过阿根廷北部、非洲南部和澳大利亚中部的直线。这条直线被称为“南回归线”，因为在每年的这个时候，太阳在其运行轨迹中位于摩羯座。

On December 21, the sun is as far south as it ever gets. It is then shining directly down on a line that passes through northern Argentina, southern Africa and central Australia. That line is called the “Tropic of Capricorn” because at that time of year the sun, in its travels, is in the constellation of Capricornus.

黄道星座的编号传统上是从春分时太阳所在的星座开始的（在希腊时代）。按照这种计数方法，摩羯座是黄道十二宫的第十个星座。

The constellations of the zodiac are traditionally numbered from the one in which (in Greek times) the sun is at the time of the spring equinox. By that count, Capricornus is the tenth “sign of the Zodiac.”

在希腊语中，还留存着阿玛尔忒亚的另一处痕迹。宙斯取下她的一只角，赋予它永远盛满食物和饮料的力量。这就是“丰饶之角”，更广为人知的拉丁语版本是“cornucopia”（丰饶角）。摩羯座较亮的几颗星（其实亮度都不高）形状酷似丰饶角。或许正是这一点，让希腊人最初认为它代表了阿玛尔忒亚。

Still another trace of Amaltheia exists in the language. Zeus took one of her horns and gave it the power of being forever filled with food and drink. This is the “horn of plenty” which is more often known by the Latin version of that phrase, the “cornucopia.” The brighter stars of Capricorn (none of which are very bright) are shaped something like a cornucopia. That may be what gave the Greeks the notion in the first place of saying that it represented Amaltheia.

另一个与宙斯相关的星座与仙女卡利斯托有关，我之前已经提到过她。当她化作熊的模样时，她的儿子阿尔卡斯发现了她。阿尔卡斯并不知道她是自己的母亲，便举起长矛要杀死她。为了阻止这一切，宙斯也把阿尔卡斯变成了熊，然后把他们两个都安置在了天空中。卡利斯托如今是大熊座，也就是“大熊”；而阿尔卡斯则是小熊座，也就是“小熊”。

Another constellation connected with Zeus involves the nymph, Callisto, whom I have already mentioned. When she was in the shape of a bear, her son, Arcas, came upon her. Not knowing she was his mother, he raised his spear to kill her. Zeus, to prevent this, changed him into a bear also and then placed both in the heavens. Callisto is now the constellation of Ursa Major, or “the Great Bear,” while Arcas is Ursa Minor or the “Little Bear.”

北极星是小熊座中的一颗星。希腊人知道，如果向北行进，北极星就会在天空中越升越高。随着北极星的升起，熊座的两个星座也随之升起。希腊语中“熊”一词是“arktos”，因此他们将北方地区称为“arktikos”。

The North Star is one of the stars in the constellation Ursa Minor. The Greeks knew that if one traveled northward, the North Star rose higher and higher in the skies. With it rose the two constellations of the bears. The Greek word for “bear” is “arktos” so they called the northern regions “arktikos.”

因此，我们将北极周围的区域称为“北极地区”，并以一条假想的线——“北极圈”——来界定它的范围。北极地区被冰雪覆盖的水体，当然就是“北冰洋”。

We therefore speak of the area around the North Pole as the “arctic zone,” and have it bounded by an imaginary line called the “arctic circle.” The body of water in the arctic zone, which is frozen over with ice, is, of course, the “Arctic Ocean.”

南极点周围是“南极带”，南极圈即为南极圈。前缀“ant-”意为“相反”，而南极的确位于地球与北极的相对一侧。南极带内的冰冻大陆称为“南极洲”，环绕它的水域称为“南极海”。

Around the South Pole is the “antarctic zone” bounded by the “antarctic circle.” The prefix “ant-” means “opposite” and the antarctic is, indeed, on the opposite side of the earth from the arctic. The frozen continent within the antarctic zone is “Antarctica,” and the water about it is the “Antarctic Ocean.”

天空中还有卡利斯托神话的另一处痕迹。在大熊座附近有一颗明亮的星星，似乎永远都在注视着它们（或许是赫拉派来的）。这颗星被称为“大角星”（Arcturus），源自希腊语，意为“守护大熊”。

There is still another trace of the Callisto myth in the sky. There is a bright star near the two bear constellations which seems forever to be watching them (sent by Hera, perhaps). This star is called “Arcturus” from Greek words meaning “to guard the bears.”

有时，宙斯会尊崇他化身的动物。例如，他为了掳走欧罗巴而化身的公牛，就被安置在天空中，被称为“金牛座”（Taurus），拉丁语中“公牛”的意思就是“公牛”。这是黄道十二宫的第二个星座。

On occasion, Zeus honored the animals into which he himself turned. For instance, the bull into which he turned himself to kidnap Europa was placed in the sky as “Taurus,” the Latin word for “the bull.” This is the second sign of the zodiac.

当然，公牛的最初含义可能纯粹是实用性的。太阳在早春进入金牛座，这可以被视为耕作的季节。在古代，人们用牛来耕地，所以你可以看出其中的联系。

Of course, the original meaning of the bull might be purely practical. The sun enters Taurus in early spring and this may be taken to symbolize the time of plowing. In ancient days, oxen and bullocks were used for plowing, so you see the connection.

也可能是因为宙斯化作鹰带走了伽倪墨得斯，而鹰被尊为星座“天鹰座”（拉丁语意为“鹰”）。

It may also be that the eagle, in the form of which Zeus carried off Ganymede, is honored as the constellation “Aquila,” which is Latin for “eagle.”

你或许会觉得这些星座中的个别恒星也被用来纪念神话传说。但实际上，大多数并非如此。大多数恒星都拥有中世纪时期赋予它们的阿拉伯名字。像大角星那样拥有可追溯至古希腊罗马时期名字的恒星，则只占少数。

It might seem to you that the individual stars in these constellations might also be used to memorialize myths. Actually, most of them do not. Most stars carry Arabic names given them in the Middle Ages. Those which, like Arcturus, have names dating back to Greek and Roman times, are in the minority.

克洛诺斯和瑞亚育有三女三子。其中，我提到了赫拉，她是宙斯的妻子，也是他的妹妹。她是婚姻、生育和女性事务的女神。另外两位分别是农业女神德墨忒尔和壁炉女神赫斯提亚，也就是家庭女神。

Cronus and Rhea had three daughters as well as three sons. Of these, I have mentioned Hera, who was Zeus’s wife as well as his sister. She was the goddess of marriage, childbirth and women’s affairs generally. The other two were Demeter, the goddess of agriculture, and Hestia, the goddess of the fireplace; that is, of the home.

克洛诺斯的女儿们在神话中不如他的儿子们那样出名。赫拉主要因其作为宙斯妻子的身份而为人所知，而赫斯提亚则鲜少被提及。德墨忒尔作为农业女神，与希腊人到来之前的古老宗教有着密切的联系。关于她的一个著名神话至今仍广为流传。

The daughters of Cronus are not as famous in myth as are the sons. Hera is important mainly as the wife of Zeus and Hestia is hardly ever mentioned. Demeter, as the goddess of agriculture, is involved with the older religions of the days before the Greeks came. One famous myth about her remained familiar.

德墨忒尔有个女儿，名叫珀耳塞福涅。珀耳塞福涅在西西里岛的田野里玩耍时，被爱慕她的冥王哈迪斯掳走了。德墨忒尔苦苦寻找女儿许久，却始终无果。悲痛欲绝的她拒绝让土地结出粮食，人类因此面临饥荒。

Demeter had a daughter, Persephone, who, while playing in the fields of Sicily, was carried off by Hades, who had fallen in love with her. Demeter searched for her without success for a long time and in her sorrow refused to let the ground bear grain. Mankind was faced with starvation.

宙斯说服哈迪斯允许珀耳塞福涅返回冥界，条件是她不能吃冥界里的任何食物。然而，在最后一刻，哈迪斯却设计让珀耳塞福涅吃了四颗石榴籽。结果，德墨忒尔不得不允许珀耳塞福涅每年在冥界待上四个月，每颗石榴籽对应四个月。

Zeus then persuaded Hades to let Persephone return provided she had eaten none of the food in the underground kingdom. At the last moment, though, Hades tricked Persephone into eating four pomegranate seeds. As a result, Demeter had to allow Persephone to remain underground with Hades four months of every year, one for each seed.

珀耳塞福涅被囚禁于地下期间，大地颗粒无收，树木落叶，就连阳光也几乎不见踪影。只有珀耳塞福涅归来，这位农业女神才允许大地恢复生机。

While Persephone was underground, the earth bore no grain, the trees lost their leaves and even the sun scarcely shone. Only with Persephone’s return, did the goddess of agriculture allow the earth to return to life.

这个神话旨在解释为什么每年冬天都会到来。

This myth is intended to explain why winter comes each year.

在雅典附近一个名为埃琉西斯的地区，德墨忒尔尤其受到崇拜。神话解释说，德墨忒尔在寻找珀耳塞福涅的途中经过埃琉西斯，在那里受到了热情款待，于是她教给他们一些祭祀仪式作为交换。

Demeter was especially worshiped at a district near Athens called Eleusis. The myth explained it by saying that Demeter had passed through Eleusis while looking for Persephone and had been kindly treated there so that she taught them certain rites in exchange.

事实上，这些仪式很可能在希腊人入侵之前就已经存在了。埃琉西斯秘仪与普通的奥林匹亚仪式截然不同。奥林匹亚仪式是公开举行的，任何人都可以参与。而埃琉西斯秘仪则是秘密进行的，只有经过特殊入会仪式的人才能参加。此外，入会者必须发誓永不泄露仪式内容（事实上也确实没有人泄露过）。发过这种誓的人被称为“密教徒”（mystes），这个词源于希腊语，意为“守口如瓶”。因此，这种秘密仪式被称为“秘仪”（mysteries）。古希腊存在许多类似的“神秘宗教”，但其中最著名的当属埃琉西斯秘仪。

Actually, though, the ceremonies probably existed before the Greeks invaded the land. The Eleusinian ceremonies were not like the ordinary Olympian ones. The Olympian rites were celebrated openly and anyone who liked could join in. The Eleusinian ceremonies were secret and no one could join until he had been specially initiated. Furthermore, one who was initiated had to swear never to reveal the rites (and no one ever did). Someone who had taken such an oath was called “mystes” from a Greek word meaning “close-mouthed.” As a result, such secret rites were called “mysteries.” There were a number of such “mystery religions” in ancient Greece, but of them all, the Eleusinian mysteries were the most famous.

当然，“神秘”一词的含义逐渐减弱，最终用来指代任何秘密或隐藏的事物。如今，它最常见的用法是指讲述扑朔迷离的犯罪案件，需要读者找到一个巧妙隐藏的答案才能破解的小说。

Of course, the word “mystery” gradually weakened and came to mean anything that was secret or hidden. The most common use, nowadays, is for a book about a puzzling crime to which someone must find an ingeniously hidden solution.

罗马人将他们自己的三位女神与克洛诺斯的三个女儿对应起来，而且在每一种情况下，我们今天更熟悉的都是罗马名字。赫拉对应着朱诺，也就是他们主神朱庇特的妻子；德墨忒尔对应着他们的农业女神刻瑞斯；赫斯提亚对应着他们的壁炉女神维斯塔。（此外，珀耳塞福涅的名字在罗马语中是普罗塞庇娜，这也是我们现在更熟悉的版本。）

The Romans identified three of their own goddesses with the three daughters of Cronus and in every case it is the Roman name that is better known to us today. With Hera, the Romans identified Juno, the wife of their Jupiter. With Demeter, they identified their goddess of agriculture, Ceres. And with Hestia, they identified their goddess of the fireplace, Vesta. (In addition, their version of the name Persephone is Proserpine and that, too, is the more familiar version now.)

这些女神留下的一些痕迹确实非常熟悉。第六个月是朱诺的圣月，至今仍被称为“六月”。朱诺（赫拉）是婚姻女神，六月至今仍被视为传统的结婚月份。许多女孩梦想着不仅成为新娘，而且成为“六月新娘”。

Some of the traces these goddesses have left are very familiar indeed. The sixth month was sacred to Juno and it is called “June” to this day. Juno (Hera) is the goddess of marriage and June is still considered the traditional month for marriage. Many girls dream of being not just a bride but a “June bride.”

刻瑞斯（德墨忒尔）是农业女神，尤其掌管谷物，例如小麦、玉米、大米、大麦和燕麦。这些是农业社会最重要的食物，它们因刻瑞斯而被称为“谷物”。

Ceres (Demeter) is the goddess of agriculture and particularly of grains, such as wheat, corn, rice, barley and oats. These are the most important foods in a farming society and they are called “cereals” after Ceres.

维斯塔的踪迹现在有点过时了。她是壁炉女神，火柴刚发明的时候看起来像微型壁炉，所以有一段时间，人们把火柴称为“维斯塔”。

Vesta’s trace is a little old-fashioned now. She was the goddess of the fireplace and when matches were first invented they seemed miniature fireplaces so, for a while, they were called “vestas.”

克洛诺斯的这些女儿中，没有一个被古希腊人列入已知的行星之列。然而，1801年，西西里天文学家朱塞佩·皮亚齐发现了一颗位于火星和木星之间的小行星，这为改变这一现状提供了契机。这颗行星比之前已知的任何行星都要小得多，直径仅为485英里。

None of these daughters of Cronus were honored among the planets known to the ancient Greeks. An opportunity to correct the situation arose in 1801, however, when a Sicilian astronomer, Giuseppe Piazzi, discovered a small planet which followed an orbit between Mars and Jupiter. It was far smaller than any planet previously known, being only 485 miles in diameter.

谷神星一直与西西里岛有着特殊的联系，西西里岛在古希腊和古罗马时期是重要的粮食产区。珀耳塞福涅正是从西西里岛被掳走的。因此，皮亚齐为了纪念他的故乡，提议将这颗小行星命名为谷神星。这个提议被采纳了。

Ceres had always been particularly connected with Sicily, which was an important grain-growing area in Greek and Roman times. It was from Sicily that Persephone was carried away. Piazzi therefore honored his native region by suggesting that this small planet be named Ceres. The suggestion was accepted.

完成上述步骤后，谷神星也被列入元素周期表。我之前提到过的克拉普罗特（Klaproth）有个习惯，喜欢用神话故事，尤其是与神话相关的行星来命名元素。因此，在1803年，他以谷神星的名字命名了一种新元素，“铈”（cerium）由此加入元素周期表。

Once that was done, Ceres entered the table of elements, too. Klaproth, whom I have mentioned before, had a habit of naming elements after myths and especially after new planets that involved myths. In 1803, he therefore named a new element after the planet Ceres, and “cerium” joined the list.

事实上，谷神星只是木星和火星之间众多微小天体中的第一个。这些天体被称为“小行星”、“行星体”或“矮行星”。1802年，人们发现了第二颗行星体；1804年，发现了第三颗；1807年，发现了第四颗。此后近四十年间，人们没有发现更多的小行星，直到更先进的望远镜以及最终摄影技术的应用，才使得数千颗小行星被发现。

Actually, Ceres proved to be only the first of a large number of tiny objects located between Jupiter and Mars. These are known as “asteroids” or “planetoids” or “minor planets.” In 1802, a second planetoid was found; in 1804, a third; and in 1807, a fourth. No more were found for nearly forty years and then better telescopes and, eventually, the use of photography resulted in the discovery of thousands.

谷神星这个名字的使用，开启了以女神的名字命名所有小行星（除了一些例外，我稍后会提到）的习俗。这使得天文学家有机会祭祀各种各样的次要女神和仙女。

The use of the name Ceres originated the custom of naming all the planetoids (with certain exceptions I’ll mention later) after goddesses. This gave astronomers a chance to honor all sorts of minor goddesses and nymphs.

最早受到尊崇的是克洛诺斯的女儿们。正如我之前所说，第一颗被发现的小行星被命名为谷神星（Ceres）。第三颗被发现的小行星被命名为“朱诺”（Juno），第四颗被命名为“灶神星”（Vesta）。

Among the first to be honored were the daughters of Cronus. The name of Ceres was given to the first planetoid, as I’ve said. The third to be discovered was named “Juno” and the fourth was named “Vesta.”

虽然灶神星不是最大的行星（谷神星才是），但它是最亮的。它是唯一一颗有时可以用肉眼勉强辨认出来的行星，因此以壁炉女神的名字命名非常贴切。

Although Vesta is not the largest planetoid (Ceres is), it is the brightest. It is the only planetoid that can sometimes, just barely, be made out with the naked eye, so it is well named after the goddess of the fireplace.

至于普罗塞庇娜，她的名字来源于已发现的第二十六颗小行星。

As for Proserpina, her name is that of the twenty-sixth planetoid to be discovered.

4 宙斯的子女

4 THE CHILDREN OF ZEUS

在希腊神话中，宙斯确立统治之后，再也没有发生过废黜事件。奥林匹斯诸神都生育了子女，但没有一个成功发动叛乱。相反，他们被和平地接纳进入奥林匹斯家族，与其他神祇平等地成为神（尽管他们仍然臣服于宙斯的统治之下）。

IN THE GREEK MYTHS, there were no further dethronements after Zeus established his power. The various Olympians had children but none rose in successful revolt. Instead they were peacefully allowed to enter the Olympian family and become gods and goddesses on an equal footing with the rest (although all remained under the rule of Zeus).

奥林匹斯诸神的子女很可能原本是各个土著部落的地方神祇，他们后来与入侵的希腊人结盟。事实上，几个神祇的名字可能被合并在一起。因此，关于同一个神祇，可能会流传着许多互不相干的故事。

Probably the various children of the Olympians were originally local gods and goddesses of various native tribes who entered into alliance with the invading Greeks. Indeed several gods or goddesses may have been combined under one name. For that reason, different stories that do not fit together may be told about a particular god or goddess.

例如，关于女神阿芙洛狄忒的诞生，有两个截然不同的故事。其中一个版本说，她是乌拉诺斯和盖亚的女儿。她乘着扇贝壳从海浪泡沫中升起，是泰坦神的姐妹，比奥林匹斯诸神古老得多。

For instance, there are two entirely separate stories about the birth of the goddess Aphrodite. In one version, she is supposed to have been the daughter of Uranus and Gaea. She rose from the sea foam on a scallop shell, a sister of the Titans and far older than the Olympians.

这很可能是一个关于女神的神话，这位女神曾被希腊人之前的民族所崇拜。希腊人到来后，将阿芙洛狄忒纳入奥林匹斯神系，认定她是宙斯和泰坦女神狄俄涅的女儿。

This is probably a myth about a goddess worshiped by the people who preceded the Greeks. When the Greeks arrived, they adopted Aphrodite into the Olympian family by making her the daughter of Zeus and of the Titaness Dione.

无论她的起源如何，希腊人都认为阿芙洛狄忒是众女神中最美丽的。她是美与爱的女神。罗马人则将他们自己的美神维纳斯与阿芙洛狄忒等同起来，而维纳斯对我们来说更为人熟知。

Regardless of her origin, the Greeks considered Aphrodite the most beautiful of the goddesses. She was the goddess of beauty and love. The Romans identified their own goddess of beauty, Venus, with Aphrodite, and Venus is more familiar to us.

无论以何种名字称呼，这位女神都是美的典范；也就是说，她是最初的模样，此后的一切都必须与之比较。因此，当我们想要赞美某个女孩的美貌时，我们会称她为维纳斯。罗马人对维纳斯如此崇敬，以至于“敬仰”（venerate）一词后来演变为尊敬和尊重。此外，由于年龄受到尊重（或者说应该受到尊重），老年人也被称为“受人尊敬的”（venerable）。

Under either name, the goddess is the prototype of beauty; that is, the original model to which everything since then must be compared. That is why when we wish to pay a high compliment to the good looks of some girl, we may call her a Venus. The Romans paid such honor to Venus that “venerate” has come to mean to honor and respect. Then, since age is respected, or should be, old men are said to be “venerable.”

维纳斯（或阿芙洛狄忒）佩戴一种名为“cestus”的腰带，据说可以增强她的魅力，使她令人无法抗拒。人们有时会说，一位美丽迷人的女子佩戴着“阿芙洛狄忒的腰带”。然而，动物学家却破坏了这种诗意，他们把“维纳斯腰带”或“cestus”的名字赋予了生活在海洋中的某些扁形虫，这些扁形虫体长约一英尺，形状酷似腰带。

Venus (or Aphrodite) wore a girdle or belt, called the “cestus,” which was supposed to increase her attractions and make her irresistible. A beautiful and charming woman is sometimes said to wear “Aphrodite’s girdle.” Zoologists spoil the poetry of that by giving the name “Venus-girdle” or “cestus” to certain flatworms living in the sea that are about a foot long and shaped just like a belt.

金星是唯一一位以女神的名字命名一颗重要行星的星体。这颗行星通常被称为“昏星”或“晨星”（取决于它位于太阳的哪一侧）。它是除太阳和月亮之外天空中最明亮、最美丽的星体，比任何恒星都要亮得多。

Venus is the one goddess whose name was given to an important planet. This is the planet commonly called the “evening star” or the “morning star” (depending on which side of the sun it happens to be). It is the brightest and most beautiful object in the heavens next to the sun and moon, and is far brighter than any star.

最初，希腊人认为它们是两颗不同的行星。他们将晨星称为磷星（Phosphorus，希腊语意为“光明使者”），因为一旦它出现在东方的天空，黎明的曙光就会到来。昏星则被称为赫斯珀洛斯（Hesperus，希腊语意为“西方”），因为它总是在日落后出现在西方的天空。

Originally the Greeks thought they were two different planets. They called the morning star Phosphorus (“light-bringer” in Greek) because once it appeared in the eastern skies, the light of dawn would soon come. The evening star was called Hesperus (“west” in Greek) because it always shone in the western sky after sunset.

希腊人得知磷星和赫斯珀洛斯星实际上是同一颗行星后，便将其命名为阿芙罗狄蒂，以赞美它的美丽。罗马人称之为金星，我们也沿用这个名字。

Once the Greeks learned that Phosphorus and Hesperus were really the same planet, they named it Aphrodite as befitted its beauty. The Romans called it Venus and so do we.

宙斯与一位泰坦女神所生的另一个女儿是雅典娜。神话中，宙斯吞下了墨提斯，墨提斯被认为是他的第一任妻子。之后他才娶了赫拉。墨提斯在宙斯腹中生下了一个女儿，宙斯是在一次剧烈头痛后才发现的。他的头痛如此剧烈，以至于不得不剖开头颅，这时，女神雅典娜从中跃然而出，她已完全长大成人，全副武装。

Another daughter of Zeus by a Titaness is Athena. The myth has Zeus swallowing Metis, a Titaness who was considered his first wife. He married Hera only afterward. Metis had a daughter within Zeus, and Zeus discovered this only after he found himself suffering from a bad headache. It was so bad that he had to have his head split open and when this was done, out sprang the goddess Athena, full grown and fully armed.

这听起来像是个有点荒诞的神话，但“metis”是希腊语中“智慧”的意思，而雅典娜被认为是知识女神，也是战争与和平艺术的掌管者。因此，这个神话的意思是，宙斯掌权后吸收了智慧，他头脑中的思想孕育了知识，而各种艺术也由此发展而来。

This seems like a rather silly myth, but “metis” is the Greek word for “wisdom” and Athena was considered the goddess of knowledge and of the arts of both war and peace. The myth is therefore a way of saying that after Zeus came to power he absorbed wisdom, and out of the thoughts in his head arose the knowledge out of which the arts developed.

雅典娜被认为是守护一座希腊城邦的女神，这座城邦以她的名字命名为“雅典娜”（Athenai）。在英语中，这座城市被称为“雅典”（Athens）。在希腊文明的鼎盛时期，雅典是所有希腊城邦中最强大、最富有、最文明的。没有哪座城市像雅典那样受到如此众多人的敬仰。时至今日，它仍然是现代希腊的首都。

Athena was considered the goddess in particular charge of a Greek city which was named “Athenai” in her honor. In English, this city is known as “Athens.” At the peak of Greek civilization, Athens was the most powerful, richest and civilized of all the Greek cities. No city has been as greatly admired by as many people as has Athens. It is still the capital of modern Greece.

由于雅典作为文明中心的盛名，许多自视为学术中心的城市仍然沿用这座希腊城市的名称。例如，波士顿就自诩为“美国的雅典”。

Because of the reputation of Athens as a chief center of civilization, cities that consider themselves to be a center of learning still call themselves after that Greek city. Boston considers itself the “Athens of America” for instance.

雅典娜的另一个名字是帕拉斯。关于她如何获得这个名字，其中一个传说是，雅典娜曾杀死一个名叫帕拉斯的巨人，并采用了他的名字。但更可能的情况是，一个崇拜帕拉斯女神的部落加入了希腊，帕拉斯就被等同于雅典娜。无论如何，在诗歌中，雅典娜经常被称为“帕拉斯·雅典娜”。

One of the alternate names of Athena is Pallas. One of the stories as to how she came to have this name was that at one time Athena killed a giant named Pallas and adopted his name. Probably what really happened is that a tribe worshiping a goddess named Pallas joined the Greeks and Pallas was identified with Athena. Anyway, in poetry, Athena was frequently referred to as “Pallas Athena.”

在已发现的小行星列表中，第二颗被发现的小行星（于1802年）被命名为帕拉斯。它的直径为304英里，是第二大的小行星。（第九颗被发现的小行星——于1848年——以雅典娜的母亲墨提斯的名字命名。）

In the list of the planetoids, the second one discovered (in 1802) was named Pallas. With a diameter of 304 miles, it is the second largest of the asteroids. (The ninth asteroid discovered—in 1848—was named Metis after the mother of Athena.)

帕拉斯几乎立刻就被列入了元素周期表。1803年，英国化学家威廉·海德·沃拉斯顿发现了一种新元素。克拉普罗斯刚刚以第一颗小行星谷神星（Ceres）的名字命名了铈（Cerium），沃拉斯顿也效仿，以帕拉斯的名字将他的新元素命名为“钯”（Palladium）。

Pallas entered the table of elements almost at once. In 1803, the English chemist, William Hyde Wollaston, discovered a new element. Klaproth had just named cerium after the first planetoid, Ceres, and Wollaston followed suit by naming his new element “palladium” after Pallas.

“帕拉迪姆”还有第二层含义。特洛伊城（我将在本书后面详细介绍）曾有一尊帕拉斯·雅典娜的雕像，被称为“帕拉迪姆”。当时流传着一个传说：只要帕拉迪姆还在城内，特洛伊就攻不破。（最终，他们失去了雕像，特洛伊也随之陷落。）如今，任何被认为能够守护国家或某种珍视的生活方式的物品或传统都被称为“帕拉迪姆”。例如，美国宪法就可以被视为我们自由的帕拉迪姆。

Palladium has a second meaning, too. The city of Troy (which I will talk about later in the book) had a statue of Pallas Athena which was called a “palladium.” There was a tradition that Troy could not be taken as long as the palladium was inside the city. (They eventually lost the statue and Troy fell.) Nowadays, any object or tradition which is supposed to guard a nation or some cherished way of life is called a “palladium.” The American constitution, for instance, can be considered the palladium of our liberties.

现代有些人根据“palladium”这个词的发音，误以为它指的是古代的马戏场或剧院，比如罗马斗兽场。因此，有时电影院也会以此命名。不过，雅典娜的名字却与一座真实的建筑联系在一起；这座建筑或许是人类历史上最美丽的建筑。由于雅典娜终身未婚，也没有任何恋情，希腊人常常称她为“雅典娜·帕特农”（Athena Parthenos，意为“处女雅典娜”）。公元前437年，雅典人为她建造了一座美丽的庙宇，并将其命名为“帕特农神庙”（Parthenon）。如今，它的遗址依然屹立，提醒着我们希腊最辉煌的时代。

Some people in modern times have thought, from the sound of the word, that a palladium was an ancient circus or theater, like the Colosseum. It sometimes happens, therefore, that a movie house may be given the name. Athena is associated with the name of an actual building, though; perhaps the most beautiful building ever erected. Since Athena never married nor had any love affairs, the Greeks often called her “Athena Parthenos” (“Athena the Virgin”). When the Athenians built her a beautiful temple, completed in 437 B.C., they called it the “Parthenon.” Its ruins still stand to remind us of the most glorious days of Greece.

罗马人将他们自己的实用艺术女神密涅瓦（源自拉丁语，意为“心灵”）与雅典娜等同起来。然而，或许是因为雅典城的盛名，在近代，希腊名字反而更为人熟知。

The Romans identified their own goddess of practical arts, Minerva (from a Latin word meaning “mind”), with Athena. However, probably because of the fame of the city of Athens, this was one case in which the Greek name remained more familiar in modern times.

宙斯的儿子之一是赫尔墨斯。他的母亲是迈亚，阿特拉斯的女儿之一。迈亚是七位女儿之一，她们被称为昴星团。昴星团曾被一位巨人猎人追赶。为了拯救她们，众神将她们变成了鸽子，并将她们安置在天空中，如今她们是金牛座中一小簇美丽的星星。其中一颗星至今仍以“迈亚”命名。另一颗星则以昴星团的父亲阿特拉斯的名字命名。

One of the sons of Zeus was Hermes. His mother was Maia, one of the daughters of Atlas. In particular she was one of seven daughters called the Pleiades. The Pleiades were at one time pursued by a giant hunter. To rescue them, the gods changed them into doves and then set them in the skies, where they are seen as a small and pretty cluster of stars in the constellation of Taurus. One of those stars still has the name “Maia.” Another is “Atlas,” after the father of the Pleiades.

虽然昴星团共有七颗星，但大多数人只能看到六颗。希腊人说，第七颗星因为爱上了凡人而非神，而感到羞愧和悲伤，黯淡了光芒。她被称为“失落的昴星团”。

Although there are seven Pleiades, most people can only see six. The seventh, say the Greeks, dimmed her light in shame and grief because she fell in love with a mortal man instead of with a god. She is referred to as the “lost Pleiad.”

事实上，昴星团的恒星数量远不止七颗。我们用肉眼只能看到其中最亮的几颗。用望远镜可以看到几十颗，用望远镜则可以看到数百颗。尽管如此，任何由七位名人组成的团体都被称为“昴星团”。

Actually, the Pleiades are a group of stars that are far more than seven in number. We only see the very brightest with the naked eye. A field glass shows dozens and a telescope will show hundreds. Despite that, any group of exactly seven famous people is called a “Pleiad.”

罗马也有一位名叫玛雅的女神。她与希腊的玛雅并无关联，但人们常常将两者混淆。罗马玛雅最重要的意义在于，五月正是以她的名字命名的。

There is a Roman goddess called Maia also. She had nothing to do with the Greek Maia, but there is often confusion between the two. The most important thing about the Roman Maia is that the month of “May” is named after her.

至于赫尔墨斯，这位希腊迈亚之子，被认为是众神的信使，因此自然被想象成速度极快。他通常被描绘成凉鞋和帽子上都长着翅膀。他也是商业、智慧和发明之神。

As for Hermes, the son of the Greek Maia, he was considered the messenger of the gods and so was naturally imagined as very swift. He is usually pictured with wings on his sandals and on his cap. He was also the god of commerce, cunning and invention.

后来，希腊人采用了埃及的学问之神赫尔墨斯，并将其与赫尔墨斯等同起来。他们将二者合称为“赫尔墨斯·特里斯墨吉斯忒斯”（意为“三重伟大的赫尔墨斯”）。赫尔墨斯·特里斯墨吉斯忒斯尤其被认为是化学知识之神，因为古埃及人在化学领域享有盛誉。（事实上，“化学”（chemistry）一词可能就源于埃及的古称。）因此，化学的一个古老名称是“赫尔墨斯艺术”（hermetic art）。

In later years, the Greeks adopted an Egyptian god of learning and identified him with Hermes. They called the combination “Hermes Trismegistus” (“Hermes the thrice-great”). Hermes Trismegistus was considered the god of chemical knowledge in particular, for the Egyptians had a great reputation as chemists in ancient days. (In fact, the very word “chemistry” may have come from an ancient name for Egypt.) As a result, an old-fashioned name for chemistry is the “hermetic art.”

“密封”一词更常见的用法源于化学家们经常捏紧容器的玻璃瓶颈，以防止容器内的物质与空气接触。任何气密的物体仍然被称为“密封的”。

A more familiar use of the term arises from the fact that chemists often pinched closed the glass neck of a vessel to keep the contents from exposure to air. Anything that is airtight is still said to be “hermetically sealed.”

在古代，君主之间或军队之间的信息传递都由“信使”负责。信使必须受到尊敬，不得受到伤害。他们手持一根特殊的权杖作为身份的象征，这根权杖被称为“双蛇杖”。赫尔墨斯在传递信息时也扮演着信使的角色，因此他手持这样一根权杖。由于赫尔墨斯行动迅捷，他的权杖、帽子和凉鞋都带有翅膀。

In ancient times, messages from one ruler to another or between armies were carried by “heralds.” These had to be treated with respect and could not be harmed. They carried a special staff as the sign of their office and this was called a “caduceus.” Since Hermes acted as a herald when he carried messages, he carried such a staff. Because Hermes moved so quickly, the staff, as well as his cap and sandals, carried wings.

后来，当他成为赫尔墨斯·特里斯墨吉斯忒斯时，他的团队又增添了一位成员。化学与医学密切相关，因此赫尔墨斯也与医学有着联系。（一种据说能治愈伤口的神秘粉末被称为“赫尔墨斯粉”。）在古代，医生与蛇有着密切的联系。这可能是因为蛇能够蜕皮，在希腊人看来，蛇蜕皮后似乎就能重返青春。这种重返青春的能力正是他们对医生的期望。

Later, when he became Hermes Trismegistus, there was an addition to the staff. Chemistry is associated with medicine and therefore so was Hermes. (A mythical powder supposed to be capable of curing wounds when applied to them was called a “hermetic powder.”) Now in ancient times, doctors were associated with snakes. This may have been because snakes had the ability to shed their skins, and seemed, to the Greeks, to be renewing their youth when they did so. This youth-renewal was what they expected or hoped for from doctors.

因此，赫尔墨斯的双蛇杖（最初可能缠绕着橄榄枝）上增添了两条盘绕的蛇。自此以后，这根带有翅膀和蛇的双蛇杖便成为医生和医学行业的象征。它至今仍是美国陆军医疗队的徽章。

Because of this, the caduceus of Hermes (which may have been wreathed with olive branches to begin with) gained two snakes which coiled about it. The caduceus, complete with wings and snakes, has symbolized doctors and the profession of medicine ever since. It still forms the insignia of the United States Army Medical Corps.

在天空中所有的行星中，那颗在星空中移动速度最快的行星自然而然地以迅捷的赫尔墨斯命名。罗马人将他们的商业之神墨丘利与赫尔墨斯联系起来，因此我们今天所知的这颗行星就是水星。

Now of all the planets in the sky, the one that moves most quickly against the background of the stars was naturally named after the swift-footed Hermes. The Romans identified their own god of commerce, Mercury, with Hermes, and so we know the planet as Mercury.

宙斯有一对双胞胎子女，他们的母亲是另一位泰坦女神勒托。罗马人称她为拉托娜，人们更熟悉她的这个名字。

Zeus had twin children of whom still another Titaness, Leto, was the mother. The Romans called her Latona and she is better known by that name.

嫉妒的赫拉不让勒托在任何陆地上安身产子。勒托只好逃到爱琴海最小的岛屿——提洛岛。提洛岛是一座漂浮的岛屿，因此不算作陆地。双胞胎阿波罗和阿尔忒弥斯出生后，提洛岛便沉入海底，不再漂浮。

The jealous Hera refused to allow Leto rest on any land in order to have the children. However, Leto fled to Delos, the smallest island in the Aegean Sea. This was a floating island so it didn’t count as land. After the birth of the twins, Apollo and Artemis, Delos was fixed to the sea bottom and floated no more.

由于这对双胞胎出生在岛上的辛瑟斯山上，所以他们有时被称为辛瑟斯和辛西娅。

Because they were born on Mount Cynthus on the island, the twins were occasionally called Cynthius and Cynthia.

两人都被描绘成年轻的弓箭手。阿波罗是男性美的化身，因此，一位非常英俊的男子可能会被称为“阿波罗”。他也是诗歌和音乐之神。罗马人没有与阿波罗对应的神祇，于是便沿用了这个名字。

Both are pictured as youthful archers. Apollo is the ideal of manly beauty, so that a very handsome man might be called an “Apollo.” He is also the god of poetry and music. The Romans had no equivalent for Apollo and they adopted him under that name.

阿尔忒弥斯是狩猎女神。罗马人将森林女神狄安娜与阿尔忒弥斯等同起来，而我们最熟悉的阿尔忒弥斯形象正是狄安娜。

Artemis is the goddess of the hunt. The Romans identified a forest goddess, Diana, with Artemis, and it is as Diana that she is most familiar to us.

勒托的母亲是泰坦女神福柏（她被尊为土星的第八颗卫星）。福柏在希腊语中意为“闪耀者”，在希腊人之前，她很可能是月亮或太阳的女神。希腊人将这一设定融入到自己的神话体系中，让阿波罗和阿尔忒弥斯成为福柏的孙辈，并赋予他们掌管太阳和月亮的权力。

Now the mother of Leto was the Titaness Phoebe (who was honored as the eighth satellite of Saturn). Phoebe, whose name in Greek means “the shining one,” was probably a goddess of either the moon or the sun in the days before the Greeks. The Greeks forced this into their own system of myths by making Apollo and Artemis the grandchildren of Phoebe and letting them rule the sun and moon.

阿波罗被认为是太阳神，甚至取代了福柏名字的男性版本。他常被称为“福玻斯·阿波罗”或简称“福玻斯”。阿尔忒弥斯（因此也包括罗马神话中的狄安娜）是月亮女神。这是奥林匹斯诸神取代泰坦神的另一个例子，阿波罗取代了赫利俄斯，阿尔忒弥斯取代了塞勒涅。然而，直到最后，希腊人仍然会提及赫利俄斯和塞勒涅，以及阿波罗和阿尔忒弥斯，因此这种取代并不完美。

Apollo was considered the god of the sun and even took over a masculine version of Phoebe’s name. He was frequently called “Phoebus Apollo” or just “Phoebus.” Artemis (and, therefore, the Roman Diana as well) was the goddess of the moon. This is another example in which the Olympians replaced the Titans, Apollo replacing Helios and Artemis replacing Selene. However, until the very end, the Greeks talked of Helios and Selene as well as of Apollo and Artemis, so the replacement wasn’t perfect.

阿波罗和阿尔忒弥斯的箭矢总是能杀人，希腊人便以此解释疾病的流行，认为勒托的儿女们四处射箭，伤害人们。自然而然地，人们相信向阿波罗祈祷就能止息瘟疫。就这样，阿波罗与治愈疾病联系在了一起。

The arrows of Apollo and Artemis invariably killed and the Greeks explained epidemics of disease by supposing that the children of Leto were shooting at people right and left. Naturally, by praying to Apollo, the epidemic might be made to stop. In this way, Apollo became associated with the cure of disease.

这衍生出一个神话，传说阿波罗有一个儿子叫阿斯克勒庇俄斯，他更为人熟知的名字是罗马神话中的埃斯库拉庇俄斯。他最终成为医药之神，但起初他只是一位普通的医生。然而，他的医术如此高明，从未让任何病人死亡，而且他甚至能够使死者复生。

This led to a myth in which Apollo was thought to have had a son Asclepius, who is better known under the Roman version of the name, Aesculapius. He was the god of medicine and healing, eventually, but at first he was considered a merely human doctor. However, he was such a good doctor that he never lost a patient and, in fact, he could raise the dead back to life.

哈迪斯抱怨生意失败，为了维护家族和睦，宙斯用雷霆击杀了阿斯克勒庇俄斯。阿斯克勒庇俄斯死后被封为神，并被列入星座，其形象是一位手持蛇的男子。正如我在本章前面解释过的，蛇是医药和医生的象征。该星座的拉丁文名称是“Ophiuchus”，意为“持蛇者”。有时，组成蛇的星群被称为一个独立的星座，称为“Serpens”（巨蛇座），有时整个星座则被称为“Serpentarius”（蛇夫座）。

Hades complained about losing business and to keep peace in the family, Zeus killed Asclepius with a thunderbolt. After his death, he was made a god. He was also placed among the constellations, where he is pictured as a man holding a serpent in his hands. The serpent, as I explained earlier in the chapter, is a symbol of medicine and doctors. The Latin name of the constellation is “Ophiuchus” which means “serpent-holder.” Sometimes the stars forming the serpent are referred to as a separate constellation called “Serpens” (“serpent”) and occasionally the whole constellation is referred to as “Serpentarius.”

你或许会好奇宙斯的子女中是否有人以赫拉为母。答案是肯定的。阿瑞斯就是宙斯和赫拉的儿子。他是残暴嗜血的战神，酷爱战争。每当他出征，他的儿子福波斯和得摩斯就会为他准备战车。由于“福波斯”在希腊语中意为“恐惧”，“得摩斯”意为“恐怖”，这恰恰说明战争总是伴随着恐惧和恐怖。

You might wonder if any of the children of Zeus had Hera for a mother. The answer is yes. Ares was the son of Zeus and Hera. He is the cruel and bloody god of war, who delighted in battle. When he went into battle, his sons, Phobos and Deimos, prepared his chariot. Since “phobos” is the Greek word for “fear” and “deimos” for “terror,” this is just a way of saying that war is attended by fear and terror.

福波斯在现代心理学中留下了印记，因为“恐惧症”指的是任何异常的恐惧。例如，“幽闭恐惧症”是对封闭空间的异常恐惧，“广场恐惧症”是对开阔空间的异常恐惧。某种疾病会影响神经，导致患者如果尝试饮水就会抽搐。因此，这种疾病被称为“恐水症”（“怕水”），因为古希腊人认为这种抽搐是由怕水引起的，而不是像我们现在所知的，是由病毒引起的。

Phobos leaves his mark in modern psychology, for a “phobia” is any abnormal fear. “Claustrophobia” is the abnormal fear of closed places, for instance, and “agoraphobia” is the abnormal fear of wide open spaces. A certain disease affects the nerves so that the sufferer falls into convulsions if he tries to drink. It is therefore called “hydrophobia” (“fear of water”) because the Greeks thought the convulsions were due to a fear of water instead of to a virus, as we now know they are.

希腊人将第四颗行星命名为阿瑞斯星，这似乎顺理成章，因为这颗行星闪耀着如同鲜血般的红色光芒。这与战神的身份十分相符。

It seems natural that the Greeks named the fourth planet after Ares, since the fourth planet shines with a ruddy color, resembling blood. That is fit for the god of war.

罗马人将他们的战神玛尔斯与阿瑞斯等同起来，因此我们今天才称这颗行星为火星。最初，玛尔斯是罗马最重要的神祇之一，一年中有一个月专门献给他。为了纪念他，我们至今仍将三月称为“三月”。

The Romans identified their god of war, Mars, with Ares, so we know the planet as Mars. Originally, Mars was one of the most important of the Roman gods and an entire month of the year was dedicated to him. We still call the third month “March” in his honor.

直到1877年，人们才发现火星拥有卫星。那一年，美国天文学家阿萨夫·霍尔发现了两颗火星卫星。它们非常小，直径只有五到十英里，但它们确实是火星卫星。霍尔将它们命名为“火卫一”（Phobos）和“火卫二”（Deimos），寓意在天体上，如同神话和现实中一样，战争总是伴随着恐惧和恐怖。

It wasn’t until 1877 that the planet Mars was found to have any satellites. In that year, the American astronomer Asaph Hall discovered two of them. They were very tiny, being only five or ten miles in diameter, but they were satellites. Hall named them “Phobos” and “Deimos” so that in the heavens, as in the myths and in reality, war is accompanied by fear and terror.

希腊神话中的战神阿瑞斯（Ares）的名字至今仍在天空中留下印记。有一颗明亮的红色恒星，颜色与火星相同，亮度也几乎一样。这颗星被称为“心宿二”（Antares），意思是“与火星相对”或“平衡火星，如同跷跷板一样”。

The Greek name, Ares, still leaves its trace in the sky. There is a bright red star, of the same color as Mars and almost as bright. This star is called “Antares,” which means “opposite to Mars” or balancing it, as on a seesaw.

实际上，两者的平衡非常不平衡，因为火星是一颗相当小的行星，直径仅略超过4000英里，质量也只有地球的十分之一左右。而心宿二则是已知最大的恒星之一，一颗比太阳大得多的巨型红巨星。

Actually the balance is very poor, because Mars is a rather small planet only a little over 4000 miles in diameter and only about 1/10 as massive as the earth. Antares, on the other hand, is one of the largest stars known, a giant red star far, far larger than our sun.

金星没有卫星。然而，与金星（或者更确切地说是阿芙洛狄忒）相关的神话人物却有机会与金星产生联系。这个机会最早出现在1898年，当时德国天文学家G·维特发现了一颗新的小行星，这是迄今为止发现的第433颗小行星。

The planet Venus has no satellites. Nevertheless, mythical characters associated with Venus (or Aphrodite, rather) did get their chance. That chance first came in 1898, when the German astronomer G. Witt discovered a new planetoid, the 433rd to be discovered.

他发现，尽管当时已知的所有小行星的轨道都位于火星和木星之间，但这颗小行星却并非如此。它的轨道位于火星和地球之间。它的轨道比曾经与我们距离最近的行星邻居——火星或金星——的轨道更接近地球轨道。因此，维特开始寻找一个既能与火星（阿瑞斯）相配，又能与金星（阿芙罗狄蒂）相配的名字。

He found that although all the planetoids then known had orbits that lay between Mars and Jupiter, this one did not. Its orbit lay between Mars and the earth. Its orbit approached that of the earth more closely than did the orbits of either Mars or Venus which were once our closest planetary neighbors. Witt therefore looked for a name that would go with both Mars (Ares) and Venus (Aphrodite).

巧合的是，阿瑞斯和阿芙洛狄忒的画像中有一个名叫厄洛斯的幼子。他是年轻的爱神，通常被描绘成一个手持弓箭的孩童。当他的箭射中一颗心时，那个人就会坠入爱河。有时他被描绘成蒙着眼睛，暗示年轻人可能会盲目地坠入爱河。厄洛斯被认为是阿瑞斯和阿芙洛狄忒的孩子，这或许是希腊人用来表达美丽女性特别容易被士兵吸引，反之亦然的一种方式。也许真是如此。我们有句谚语：“只有勇敢的人才配得上美丽的女人。”

It so happens that Ares and Aphrodite were pictured as having a young son named Eros. He is the young god of love and is pictured as a child with a bow and arrow. When one of his arrows strikes a heart, that person falls in love. Sometimes he is shown blindfolded to indicate that young people may fall in love blindly. The fact that Eros is supposed to be the child of Ares and Aphrodite may be the Greek way of saying that beautiful women are particularly attracted to soldiers, and vice versa. Perhaps so. We have a saying, “Only the brave deserve the fair.”

总之，情人节卡片和卡通片上仍然可以看到爱神厄洛斯的身影。我们也能看到穿过箭矢的心形图案，用来表示爱情。我们更熟悉的是厄洛斯的罗马名字——丘比特。这两个名字都留下了痕迹。任何能唤起浪漫爱情的事物都被认为是“情色的”。然而，爱还有其他类型。例如，这位爱神罗马名字的词根就出现在“贪婪”（cupidity）一词中，用来形容对金钱或物质的过度贪恋。

Anyway, we still have Eros on Valentine cards and in cartoons. We also see hearts with arrows through them to indicate love. We know Eros better by the equivalent Roman name of Cupid. Both names have left traces. Anything which arouses feelings of romantic love is said to be “erotic.” However, there are other types of love, too. Thus, the Roman name of the little god of love is to be found in the word “cupidity” which describes too much love of money or material things.

于是，维特将他新发现的小行星（其最长方向只有 15 英里）命名为“爱神星”。这开启了一种风潮，即用男性化的名字来命名那些运行在火星和木星之间空间之外、轨道异常的小行星。

So Witt named his new little asteroid (it is only 15 miles through in its longest direction) “Eros.” This started a fashion of naming planetoids which followed unusual orbits, outside the space between Mars and Jupiter, with masculine names.

二十世纪期间，人们发现了一些比火星或金星更接近地球的小行星。其中一些被赋予了与阿芙罗狄蒂相关的名字。一颗名为“安忒洛斯”（Anteros，意为“与厄洛斯相对”），另一颗名为“阿莫尔”（Amor），这是拉丁语中“爱”的另一个词。

During the twentieth century, other small asteroids have been found which approach the earth more closely than either Mars or Venus. Some have received other names connected with Aphrodite. One is “Anteros” (“opposite to Eros”) and another is called “Amor,” which is another Latin word for “love.”

还有一位名叫“阿多尼斯”的神，是以阿芙洛狄忒爱上的一位年轻男子的名字命名的。他被描绘得如此英俊，以至于如今人们仍然用“阿多尼斯”和“阿波罗”来指代相貌英俊的男子。

Then there is one called “Adonis” after a young man with whom Aphrodite had been in love. He was pictured as so handsome, that a good-looking man is still referred to as an “Adonis” as well as an Apollo.

另外两颗小行星分别被命名为“阿波罗”和“赫尔墨斯”。赫尔墨斯是一颗非常特别的小行星，它有时能接近到距离地球仅20万英里（约32万公里）的地方。这比任何其他可见天体都能达到的距离都要近，甚至比我们的月球还要近。

Two others have been named “Apollo” and “Hermes.” Hermes is a very remarkable little planetoid in that it can sometimes approach within 200,000 miles of the earth. This is closer than any other visible body can approach. It is an even closer approach than our own moon can manage.

赫拉的另一个孩子是赫菲斯托斯。他是一位与众不同的神，因为他身体残疾，是个瘸子。关于他残疾的原因，有一种说法是赫拉因为他出生时体弱多病而感到羞愧，将他从奥林匹斯山上扔了下去。另一种说法是，宙斯因为他站在母亲一边，在赫拉与宙斯的争吵中，将他从天上扔了下去。无论哪种说法，他都从天上摔到了地上，因此落下了跛脚的毛病。

Another child of Hera’s was Hephaestus. He was an exceptional god in that he had a physical deformity, for he was lame. One story to account for this was that Hera dropped him from Olympus in shame because he was weakly at birth. Another is that Zeus threw him from heaven because he sided with his mother during one of her quarrels with Zeus. In either case, he fell all the way from heaven to earth, and was lame as a result.

他是铁匠之神，总是被描绘成在铁匠铺里工作。他是唯一一个被描绘成亲手工作的神。

He was the god of smiths and was always pictured working at his forge. He was the only god shown working with his hands.

罗马人自己也有一位掌管火与锻造的神，他们想象这位神在西西里岛那座伟大的火山——埃特纳火山的深处工作。罗马人称这位神为伏尔甘（Vulcan），并将其与赫菲斯托斯（Hephaestus）等同起来。伏尔甘（Vulcan）这个名字对我们来说更为熟悉。原因之一是，所有像埃特纳火山那样喷发火焰的山峰，现在都被称为“火山”。

The Romans had a god of fire and forge themselves, whom they pictured as working in the depths of Mount Etna, the great Sicilian volcano. The Romans called this god Vulcan and identified him with Hephaestus. Vulcan is much the more familiar name to us. For one thing, all mountains that, like Mount Etna, belch fire and flame are now called “volcanoes.”

这个名字的另一个线索与橡胶有关。19世纪初，人们尝试将橡胶用于防水服装。不幸的是，橡胶在温暖的天气里会变得柔软粘腻，而在寒冷的天气里则会变得坚硬如皮革。化学家们试图找到解决这个问题的方法。

Another trace of the name is to be found in a matter concerning rubber. In the first part of the 1800’s, attempts were made to use rubber in waterproof clothing. Unfortunately, the rubber got soft and sticky in warm weather and hard and leathery in cold weather. Chemists tried to find ways to prevent this.

1839年，美国发明家查尔斯·古德伊尔偶然发现，如果将橡胶和硫磺一起加热，橡胶就会变得干燥且富有弹性，无论在炎热还是寒冷的天气下都能保持这种特性。这样一来，橡胶终于可以应用于许多实际用途。这一过程需要用到热能，因此，这种橡胶被称为“硫化橡胶”。

In 1839, an American inventor named Charles Goodyear discovered by accident that if rubber and sulfur were heated together, the rubber became dry and flexible in both hot and cold weather. In this way, rubber could finally be put to many practical uses. The process by which this was done involved the use of heat. Consequently, such rubber was said to be “vulcanized.”

然而，铁匠之神的名字在历史上最引人注目的一段插曲发生在硫化技术被发现之后不久。当时，水星绕太阳运行的轨道上存在着一种非常细微的不均匀性，天文学家对此无法解释。

However, the most dramatic episode in the history of the name of the god of the smiths came shortly after the discovery of vulcanization. The planet Mercury had a very small unevenness in its path of travel about the sun which astronomers could not account for.

1845年，勒维耶在研究即将被发现的海王星的位置时，也着手解决这个问题。他认为可能存在一颗比水星更靠近太阳的行星。这颗行星的引力可能正在牵引水星，从而解释水星运动的不均匀性。

In 1845, Le Verrier, who was in the process of working out the position of the soon-to-be-discovered planet Neptune, tackled this problem also. He thought there might be a planet even closer to the sun than Mercury was. Its gravitational pull might be tugging at Mercury and that would account for the unevenness in its motion.

这样一颗行星比任何其他行星都更接近太阳的炽热光芒。它就像一位铁匠在太阳系的熔炉旁工作。因此，勒维耶建议将这颗新行星命名为“火神星”。

Such a planet would be closer to the fire of the sun than any other would be. It would be like a smith working at the very forge of the solar system. Le Verrier therefore suggested that the new planet be called “Vulcan.”

然而，尽管天文学家多年来一直在寻找火神星，却始终未能找到它。直到1915年，阿尔伯特·爱因斯坦提出了一项新理论，完美地解释了水星运动的不均匀性。这样一来，就没有必要存在任何新的行星了，因此，火神星最终被证明并不存在。

However, although astronomers searched for Vulcan for many years they could never find it. Then, in 1915, Albert Einstein advanced a new theory which explained perfectly the unevenness in Mercury’s motion. There was no need for any new planet and so it turned out that the planet Vulcan did not exist after all.

我已经描述了太阳系所有主要行星名称的由来。不过，在结束对行星的探讨之前，我想描述一下它们是如何进一步传播神话痕迹的。

I have now described the origin of the names of all the major planets of the solar system. Before leaving the planets, however, I would like to describe some ways in which they continued to spread the traces of myth even farther.

例如，早在现代天文学出现之前，人们就发明了符号来代表不同的行星。这些符号与神灵有所关联，而且由于这些符号至今仍在使用，因此神话的痕迹仍然存在。

For instance, long before the days of modern astronomy, symbols were invented to represent the different planets. These involved some reference to the god, and since the symbols are still used today, the traces of myth remain.

因此，水星的符号是☿，它简略地描绘了蛇杖，两条蛇缠绕在一起，顶端长着翅膀。金星的符号是♀，它是一面镜子，这无疑是与美之女神联系在一起的自然之物。

Thus, the symbol of the planet Mercury is ☿, which is a crude picture of the caduceus, with the two serpents twining and the wings on top. The symbol of Venus is ♀, which is a looking glass, surely a natural object to connect with a goddess of beauty.

火星的符号是♂，它象征着盾牌和长矛，很适合这位战神。

The symbol of Mars is ♂, which is supposed to be a shield and spear, suitable for the god of war.

♃ 的含义更难确定，它是朱庇特的象征。有人认为它是一只展开双翼的鹰的简略图案。鹰是宙斯在冒险途中化身为的众多生物之一。

It is more difficult to work out the meaning of ♃, which is the symbol of Jupiter. Some think it is a crude picture of an eagle with its wings outstretched. An eagle was one of the creatures into which Zeus turned himself on his adventures.

土星的符号是♄，也就是克洛诺斯用来击败他父亲乌拉诺斯的镰刀。

The symbol for Saturn is ♄, the scythe with which Cronus defeated his father, Uranus.

在近代发现的行星中，天王星和冥王星拥有现代符号，这些符号都包含字母。然而，海王星的符号是♆。这象征着海神波塞冬用来控制海浪的特殊三叉戟。它被称为“三叉戟”，源自拉丁语，意为“三颗牙齿”。

Of the planets discovered in modern times, Uranus and Pluto have modern symbols, involving letters of the alphabet. The symbol of Neptune, however, is ♆. This symbolizes the special three-pronged spear which Poseidon used to control the waves. It is called a “trident” from Latin words meaning “three teeth.”

其中两个符号在天文学以外的领域也有应用。当科学家追溯某人或某动物的血统，以确定某些性状的遗传方式时，通常用金星符号（♀）表示女性，用火星符号（♂）表示男性。

Two of these symbols are used in fields other than astronomy. When a scientist is tracing the line of descent of some man or animal, in order to determine the manner in which certain traits are inherited, he usually indicates females by the symbol for Venus (♀) and males by the symbol for Mars (♂).

古人认为七大行星（包括太阳、月亮以及水星、金星、火星、木星和土星）对人类事务至关重要。研究出生时七大行星在星空中的位置，据说可以预知人的命运。这种研究被称为“占星术”，尽管科学家们确信它完全是无稽之谈，但仍有许多人相信其中蕴含着一些道理。

The ancients felt that the seven planets (they included the sun and moon along with Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter and Saturn) were particularly important in the affairs of men. A study of their position among the stars at the moment of his birth was supposed to give information about his fate. This type of study is called “astrology” and there are many people who still believe there is something in it, although scientists are certain it is complete foolishness.

根据占星家的计算，一个人的总体性格被认为是由他“出生在”哪个行星之下决定的，而这种迷信的痕迹至今仍然存在于我们的语言中。

The general character of a person was thought to be determined by which planet he was “born under,” according to the calculations of astrologers, and there are traces of this superstition in our language, still.

例如，水星是恒星运行速度最快的行星。因此，出生在水星之下的人应该机智敏捷、变化无常、活泼开朗。这就是“mercurial”（善变的）一词的含义。另一方面，出生在运行缓慢的土星之下的人则性格沉闷、阴郁、迟钝，这就是“saturnine”（土星般的）一词的含义。

For instance, the planet Mercury moves faster among the stars than does any other planet. A person born “under Mercury” would therefore have to be quick-witted and changeable, lively and gay. This is what is meant by the word “mercurial.” On the other hand, those born under Saturn, the slow-moving planet, are heavy, gloomy and dull, and this is what is meant by the word “saturnine.”

火星所生的人自然好战，或者说“好战”。更令人惊讶的是，木星所生的人却被认为是快乐的人。正因如此，“jovial”（快乐的）这个形容词才有了“joous”（喜悦的）的意思。

Someone born under Mars would be warlike, of course, or “martial.” More surprising is the fact that those born under Jupiter are thought to be happy people. It is for this reason that the adjective “jovial” means joyous.

至于月亮，人们认为它会扰乱人的心神。人们怀疑，在满月的月光下待着会导致精神错乱。这只是迷信，但语言中仍然保留着“moon-mad”（月痴狂）、“moon-sick”（月病）和“moon-struck”（月迷）之类的词语，都表示“疯狂”，而“moon-calf”（月痴）则指傻瓜。

As for the moon, it was thought to have an unsettling effect on people’s minds. To be out in the light of the full moon would lead to madness, people suspected. This is just superstition, but the language still has words like “moon-mad,” “moon-sick” and “moon-struck,” all meaning “crazy,” while a “moon-calf” is a fool.

“疯子”这个词更体面的说法是“lunatic”，但它源自罗马月亮女神卢娜（Luna）。而在日常口语中，它被缩写成了不太体面的“loonies”。

A more dignified word for “crazy” is “lunatic” but that comes from Luna, the Roman goddess of the moon. And it has been shortened in common speech to the not so dignified “loonies.”

七大行星中的每一颗都应负责一周中的某一天，具体如下：第一天（太阳）；第二天（月亮）；第三天（火星）；第四天（水星）；第五天（木星）；第六天（金星）；第七天（土星）。

Each of the seven planets was also supposed to have a special day of the week in its charge, as follows: First day (Sol or the sun); Second day (Luna or the moon); Third day (Mars); Fourth day (Mercury); Fifth day (Jupiter); Sixth day (Venus) and Seventh day (Saturn).

拉丁语中，一周中的每一天都以行星的名字命名。这些名称在与拉丁语密切相关的语言中也沿用了下来。例如，在法语中，一周的第二天、第三天和第四天分别是“lundi”、“mardi”和“mercredi”。

The days of the week were named after the planets in the Latin language. These names held on in the languages closely related to Latin. For instance, in French, the second, third and fourth days of the week are “lundi,” “mardi,” and “mercredi.”

然而，在英语中，除了第七天之外，一周中的其他日子都以北欧诸神的名字命名，这些诸神是盎格鲁-撒克逊人在皈依基督教之前所崇拜的。北欧诸神与行星之间存在着某种粗略的对应关系。例如，英语中一周的第一天和第二天分别是“星期日”（Sunday）和“星期一”（Monday）。但第七天的情况则截然不同。不知何故，它保留了拉丁语名称“星期六”（Saturday）——意为“土星日”。

In English, however, all the days of the week but the seventh are named after Norse gods, which were worshiped by the Anglo-Saxons before their conversion to Christianity. The Norse gods corresponded to the planets in a rough sort of way, however. For instance, the first and second days of the week, in English, are “Sunday” and “Monday.” It is the seventh day that is the clear case, however. For some reason, it retained the Latin name of “Saturday”—“Saturn’s day.”

中世纪的炼金术士注意到有七大行星和七种金属。他们将天体与金属对应起来，因此，化学中也出现了神话人物的痕迹。炼金术士所知的七种金属分别是（英文）：金、银、铜、铁、锡、铅和水银。

The alchemists of the Middle Ages noticed that there were seven planets and seven metals. They matched up heavenly objects and metals and, as a result, additional traces of mythical characters are to be found in chemistry. The seven metals known to the alchemists were (in English): gold, silver, copper, iron, tin, lead, and quicksilver.

由于金银的颜色，它们分别与太阳、白银和月亮对应起来。铜是第三大最有价值的金属，因此它与金星——天空中第三亮的星体——相对应。

Because of their colors, gold was matched with the sun and silver with the moon. The third most valuable metal was copper so that was matched with Venus, the third brightest object in the heavens.

制造战争武器的铁自然与火星相对应；而沉重而黯淡的铅则与沉重而黯淡的土星相对应。另一方面，水银呈液态且易于流动，因此与运行迅速的行星水星相对应。剩下的锡则与另一颗行星木星相对应。

Iron, which made the weapons of war, was naturally matched with Mars, while lead, the heavy, dull metal, was matched with Saturn, the heavy, dull planet. On the other hand, quicksilver was liquid and moved about easily, so it was matched with the quick-moving planet, Mercury. That left tin, which was matched with the remaining planet, Jupiter.

当然，随着化学家对化学了解的加深，大部分此类命名方式都消失了。一方面，人们发现了更多金属，虽然一些新金属仍然以新发现的行星命名，但大多数金属并非如此。炼金术士真正成功地将一种元素的名称改为行星名称的情况仅出现过一次。如今，水银被称为“汞”。

Naturally, most of this vanished as chemists grew to know more of the subject. For one thing, many more metals were discovered, and though some new metals continued to be named after new planets, most were not. In only one case did alchemists actually succeed in changing the name of an element to the name of a planet. Quicksilver is known as “mercury” these days.

在其他情况下，仅留下零星痕迹。最著名的例子是硝酸银化合物，它至今仍保留着“月光苛性碱”这个古老的名称。“苛性碱”一词源于希腊语，意为“腐蚀性的”，指的是它与硝酸（一种强腐蚀性物质）有关。“月光”则指的是其中含有银。

In the other cases only occasional traces remain. The best-known example is the compound silver nitrate, which still carries the old-fashioned name of “lunar caustic.” The “caustic,” from a Greek word meaning “biting,” refers to the fact that it is related to nitric acid, a strong and biting substance. The “lunar” refers to the fact that silver is involved.

还有一种由铅和氧组成的鲜红色化合物，俗称“赤铅”。不过，由于它含有铅——土星的金属——所以有时也被称为“土星红”。此外，还有一系列不同颜色的铁化合物，它们的名字都带有与火星相关的古老色彩，例如火星黄、火星棕、火星橙、火星紫等等。

Then there is a bright-red compound of lead and oxygen which is commonly known as “red lead.” It is still, however, sometimes referred to as “Saturn red” because of its content of lead, the Saturnian metal. There is also a whole series of iron compounds of different colors which have old-fashioned names involving Mars, the planet matched with iron: Mars yellow, Mars brown, Mars orange, Mars violet and so on.

但中世纪炼金术士的影响仅限于此。现代化学家几乎完全摆脱了这种影响。

But there the influence of the medieval alchemist ends. The modern chemist has shaken himself free almost entirely.

5 半神与怪物

5 DEMIGODS AND MONSTERS

在希腊神话的诸神之中，存在着许多次要角色。其中一些被称为“半神”。他们的力量通常弱于神，但强于凡人。有时，他们原本是凡人，死后却能成神。阿斯克勒庇俄斯就是我之前提到的一个例子。

THERE ARE any number of minor characters among the gods and goddesses of the Greek myths. Some of these are referred to as “demigods” (“half-gods”). These are usually less powerful than gods but more powerful than human beings. Sometimes they are mortal to begin with but become gods after they die. Asclepius is one example to which I have already referred.

狄俄尼索斯是位列半神之首的神祇之一。他原本是农业之神，后来成为神秘宗教的核心人物。他最特别之处在于，他曾被杀死，却又复活，正如珀耳塞福涅下到冥界又返回人间一样。在所有神秘宗教中，死亡与重生都扮演着重要的角色，因为这象征着植物在冬季的凋零和在春季的复苏。

One god who was in the top rank of demigods was Dionysus. He was originally a god of agriculture, who was made the center of a mystery religion. The important thing about him was that he was killed but brought back to life, just as Persephone went down to Hades and returned. In all mystery religions, death and rebirth plays a part because that symbolizes the death of vegetation in the winter and its rebirth in the spring.

后来，狄俄尼索斯被称为巴克斯。为纪念他而举行的庆祝活动被称为“狄俄尼索斯节”或“酒神节”。这些庆祝活动非常狂野，通常有女性参与，她们被称为“酒神女祭司”。如今，“酒神节”一词指代任何狂野的庆祝活动，而任何情绪失控到近乎疯狂的女性都被称为“酒神女祭司”。

Later on, Dionysus was known by the name of Bacchus. Celebrations in his honor were called “Dionysia” or “Bacchanalia.” They were very wild and usually involved women, who were called “Bacchantes.” The word “bacchanalia” now refers to any wild celebration, while any woman overcome with emotion to the point of madness is called a “bacchante.”

狄俄尼索斯声名鹊起，最终被纳入奥林匹斯众神之列。希腊神话中，他是宙斯的儿子，母亲是一位名叫塞墨勒的凡人女子。塞墨勒很可能原本是一位大地女神，在酒神节中扮演着重要角色。

Dionysus grew so popular that he was adopted into the Olympian family. The Greek mythmakers had him the son of Zeus, while his mother was a mortal woman named Semele. Probably Semele was herself an earth goddess originally who played a part in the Bacchanalia.

潘神是位地位较低的神祇，据说是赫尔墨斯的儿子。潘神掌管田野和森林，实际上是万物之灵，他的名字可能就来源于此，因为“潘”在希腊语中意为“一切”。潘神被描述为拥有山羊的后半身和腿，以及耳朵和角，这表明他与动物生命有着密切的联系。

A lesser god is Pan, supposed to be a son of Hermes. Pan is a god of fields and woods, a spirit of all nature in fact, and that may be where he gets his name, for it is a Greek word meaning “all.” Pan is described as having the hindquarters and legs plus the ears and horns of a goat, which shows his connection with animal life.

他通常被描绘成一边快乐地跳舞，一边演奏一种由长短不一的空心管或芦苇组成的简单乐器。这种乐器至今仍被称为“排箫”或“潘神的笛子”。潘神获得这件乐器的故事是这样的：他爱上了一位水仙女。她逃离了他，他却紧追不舍。她向众神祈求逃脱，众神将她变成了河边的一根芦苇。潘神悲伤地砍下芦苇，用它们制作了第一支排箫。这位水仙女名叫叙林克斯（Syrinx），在希腊语中意为“笛子”，因此潘神的笛子至今有时也被称为“叙林克斯笛”。

He is usually pictured as dancing happily while he plays on a simple instrument, made up of a series of hollow tubes or reeds of different lengths. Such an instrument is still called a “Pan pipe,” or the “pipes of Pan.” Pan obtained this instrument as follows: He fell in love with a water nymph. She ran from him and he pursued. She prayed for escape to the gods, who turned her into a reed at the riverside. Sadly, Pan cut the reeds and made the first pipes out of them. The name of the nymph was Syrinx, which is a Greek word meaning “pipe,” and the pipes of Pan are still sometimes called a “syrinx.”

可怜的仙女留下的痕迹与另一种音乐形式有关。鸣禽的气管中有一个特殊的区域，专门用于发出鸣声。这个区域也被称为“鸣管”。

The poor nymph leaves her traces in connection with another form of music. The windpipe in song-birds has a special region shaped for the production of their songs. This section is also called the “syrinx.”

那些用来将水从水库中排出或将水吸入水库的管道，就没那么悦耳动听了。这些管道被称为“注射器”，它只是“注射器”（syrinx）的复数形式。

Less musical are those pipes through which water can be forced out of a water reservoir, or up which water can be sucked into a water reservoir. These are called “syringes,” which is only the plural form of “syrinx.”

另一个仙女逃离神灵的例子是达芙妮，她逃离阿波罗，最终化作月桂树才得以逃脱。植物学家将月桂树所属的植物属命名为“达芙妮属”（Daphne），而“达芙妮属”（daphnean）则意为“羞涩的”或“害羞的”。

Another case of a nymph running from a god was that of Daphne, who ran from Apollo and escaped only by being turned into a laurel tree. The genus of plants to which the laurel belongs is called “Daphne” by botanists, and “daphnean” means “bashful” or “shy.”

早期基督徒认为潘神和其他木灵生物尤其不道德，他们开始用潘神的形象来描绘魔鬼。现代人眼中长着角、蹄和尾巴的魔鬼形象，其实就是可怜的潘神被邪恶化身的翻版。

The early Christians thought of Pan and of other wood and spirit beings as particularly immoral, and they began to describe the devil in the shape of Pan. The modern picture of the devil, with his horns, hoofs and tail, is just poor old Pan made wicked.

罗马人也有一位类似的自然神，名叫法乌努斯（Faunus），他们将其与潘神等同起来。法乌努斯尤其掌管动物，他还有一个妹妹叫芙罗拉（Flora），是花卉和植物的女神。如今，这两个名字被用来指代动物和植物，因此生物学家会用“fauna”（动物）和“flora”（植物）来描述特定地区的生态。芙罗拉的名字也被用来命名第八颗被发现的小行星。（“fawn”一词指的是幼鹿，源自盎格鲁-撒克逊语，与法乌努斯无关。）

The Romans had a similar nature god, Faunus, whom they identified with Pan. Faunus was a god of animal life in particular and had a sister named Flora, who was the goddess of flowers and of plant life in general. These names are now applied to animal and plant life, so that biologists will speak of the “fauna” (animal life) and “flora” (plant life) of a particular region. Flora’s name was also given to the eighth planetoid to be discovered. (The word “fawn,” meaning a young deer, is from the Anglo-Saxon and has no connection with Faunus.)

罗马神话中掌管果树的女神是波摩娜（Pomona），因此拉丁语单词“pomum”意为“水果”。由此衍生出“pome”，指像苹果一样多汁的水果；还有“pomegranate”，一种含有籽粒、外形酷似苹果的水果。此外，还有“pomade”，一种以苹果为成分之一的药膏；盛放这种药膏的小盒子则被称为“pomander”。第三十二颗小行星也被命名为波摩娜（Pomona）。

The Roman goddess of fruit trees, in particular, among the plants was Pomona and so the Latin word “pomum” means “fruit.” From that we have “pome” meaning a juicy, fleshy fruit like that of the apple, and “pomegranate,” which is a fruit containing grains and with the outer appearance of an apple. There is also “pomade” which is a kind of ointment that has apples as one of its ingredients, and a small box to carry such an ointment is a “pomander.” The thirty-second planetoid is named Pomona.

例如，伊里斯是一位地位较低的女神，她与赫尔墨斯一样，都是众神的信使。她专门负责将神谕传达给凡人，为此她经常需要从天界降临人间。彩虹便是她降临人间的天然通道，而“iris”在希腊语中正是“彩虹”的意思。

As an example of a minor goddess there is Iris, who, like Hermes, but on a lower level, served as a messenger of the gods. She specialized in bearing messages from the gods to human beings and to do this she had frequently to descend from heaven to earth. The logical stairway by which this might be done was the rainbow and, indeed, “iris” is the Greek word for rainbow.

彩虹最引人注目之处当然在于它缤纷的色彩，因此，“虹膜”一词也被用来指代其他色彩斑斓的物体。例如，众所周知，人眼拥有不同的颜色。1721年，丹麦博物学家雅各布·贝尼格努斯·温斯洛将眼睛的彩色部分称为“虹膜”，这个名称沿用至今。此外，“虹膜”一词也用来指代一种名为“flag”的植物，它开着硕大的花朵，颜色也多种多样。

The most remarkable thing about the rainbow is its many colors, of course, so the word “iris” has come to be applied to other many-colored objects. For instance, as we all know, human eyes come in different shades of color. In 1721, a Danish naturalist, Jakob Benignus Winslow, called the colored part of the eye the “iris” and the name has been kept. It is also applied to the flag, a plant with large flowers that come in a variety of colors.

希腊语中“iris”的复数形式是“irides”，而字母“d”也出现在其他一些英语单词中。例如，像水面上的油膜、肥皂泡、珍珠母等薄膜，都会随着视线角度的变化而呈现出不同的色彩，这种现象被称为“虹彩效应”。

The plural form of “iris” in the Greek is “irides” and the “d” shows up in several other English words. For instance, thin films such as those of oil on water, soap bubbles, mother-of-pearl and so on, show a play of colors that changes as the line of sight is shifted. This is called “iridescence.”

1803年，英国化学家史密森·坦南特发现了一种新元素，这种元素能与其他元素结合形成各种颜色的物质。为了纪念这种元素的丰富色彩，他将这种元素命名为“铱”。

And in 1803, an English chemist named Smithson Tennant discovered a new element which combined with other elements to form substances of various colors. In honor of the many colors, he named the element “iridium.”

另一个更令人愉悦的小女神例子，这次是一群女神，那就是缪斯女神。她们被描绘成九位美丽的少女，是宙斯的女儿。她们的母亲是泰坦女神谟涅摩绪涅。在希腊语中，谟涅摩绪涅的意思是“记忆”，这个含义至今仍然存在，最常见的是体现在“记忆术”（mnemonic）一词中，它指的是任何与记忆相关的事物，特别是那些有助于记忆的事物（例如那句小诗“九月有三十天……”）。

An even more pleasant example of the minor goddess, this time a whole group of them, are the Muses. These were pictured as nine beautiful girls, the daughters of Zeus. The mother was the Titaness Mnemosyne. The name, in Greek, means “memory” and this survives today, most commonly, in the word “mnemonic” meaning anything that pertains to memory and particularly something that aids the memory (such as the little verse “Thirty days hath September…”).

缪斯女神是各种艺术的女神。（在现代，我们或许可以将她们视为“演艺界”的女神。）她们尤其掌管诗歌的灵感，古代乃至近代早期的诗人创作重要诗篇时，总会祈求缪斯女神赐予灵感。由于文字出现之前的诗歌需要背诵，因此，代表诗歌灵感的缪斯女神被视为记忆女神（谟涅摩绪涅）的女儿也就不足为奇了。

The Muses themselves are the goddesses of the various fine arts. (In modern times, we might consider them the goddesses of “show business.”) They were particularly the goddesses of poetic inspiration and poets of ancient times and even of early modern times would always start important poems by calling upon the Muses for inspiration. Since poetry before the days of writing had to be memorized, it is not surprising that the Muses, who represent poetic inspiration, were considered the daughters of memory (Mnemosyne).

此外，由于古代的诗歌、戏剧和其他形式的朗诵总是伴随着优美的旋律，因此将这种优美的旋律称为“音乐”也就不足为奇了。

Furthermore, since poetry, drama and the other forms of recitation in ancient times were always done to the accompaniment of melodious sound, it is not surprising that this melodious sound should be termed “music.”

为纪念缪斯而建造的庙宇致力于学习和研究，这类庙宇被称为“博物馆”。如今，“博物馆”一词的用法略有不同，它指的是用于收藏艺术或科学领域珍贵物品的建筑物。

Temples built in honor of the Muses were dedicated to learning and study and such temples were known as “museums.” The word is used today a little differently. It refers to buildings used for the collection of objects of interest in some art or science.

九位缪斯女神最终都被描绘成掌管着不同的艺术门类。通常的记载中，卡利俄珀是缪斯之首。她的名字源于希腊语，意为“美妙的声音”。她是雄辩和英雄诗歌的缪斯。如今，她的名字被用来指代一种由一系列蒸汽汽笛组成的乐器。我们经常在马戏团游行和旋转木马上听到它的演奏。然而，尽管它活泼欢快，却并非真正意义上的“美妙的声音”。

Each of the nine Muses was eventually pictured as in charge of a different branch of fine art. The usual listing has Calliope as chief of the Muses. Her name comes from Greek words meaning “beautiful voice.” She was the Muse of eloquence and heroic poetry. Her name is now used for an instrument made of a series of steam whistles. We are used to hearing it played in a circus parade and on merry-go-rounds. However, although it is lively and cheerful, it is not exactly a “beautiful voice.”

克利俄（Clio），意为“讲述”，是历史女神。厄拉托（Erato）和乌拉尼亚（Urania）是两位缪斯女神，她们的名字分别是爱神厄洛斯（Eros）和天王星（Uranus）的阴性形式。厄拉托自然是爱情诗的缪斯，而乌拉尼亚是天文学的缪斯，因为“eros”意为“爱”，“uranus”意为“天空”。

Clio, meaning “to tell of,” was the Muse of history. Erato and Urania are two Muses with names that are feminine forms of Eros and Uranus. Erato is naturally the Muse of love poetry and Urania is the Muse of astronomy, since “eros” means “love” and “uranus” means “sky.”

欧忒耳佩（Euterpe）意为“带来愉悦”，是世俗音乐的缪斯；而波吕许谟尼亚（Polyhymnia）则是宗教音乐的缪斯。这位缪斯的名字意为“众多赞美诗”，而“赞美诗”（hymn）一词至今仍用来指代任何宗教题材的歌曲。

Euterpe, meaning “to delight well,” is the Muse of ordinary music, while Polyhymnia is the Muse of religious music. The name of that Muse means “many hymns” and “hymn” is still used, of course, for any song on a religious subject.

塔利亚（Thalia），意为“盛开”，是喜剧的缪斯；而墨尔波墨涅（Melpomene），意为“歌唱”，是悲剧的缪斯。

Thalia, meaning “blooming,” is the Muse of comedy, while Melpomene, meaning “to sing,” is the Muse of tragedy.

最后，特普西科瑞是舞蹈的缪斯。不知何故，这个名字一直沿用至今，人们有时会开玩笑地把舞蹈称为“特普西科瑞式的艺术”。

Finally, Terpsichore is the Muse of the dance. For some reason, this name hangs on and dancing is sometimes spoken of, in a joking sort of way, as the “terpsichorean art.”

早期发现的三颗小行星以不同的缪斯女神命名。第十八颗小行星被命名为墨尔波墨涅（Melpomene）；第二十二颗小行星被命名为卡利俄珀（Calliope）；第二十三颗小行星被命名为塔利亚（Thalia）。这三颗小行星均于1852年被发现。

Three of the early planetoids to be discovered were named after various muses. Planetoid number eighteen was named Melpomene; planetoid number twenty-two was named Calliope, and planetoid number twenty-three was named Thalia. All three planetoids were discovered in 1852.

另一组令人愉悦的姐妹是三位在拉丁语中被称为“Gratiae”的女神。她们是女性所有美好特质的女神，当然，拉丁语名称的英文译名是“三美神”（the three Graces）。我们都熟悉“优雅的”（graceful）和“亲切的”（gracious）这样的词。

Another pleasant group of sisters are three who are known as “Gratiae” in Latin. They are the goddesses of all that is charming in women and, of course, the English version of the Latin name is “the three Graces.” We are all familiar with words like “graceful” and “gracious.”

行星是许多次要女神进入现代语言的一种途径。我仅举几例为例。

The planetoids serve as one way in which many minor goddesses enter the modern language. I will list just a few by way of example.

第五颗小行星阿斯特赖亚（Astraea）代表正义女神，尽管她的名字在希腊语中意为“星星”。她被认为是黄金时代末期最后一位离开地球的女神。希腊人将她与黄道十二宫第六星座——室女座（Virgo）——联系起来，室女座意为“少女”。她通常被描绘成一位手持麦穗的带翼少女。麦穗上有一颗名为“角宿一”（Spica）的明亮恒星，在拉丁语中意为“麦穗”。事实上，这个星座最初是一个农业符号。当它在日落时分首次出现时，意味着播种谷物的季节即将到来。

Planetoid number five, Astraea represents the goddess of justice, though her name is the Greek word for “stars.” She was pictured as the last goddess to leave the earth at the end of the Golden Age. The Greeks visualized her as the sixth of the constellations of the zodiac, Virgo meaning “the maiden.” She is usually pictured as a winged girl with a sheaf of wheat in her hand. The wheat is marked by a bright star called “Spica” which is the Latin word for a spike or ear of grain. Actually, the constellation was originally an agricultural sign. When it first appears at sunset, it means the time of planting of grain is near.

第六颗小行星是赫柏。这个词的意思是“青春”，赫柏是青春女神，也是众神的斟酒女神，直到木卫三出现接替了她的工作。

Planetoid number six is Hebe. The word means “youth” and Hebe is the goddess of youth and the cupbearer of the gods, until Ganymede arrived to take over the job.

第十颗小行星：许癸厄亚（Hygeia），阿斯克勒庇俄斯的女儿，健康女神，她的名字意为“健康”。我们今天用“hygiene”（卫生）一词来指研究保持健康的方法。阿斯克勒庇俄斯的另一个女儿是帕那刻亚（Panacea），在希腊语中意为“万灵药”。这个词至今仍被用来指代任何据说能轻松治愈多种疑难杂症的灵丹妙药。

Planetoid number ten: Hygeia, the daughter of Asclepius and the goddess of health, her name meaning “health.” We use the word “hygiene” today to mean the study of methods for preserving health.
