Another daughter of Asclepius was Panacea a word meaning “all-cure” in Greek. The word is still used today for anything supposed to be an easy cure for many difficult problems.

罗马的健康女神萨卢斯（Salus），罗马人将其与许癸厄亚（Hygeia）等同起来。因此，“salutary”（有益健康的）一词意为“健康的”。此外，当我们问候他人时，通常会祝愿对方身体健康，或者通过询问对方的近况来表达我们的祝福。我们通常会说：“你好吗？”。因此，问候语可以是“salutation”或“salute”。

The Roman goddess of health, whom the Romans identified with Hygeia, was Salus. For this reason, the word “salutary” means “healthful.” Furthermore, when we greet someone, we usually wish him or her good health or imply that we do by asking after their wellbeing. “How are you?” we usually say. Therefore, a greeting is a “salutation” or a “salute.”

还应该提及一位罗马次要神祇，因为他与其中一个月份的名称有关，而这些月份的名称都源于拉丁语。

A minor Roman god should also be mentioned in connection with the name of one of the months, which are all Latin in origin.

我之前已经提到了三月（火星）、五月（玛雅）和六月（朱诺）。但还有一个月份与雅努斯有关，他原本是罗马的门神。因此，他成为了掌管开始（通过门进入）和结束（通过门离开）的神。他通常被描绘成双面神，一张脸向前看，一张脸向后看。

I have already mentioned March (Mars), May (Maia) and June (Juno). But then there is a month involving Janus who was the Roman god of doors, to begin with. He therefore became the god who presided over beginnings (entrance through a door) and endings (exit by a door). He was usually pictured with two faces, one looking forward and one backward.

一年中的第一个月是开启新一年、告别旧年的时刻。我们仍然庆祝这个月，因为它既是对过去的感伤，也是对未来的美好期盼。因此，它以罗马神话中的雅努斯命名，被称为“一月”。当然，负责建筑物门窗及其他区域的人，也被称为“清洁工”。

The first month of the year is a time for beginning a new year and ending the old. We still celebrate it as a time for looking sentimentally at the past and hopefully toward the future. So it is named for Janus and called “January.” And, of course, a man who is in charge of the doors of a building and of other parts of it, as well, is a “janitor.”

其余月份的名称并非源于神话传说。

The names of the remaining months are not mythical in origin.

事实上，罗马人有很多次要的神话人物，每个人物都掌管着生活的某个方面。他们有一位女神掌管婴儿迈出的第一步，另一位女神掌管婴儿长出的第一颗牙齿，等等。他们还有专门掌管特定地点甚至特定人物的神灵或精灵。罗马人称这种个人精灵为“守护神”（genius）。

The Romans, in fact, had any number of minor mythical characters, each governing some single part of life. They had a goddess who was in charge of a baby’s first step, another in charge of its first tooth and so on. They had special gods or spirits in charge of a particular place and even of each particular person. The Romans called this personal spirit a genius.

凡是使一个人与众不同、独具特色的特质，都被称为他的天赋。当一个人在智力、才华或创造力方面远超常人时，他的天赋似乎就拥有了特殊的力量，因为凡人若非借助某种超自然的力量，是不可能达到如此卓越的。他与其说是凡人，不如说是指引方向的神灵。事实上，他就是一位“天才”。

Whatever made a person individual, and different from other persons, was referred to as his genius. When a particular person was far above other people in intelligence, brilliance or creative ability, his genius seemed in special charge because a mere man could not be that good without a little supernatural help. He was more the guiding spirit itself than a man. In fact, he was a “genius.”

希腊语中用来指代附身并引导人的灵体的词是daimon。由于早期基督徒认为任何附身于人的灵体都是邪恶的，因此这个词（在英语中是“demon”）逐渐演变为指代魔鬼。

The Greek word for the spirit possessing and guiding the individual was daimon. Since to the early Christians any kind of spirit possessing a human being was evil, the word, which in English is “demon,” came to mean a kind of devil.

富有想象力的希腊人并不局限于神祇和半神，他们还创造了各种各样的怪物。当然，有些怪物之所以可怕，主要是因为它们体型庞大。我之前已经提到过巨人族、独眼巨人以及泰坦巨人。

The imaginative Greeks did not confine themselves to gods and demigods. They also dreamed up a variety of monsters of all types. Some, of course, were monstrous mainly because of their size. I have already mentioned the Gigantes, Cyclops and Titans.

宙斯击败泰坦巨人并确立自己宇宙统治者的地位后，偶尔会发生叛乱，但都以失败告终。（这或许反映了被征服的前希腊人反抗希腊入侵者的起义。）

After Zeus had defeated the Titans and established himself as ruler of the universe there were occasional revolts, all unsuccessful. (This may be a reflection of revolts of the conquered pre-Greeks against the Greek invaders.)

巨人起义的故事被神话创造者们用来展现极端的一面。例如，雅典娜用一块巨大的岩石压死了巨人恩克拉多斯，这块岩石最终沉入地中海，形成了西西里岛。神话创造者们还让奥林匹斯诸神运用各自的特长赢得这场战斗。例如，赫菲斯托斯用一勺熔化的金属浇在巨人弥玛斯身上，杀死了他。（据说，雅典娜也是在这场战斗中杀死了巨人帕拉斯，并取了他的名字。）

The story of the revolt of the Gigantes (giants) was used by the mythmakers as a chance to go to extremes. For instance, Athena killed the giant Enceladus by crushing him under a rock so large that it lay there in the Mediterranean as the island of Sicily. The mythmakers also had the various Olympians use their specialties in winning the battle. For instance, Hephaestus managed to kill the giant Mimas by pouring a ladle of molten metal over him. (It was in this same battle that Athena was supposed to have killed the giant Pallas, and taken his name.)

那场叛乱的现代痕迹就隐藏在土星的卫星之中。1789年，发现天王星的赫歇尔也发现了土星的第一颗和第二颗卫星。这两颗卫星是唯一没有以泰坦神命名的。赫歇尔将它们命名为“弥玛斯”（Mimas）和“恩克拉多斯”（Enceladus），以便让那些曾与奥林匹斯诸神交战的巨人与同样反抗的泰坦神们汇合。

The modern trace of that revolt lies among Saturn’s satellites. In 1789 Herschel, the discoverer of Uranus, likewise discovered satellites one and two of Saturn. These were the only ones not named after Titans. Instead, Herschel named them “Mimas” and “Enceladus” so that the giants who warred against the Olympians might join the Titans who did the same.

之后，两位名叫奥图斯和埃菲阿尔忒斯的巨人袭击了奥林匹斯山。为了达到目的，他们将附近的佩利翁山堆在了奥萨山上，试图建造一座足以俯瞰奥林匹斯山的巍峨山峰。他们最终被击败，但从此以后，“将佩利翁山堆在奥萨山上”这句话就被用来形容任何极其艰巨的任务。

After that, a pair of giants named Otus and Ephialtes attacked Olympus. In doing so, they piled the nearby mountain of Pelion upon the mountain of Ossa in order to get a combined mountain high enough to overlook Olympus. They were eventually destroyed, but ever since, the phrase “to pile Pelion upon Ossa” has been used to describe any superhumanly difficult task.

接下来爆发了最危险的叛乱。大地女神盖亚因她的孩子们——巨人族——被毁灭而愤怒，于是创造了有史以来最可怕的怪物，也是体型最大的巨人。他名叫提丰。他身形庞大，身高数百英里，四肢末端都是蛇。

The most dangerous revolt came next. Gaea, the goddess of the earth, annoyed at the destruction of her children, the Gigantes, brought forth the most terrible monster of all, and the largest giant who ever existed. He was named Typhon. He was hundreds of miles high and wide and his arms and legs ended in serpents.

曾有一段时间，奥林匹斯众神都对他感到恐惧。例如，阿芙洛狄忒和厄洛斯曾在一条小溪边与堤丰相遇。他们惊恐万分，跳入河中化作鱼儿逃走。之后，他们将两条鱼的形象定格在天上，作为黄道十二宫的第十二个星座——双鱼座，意为“鱼”。

For a while, the Olympians were completely terrified of him. For instance, Aphrodite and Eros once met up with Typhon on the banks of a stream. In fright, they jumped into the river and changed themselves into fish in order to escape. Having done so, they placed the image of the two fish in the heavens, as the twelfth sign of the zodiac, Pisces meaning “fish.”

宙斯最终被激怒，与提丰决斗。起初，宙斯似乎真的要被打败甚至毁灭，但经过一场惨烈的战斗，宙斯最终凭借雷霆之力取得了胜利。

Zeus was finally goaded into fighting Typhon. For a while it looked as though Zeus might actually be beaten and destroyed, but after a terrible fight, Zeus managed to use his thunderbolts and win.

“台风”一词源于这个神话（由于这场大战的激动人心而广为人知），中世纪的阿拉伯人将其带到东南亚，现在它以“台风”的形式被用作飓风的词语。

The word Typhon was picked up from this myth (which became rather famous because of the excitement of the great fight) by the Arabs of the Middle Ages, who carried it to southeast Asia where it is now used in the form of “typhoon” as a word for the hurricane.

有一次，奥林匹斯众神反抗宙斯，他们本可能获胜，但这一次，一个可怕的巨人加入了宙斯的阵营。这个巨人名叫百臂巨人布里阿瑞俄斯，他是三个被描绘成拥有百臂的巨人之一。因此，“百臂巨人”这个形容词有时用来形容复杂棘手的问题。

On one occasion, the Olympians themselves revolted against Zeus and might have won except that this time a monstrous giant came to Zeus’s side. This was Briareus, who was one of three giants pictured as having a hundred arms. The adjective “briarean” is for that reason sometimes used to picture a problem with numerous complications.

并非所有巨人都参与了反抗诸神的叛乱。其中一位巨人，俄里翁，主要是一名猎人。他就是我在前一章中提到的追逐昴星团的巨人。

Not all giants were involved in revolts against the gods. One giant, Orion, was mainly a hunter. He was the giant whom I mentioned in an earlier chapter as chasing the Pleiades.

最终，俄里翁触怒了阿波罗，阿波罗派了一只蝎子去杀他。蝎子没能杀死他，但阿波罗设计让阿尔忒弥斯完成了这项任务。阿尔忒弥斯（她深爱着俄里翁）发现自己杀死了他后，悲痛欲绝，恳求阿斯克勒庇俄斯让他复活。阿斯克勒庇俄斯照做了，正如我之前所说，哈迪斯因此抱怨，于是宙斯用雷霆击杀了俄里翁和阿斯克勒庇俄斯。

Eventually, Orion offended Apollo, who sent a scorpion to kill him. The scorpion failed but Apollo tricked Artemis into completing the job. Artemis (who loved Orion), on finding she had killed him, in grief begged Asclepius to bring him back to life. This Asclepius did and that was when Hades complained, as I said earlier, so that Zeus killed both Orion and Asclepius with thunderbolts.

正如我之前所说，阿斯克勒庇俄斯不仅被定位为星座，猎户座和天蝎座也被定位为星座。天蝎座后来成为黄道带的第八个星座——天蝎座。我之前提到的那颗明亮的恒星——心宿二，就位于这个星座中。

Not only was Asclepius placed in the sky as a constellation (as I said earlier), but so were Orion and the scorpion. The scorpion became the eighth sign of the zodiac, Scorpio. It is in this constellation that the bright star, Antares, which I have already mentioned, is to be found.

猎户座位于天空中，与金牛座相邻，与天蝎座相对（仿佛将它们拉得更近只会重新引发争斗）。

The constellation, Orion, is to be found in the heavens neighboring Taurus and on the side opposite Scorpio (as though bringing them any closer together would only renew the fight).

在猎户座附近，金牛座的另一侧，坐落着天空中最亮的恒星——天狼星。希腊人解释说，猎户座有一只名叫天狼星的猎犬，因此他们将天狼星纳入了一个名为“大犬座”（拉丁语为Canis Major，意为“大狗”）的星座中。

Near Orion, on the other side of Taurus, is the brightest star in the heavens, Sirius. The Greeks explained its existence by saying that Orion had a hunting dog named Sirius and they made Sirius part of a constellation called, in Latin, Canis Major (“Great Dog”).

事实上，这种解释很可能是后人编造的。天狼星在仲夏时节与太阳一同升起，古人认为正是天狼星的光芒与太阳的光芒叠加，才导致了仲夏的酷热。当然，事实并非如此，但人们仍然将盛夏最热的时期称为“三伏天”。天狼星（Sirius）一词可能也源于希腊语，意为“灼热的”，原因也正是如此。

Actually, this explanation was probably made up later. Sirius rises with the sun in midsummer and the ancients felt that its brightness added to that of the sun was what caused midsummer heat. This is not so, of course, but the hottest part of summer is still referred to as the “dog days.” Sirius probably comes from a Greek word meaning “scorching” for this same reason.

猎户座附近另一颗明亮的恒星是南河三（Procyon），它的名字源于希腊语，意为“在狗之前”，因为它比天狼星升起得稍早一些。希腊人将南河三划归到另一个星座，拉丁语称之为小犬座（Canis Minor，意为“小狗”），这样猎户座就有了两只“狗”来帮助他对抗公牛。

Another bright star near Orion is Procyon, a name that comes from Greek words meaning “before the dog” because it rises a little earlier than Sirius does. The Greeks made Procyon part of another constellation called, in Latin, Canis Minor (“Little Dog”) so that Orion has two dogs to help him face the bull.

希腊人擅长描绘半人半兽（或更常见的是半人半女）的怪物。例如，哈耳庇厄的形象就是长着女人的头和鸟的身体。最初，它们可能是风神，会夺走垂死之人的灵魂（“哈耳庇厄”一词源于希腊语，意为“夺取”）。

The Greeks specialized in monsters that were part animal and part man (or, more usually, woman). For instance, the Harpies are pictured as having women’s heads on birds’ bodies. Originally, they may have been wind spirits which snatched away the souls of dying men (the word “harpy” comes from a Greek word meaning “to snatch”).

然而，最终，她们被描述为极其肮脏、散发恶臭，而且贪婪无比。她们想要的不是灵魂，而是食物。她们会突然袭击正在享用盛宴的男人们，抢走食物，并将没抢到的东西弄得脏兮兮的。这个词至今仍被用来形容极其贪婪的女人。

Eventually, however, they were described as very filthy and foul-smelling, and greedy, too. It was not souls they wanted, but food. They would swoop down on feasting men, snatching up the food and dirtying what they could not snatch. The word is still used today to describe very greedy women.

还有一种比这更可怕的“鸟女”是戈耳工，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“可怕的”。她们和哈耳庇厄一样，拥有鸟的翅膀和利爪，但最可怕的是，她们的头发是由活生生的、扭动的蛇组成。她们的容貌极其恐怖，任何看到她们的人都会变成石头。如今，丑陋而可怕的女人仍然被称为“戈耳工”。

An even more terrible type of “bird-women” were the Gorgons, a name coming from a Greek word meaning “terrible.” Like the Harpies, they had the wings and claws of birds, but, worst of all, their hair was made up of living, writhing snakes. They were so horrible to look at that anyone seeing them was turned to stone. An ugly and terrifying woman is still called a “gorgon” today.

传说中存在的三位戈耳工中，最年轻也最可怕的一位，名叫美杜莎。她的名字也出现在动物学中，因为水母有着长长的触手，这些触手会扭动着寻找食物，看起来就像附着在身体上的活蛇。因此，这类水母就被称为“美杜莎”。

The best known of the three Gorgons which were supposed to exist was the youngest and most horrible. Her name was Medusa. She has found her way into zoology, for a jellyfish has long tentacles that writhe about in search of food, and these look like living snakes attached to a body. For that reason, such jellyfish are referred to as “medusas.”

戈耳工的姐妹之一是针鼹。她上半身是美丽的女子，下半身则是可怕的蛇。一种奇特的澳大利亚动物也被赋予了相同的名字，通常被称为“刺猬食蚁兽”。它身上有毛，所以是哺乳动物；但另一方面，它又像爬行动物一样产卵。因为它看起来像是半人半兽的生物，一半是哺乳动物，一半是爬行动物（针鼹也是如此，因为女人的一半是哺乳动物，蛇的一半是爬行动物），所以刺猬食蚁兽被命名为“针鼹”。

A sister of the Gorgons was Echidna. She was a beautiful woman down to the waist and a horrible serpent below that. The same name has been given to a queer Australian animal, usually called the “spiny anteater.” It has hair so it is a mammal, but on the other hand it lays eggs like a reptile. Because it seems a half-and-half creature, half mammal and half reptile (and so is Echidna since the woman half is a mammal and the serpent half a reptile) the spiny anteater was named the “echidna.”

厄喀德娜是希腊神话中其余大部分怪物的母亲，它们的父亲是堤丰。

Echidna was the mother of most of the remaining monsters of Greek mythology, the father being Typhon.

厄喀德娜的孩子中有一只名叫许德拉的水怪，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“水”。它有着蛇身九头。每当一个头被砍掉，就会长出两个新的头来。因此，任何一种邪恶的境况，如果每次试图治愈它都只会变得更糟，就被称为“九头蛇般的”。

Among the children of Echidna was a water monster named Hydra from the Greek word for “water.” It had a serpent’s body with nine heads. Each time one head was cut off, two others grew in its place. For that reason, any set of evil conditions which simply gets worse every time an effort is made to cure it, is said to be “hydra-headed.”

长蛇座之所以被放置在天空中，是因为有一条长长的、蜿蜒的星带，环绕黄道带南缘四分之一处，就叫长蛇座。它是天空中最大的星座，但除此之外，它并没有什么特别之处。

The Hydra has been placed in the heavens, for there is a long snaky string of stars stretching a quarter way around the southern rim of the zodiac which is called by that name. It is the largest constellation in the heavens but otherwise there is nothing particularly remarkable about it.

有一种无害的小动物，与水母有亲缘关系，体长只有半英寸左右，长着大约六条细小的触手，用来捕捉微小的食物颗粒。它看起来像一个体型很小的多头水生生物，被称为“淡水水螅”。

There is a harmless little animal, related to the jellyfish, and only half an inch long or so, which has a set of about half a dozen tiny tentacles with which it catches tiny bits of food. It looks like a very small many-headed creature of the water, and it is named the “fresh-water hydra.”

厄喀德娜的另一个孩子是斯芬克斯，它有着女人的头和狮子的身体。它会拦住过路的旅人，提出谜语。如果旅人解不开谜语，斯芬克斯就会杀死他。因此，任何难以理解或喜欢说谜语的人都被称为“斯芬克斯”。

Another child of Echidna was the Sphinx, a creature with a woman’s head and a lion’s body. This would stop travelers and propose riddles. If the traveler could not solve the riddle, the Sphinx would kill him. Any person, therefore, who is hard to understand, or who speaks in riddles is called a “sphinx.”

由于一个人不说话时最难理解，因此“斯芬克斯”一词特指那些很少说话的人。这与它的希腊语词源相符，该词源于一个意为“紧紧束缚”的词。能够紧紧闭合开口的环状肌肉，例如用来抿嘴的肌肉，就叫做“括约肌”。

Since a person is sometimes hardest to understand when he doesn’t speak at all, “sphinx” has come to be applied particularly to people who speak very little. This fits in with the Greek derivation, which is from a word meaning “to bind tight.” A ring of muscle which can close an opening tightly, such as the muscle which is used to purse the lips, is a “sphincter.”

埃及人有时会建造狮身人面像，将国王的头颅安在狮子的身体上。希腊人也称这些雕像为斯芬克斯。其中最著名的当属“大斯芬克斯”，它长172英尺，高66英尺。大多数人听到“斯芬克斯”这个词时，脑海中浮现的往往就是这座雕像。

The Egyptians occasionally built statues with the heads of their kings on lion’s bodies. The Greeks called these sphinxes, too. In particular, there is the “Great Sphinx,” which is 172 feet long and 66 feet high. This is what most people think of when they hear the word “sphinx.”

厄喀德娜的另一个怪兽孩子是奇美拉，它并非简单的“一半一半”，而是“三分之一三分之一三分之一”。它通常被描绘成一个喷火的怪物，长着狮头、山羊身（有时背部还长着一个山羊头），尾巴则是龙或蛇。这种怪物比我们通常想象中的怪物要怪诞得多，以至于“奇美拉”一词如今已进入我们的语言，用来指代任何虚构的生物，或者更确切地说，任何荒谬的想法。

Another monstrous child of Echidna is the Chimaera, which is not merely half-and-half, but third-and-third-and-third. It is usually pictured as a fire-breathing monster with a lion’s head, a goat’s body (sometimes with a goat’s head arising from the back) and a dragon or serpent as the tail. This is so much wilder than the usual run of imaginary monsters that the word “chimaera” has come into our language to mean any imaginary creature or, in fact, any ridiculous idea.

这个词也可以指现实生活中的事物。植物学家可以将一种植物的一部分嫁接到另一种植物上，动物学家有时也会对一些简单的动物进行同样的嫁接。这种组合被称为“嵌合体”。此外，还有一种形状奇特的鲨鱼也被称为“嵌合体”。

The word can refer to real things, too, however. Botanists can graft a part of one plant onto a second, and zoologists can sometimes do the same thing with simple animals. Such combinations are called “chimaeras.” There is also an oddly shaped shark called a “chimaera.”

厄喀德娜也是我之前提到的地狱犬刻耳柏洛斯和稍后我会提到的尼米亚狮子的母亲。

Echidna is also the mother of Cerberus whom I have mentioned before and of the Nemean Lion which I will mention later.

另一种更直接的怪物，就是一条巨蛇，体型巨大，令人胆寒，名叫皮同（Python）。阿波罗用箭射杀了它，因此有时也被称为“阿波罗·皮提乌斯”（就像雅典娜后来被称为帕拉斯·雅典娜一样）。在杀死皮同的地方，阿波罗建立了德尔斐神谕所。

A more straightforward monster, a serpent and no more, but one of gigantic and horrible size, was called Python. Apollo killed it with his arrows and was therefore sometimes called “Apollo Pythius” (just as Athena became Pallas Athena). At the spot where the slaying took place, Apollo established the oracle of Delphi.

事实上，皮提亚似乎是德尔斐的早期名称，而阿波罗与皮同的神话可能仅仅意味着希腊神阿波罗驱逐了希腊人之前的居民的一位女神。不过，这位早期女神的痕迹并未完全消失。负责发布神谕的女祭司仍然被称为“皮同”，而每四年在德尔斐举行的体育赛事（其重要性仅次于奥林匹克运动会）则被称为“皮提亚运动会”。

Actually, Pytho seems to have been the earlier name of Delphi and the myth of Apollo and Python may just signify that the Greek god Apollo had ousted an earlier goddess of the pre-Greek inhabitants. Not all traces of the earlier goddess were removed. The priestess who gave out the oracles was still called the “pythoness” and the athletic games celebrated at Delphi every four years (which were next to the Olympian games in importance) were called the “Pythian games.”

现代动物学也反映了这种神话，因为我们把现存最大的蛇类，即蟒蛇和水蟒，称为“蟒蛇”。

In modern times zoology mirrors the myth, for we call the very largest living snakes, the boas and anacondas, “pythons.”

相比之下，半人马则没那么可怕，它们被描绘成半人半马的形象。这种生物的概念或许源于没有马匹的人们第一次遇到骑马的劫掠队伍。由于从未见过人骑马，他们很可能在恐惧中认定马和人是同一生物。

Less horrible are the Centaurs, which are pictured as half man and half horse. The notion of such creatures arose, possibly, when people without horses first met up with a raiding party of mounted men. Never having seen men riding horses before, they could easily decide, in their terror, that horse and man were a single creature.

半人马通常被描绘成野蛮粗鲁、野蛮无礼的生物，使用弓箭作战。然而，其中一位名叫喀戎的半人马却是个例外。他高贵、温和、智慧，曾教导许多希腊英雄医术，甚至包括阿斯克勒庇俄斯。

The Centaurs are usually pictured as wild, uncouth creatures, rude and uncivilized, fighting with bow and arrows. One of them, however, Chiron, was an exception. He was noble, gentle and wise, and taught medicine to many of the Greek heroes, even to Asclepius.

凯龙星死后，被归入星座，成为黄道十二宫的第九宫——射手座，这个星座名称源自拉丁语，意为“弓箭手”。该星座通常被描绘成一个半人马，正在拉弓射箭。

After his death, Chiron was placed among the constellations as the ninth sign of the zodiac, Sagittarius, from the Latin word for “archer.” The constellation is often pictured as a centaur, drawing a bow in the act of shooting an arrow.

古希腊人和其他古代民族只能在他们自然可见的天空范围内描绘星座。南极高空的星辰，从埃及、巴比伦和希腊等地是看不到的。直到15世纪欧洲人开始沿着非洲海岸向南航行，北半球的人们才得以看到这些星辰。

Now the Greeks and other ancient people only formed constellations in that part of the sky which they could see, naturally. The stars which are high in the sky at the South Pole are never visible from such lands as Egypt, Babylonia and Greece. It was only when Europeans started sailing southward along the coast of Africa in the 1400’s that these stars were seen by northern eyes.

最终，天文学家将这些新发现的恒星划分成星座。其中一些星座被描绘成现代物体的形象，例如显微镜座（Microscopium）和望远镜座（Telescopium）。而另一些星座则以神话传说中的名字命名。其中一个位于南方的大型星座被命名为半人马座（Centaurus）。

Eventually, astronomers divided these newer stars into constellations. Some of these were drawn up into imaginary pictures of rather modern objects such as Microscopium (“the microscope”) and Telescopium (“the telescope”). Others, however, were given names in the mythological tradition. One large southern constellation was named Centaurus.

希腊神话中的一些怪物远非可怕，反而颇具魅力。例如，塞壬女妖通常被描绘成绝世佳人，她们栖息在海边的岩石上，唱着天籁之音。过往的水手会被她们的歌声吸引，驶近船只，最终却难逃触礁身亡的命运。

Far from being horrible, some of the Greek monsters were quite attractive. For instance there were the Sirens, who were usually pictured as very beautiful young women, who rested on the rocks of a seashore and sang in heavenly voices. Sailors passing near would be attracted and would steer their boats nearer, only to meet death upon the rocks.

这大概是希腊人用来象征危险海岸线的一种方式，这些海岸线表面平静无害，似乎在引诱水手和他们的船只，但实际上却用隐藏在水下的岩石摧毁它们。

This is probably the Greek way of symbolizing dangerous stretches of seashore which seem to invite sailors and their ships with their peaceful and harmless appearance only to destroy them with rocks hidden below the water.

在现代语言中，那些喜欢卖弄风情、让爱上她们的男人痛苦不堪的女人被称为“塞壬”。任何用虚假的承诺引诱男人偏离正道的事物，都被称为“塞壬之歌”。“塞壬”有时指甜美的歌者，但更多时候指的是一种发出尖锐刺耳、音调忽高忽低的警报声的装置。这种装置被用作警车和消防车上的警示信号，但它绝非神话中塞壬女妖那动人的歌声。

In modern language, a flirtatious woman who delights in making those men who fall in love with her miserable is called a “siren.” Anything which attracts men away from their duty with deceiving promise, is called a “siren song.” A “siren” sometimes refers to a sweet singer, but more often it refers to a device which sends out a thin, piercing scream that rises and falls in pitch. It is used as a warning signal attached to police cars and fire engines, but it is certainly not the beautiful song of the mythological sirens.

塞壬最初可能和哈耳庇厄一样，是带走亡灵灵魂的风之精灵。有时，塞壬和哈耳庇厄一样，被描绘成拥有鸟身的形象。然而，由于塞壬始终与海洋联系在一起，她们最终被描绘成上半身是女性、下半身是鱼的生物。换句话说，她们变成了“美人鱼”。

The Sirens may originally have been wind spirits carrying off the souls of the dead, as the Harpies originally were. Sometimes the Sirens, like the Harpies, were pictured with birds’ bodies. However, since the Sirens were always associated with the sea, they eventually began to be pictured as creatures who were women down to the waist and fish below that. In other words, they became “mermaids.”

正因如此，塞壬以一种颇为奇特的方式进入了动物学领域。有一种名为海牛的海洋哺乳动物，它们习惯将头和肩膀露出水面，用一只手臂像母亲抱着婴儿一样紧紧地抱着幼崽。它们的后半身末端长着鳍状肢。水手们远远地看到它们，先是像人一样抱着幼崽，然后潜入水中露出鳍状肢，便误以为自己看到了美人鱼。

Because of this, the Sirens entered zoology in a rather queer way. There are certain sea mammals called sea cows who have a habit of sticking their heads and shoulders above water and holding their young clasped to them with one arm just as a mother might hold a baby. Their hindquarters end in a flipper. Sailors seeing them at a distance, first with their babies in a human pose, then with the flipper exposed as they dived, thought they saw a mermaid.

由于这种荒谬的错误，海牛所属的动物群体（实际上非​​常丑陋，一点也不像塞壬）被称为“塞壬”。

Because of this grotesque error, the group of animals of which the sea cow is a member (which are actually very ugly and not the least like Sirens) are called “Sirenia.”

不过，虽然这些生物不如最初的塞壬那样美丽，但它们也远没有塞壬那么危险。事实上，它们完全无害，水手遇到塞壬人比遇到塞壬要幸运得多，尽管乍一看似乎并非如此。

Still, if these creatures are not as beautiful as the original Sirens, neither are they as dangerous. They are, in fact, entirely harmless, and a sailor would be far luckier to meet a sirenian than a Siren, however things might seem just at first.

六个男人的故事

6 TALES OF MEN

希腊神话中关于人类起源的描述并不多。最接近这一主题的记载是关于泰坦神普罗米修斯的。他的名字源于希腊语，意为“预见”，他还有一个兄弟厄庇墨透斯，名字的意思是“后见”。换句话说，普罗米修斯智慧过人，能够预见行动的后果；而厄庇墨透斯则愚钝无知，只能在事情发生之后才明白结果。

THE GREEKS DID NOT DO a great deal in their myths as far as the creation of man was concerned. The nearest thing to such an account involves the Titan, Prometheus. His name comes from Greek words meaning “forethought,” and he had a brother, Epimetheus whose name meant “hindthought.” In other words, Prometheus was wise, seeing the results of actions in advance, while Epimetheus was foolish, understanding results only after they had come about.

他们都是泰坦神伊阿珀托斯的儿子，因此也是阿特拉斯的兄弟。然而，当泰坦神族与奥林匹斯诸神交战，阿特拉斯率领泰坦神族作战时，普罗米修斯预见到奥林匹斯诸神将会获胜，便抛弃了泰坦神族，并说服厄庇墨透斯也这样做。结果，这两兄弟免于遭受泰坦神族普遍遭受的惩罚。

Both were the sons of the Titan Iapetus, and therefore brothers of Atlas. However, when the Titans and Olympians were at war, with Atlas leading the Titans, Prometheus, foreseeing that the Olympians would win, abandoned the Titans and persuaded Epimetheus to do likewise. As a result these two brothers were spared the punishment that fell on the Titans generally.

与泰坦的战争结束后，普罗米修斯奉宙斯之命创造了人类。或者，正如一些版本所说，普通的凡人是普罗米修斯和厄庇墨透斯的后裔。

After the war with the Titans was over, men were created by Prometheus at the order of Zeus. Or, as some versions have it, ordinary mortal human beings were the descendants of Prometheus and Epimetheus.

无论如何，宙斯并不完全信任普罗米修斯，他厌恶人类；而普罗米修斯则竭尽全力帮助人类对抗奥林匹斯诸神。当宙斯试图用洪水毁灭人类时，普罗米修斯设法警告了一个人——丢卡利翁，根据一些神话传说，丢卡利翁据说是普罗米修斯的儿子。丢卡利翁建造了一艘船，他和妻子皮拉（据说是厄庇墨透斯的女儿）乘船逃生。

In either case, Zeus, who did not entirely trust Prometheus, disliked men, while Prometheus, on his part, did his best to help men against the Olympians. When Zeus tried to destroy mankind with a huge flood, Prometheus managed to warn one man, Deucalion, supposedly the son of Prometheus, according to some of the mythmakers. Deucalion built a ship in which he and his wife, Pyrrha, supposedly a daughter of Epimetheus, escaped.

人类从那场灾难中艰难复苏之时，却被奥林匹斯诸神所忽视，只能过着悲惨而野蛮的生活。普罗米修斯怜悯他所创造或孕育的人类，教给他们各种技艺和科学，以期改善他们的生活。不仅如此，他还从太阳那里盗取了火种，并教会人类如何使用它。（这或许表明他是希腊人之前的火神，在希腊人入侵后被赫菲斯托斯取代。）

As mankind struggled back from that catastrophe, they were neglected by the Olympians and allowed to live lives of misery and savagery. Prometheus took pity on the men he had created, or fathered, and taught them various arts and sciences that might help them to an easier life. To top things off, he stole fire from the sun and taught mankind how to use it. (This may indicate that he was the pre-Greek god of fire, replaced by Hephaestus after the Greek invasion.)

这一切都是在违抗宙斯的命令，因此任何非常大胆、反抗、或者非常原创、富有创造力的行为都被称为“普罗米修斯式的”。

All this was in defiance of Zeus, so that any act which is very daring and defiant, or very original and creative, is called “promethean.”

为了报复，宙斯创造了一位绝世佳人，众神都赐予她美貌、优雅、智慧、歌喉等等天赋。她被命名为潘多拉，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“天赋异禀”。之后，她被赐予厄庇墨透斯为妻。尽管普罗米修斯曾警告厄庇墨透斯不要接受宙斯的任何馈赠，但厄庇墨透斯还是被她的美貌所倾倒，接受了她。

In revenge, Zeus created a beautiful woman to whom all the gods gave gifts of beauty, grace, wit, melody and so on. She was called Pandora from Greek words meaning “all-gifted.” She was then given to Epimetheus for a wife. Because of her beauty, Epimetheus accepted her although Prometheus had warned him against taking any gift from Zeus.

与潘多拉一同得到的还有埃庇米修斯，潘多拉被禁止打开这个盒子。然而，她是一位真正的女子。她一有机会就打开了盒子，想看看里面装的是什么，结果从中飞出了衰老、死亡、饥荒、疾病、悲伤以及所有折磨人类的苦难之灵。只有希望留在盒子的底部，当它最终被释放出来时，便成了人类在苦难重压下苟活下去的唯一希望。

Along with Pandora, Epimetheus received a box which Pandora was forbidden to open. However, she was a true woman. At her first opportunity, she opened the box to see what was in it and out flew the spirits of old age, death, famine, sickness, grief, and all the other ills that plague human beings. Only hope was left at the bottom of the box and, when finally let out, was all that kept human beings alive under the weight of their misery.

因此，任何不受干扰时无害，但受到干扰时会释放出许多麻烦的事物，都被称为“潘多拉魔盒”。

Thus, anything which is harmless when undisturbed, but which lets loose many troubles when interfered with, is called a “Pandora’s box.”

宙斯不仅报复了人类，还直接惩罚了普罗米修斯。他将普罗米修斯锁在高加索山脉的一块岩石上，在古希腊人眼中，这块岩石象征着世界的尽头。在那里，普罗米修斯不断遭受一只鹰的折磨。

Not satisfied with having his revenge on humanity, Zeus also punished Prometheus directly. He chained him to a rock in the Caucasus Mountains which, to the ancient Greeks, represented the eastern end of the world. There he was continually tortured by an eagle.

潘多拉的故事实际上是一个道德寓言，旨在教导人们如何为人处世。例如，厄庇墨透斯的故事警示人们不要轻率行事，而应慎重考虑可能的后果。潘多拉的故事则警示人们不要出于愚蠢的好奇心而行事。

The story of Pandora is actually a moral tale, a kind of fable intended to teach men how to behave. For instance, Epimetheus is a warning against careless action taken without due consideration of possible consequences. Pandora herself is a warning against foolish curiosity.

许多神话故事都告诫人们要警惕那种自以为凌驾于法律之上的傲慢。这种傲慢会导致无礼的行为，漠视他人的权利。在希腊神话中，它通常表现为违抗神明——希腊人称之为“傲慢”（hubris）。当这种情况发生时，众神会安排复仇女神涅墨西斯（Nemesis）惩罚这个傲慢之人。涅墨西斯的名字源于希腊语，意为“分配”。换句话说，涅墨西斯确保一切事物得到公平分配。如果一个人拥有太多的好运以至于变得自夸、骄傲和傲慢，涅墨西斯就会让他遭受相应的厄运，以求平衡。

Many mythical warnings were against the kind of pride which made people consider themselves above the law. Such pride led to insolent behavior and a disregard of the rights of others. In the Greek myths, it usually involved defying the gods—the sort of pride the Greeks called “hubris.” When that happened, the gods saw to it that the proud individual was dealt with by Nemesis, the goddess of retribution. The name comes from Greek words meaning to “distribute.” In other words, Nemesis sees to it that matters are distributed evenly. If a person has so much good fortune that he becomes boastful, proud and insolent, she sees to it that he has a corresponding amount of bad fortune to even things out.

由于大多数希腊神话都涉及厄运而非好运来平衡事物，因此“复仇女神”在我们的语言中已经意味着无法避免的厄运。

Since most of the Greek myths involve matters evened out by bad fortune, rather than by good fortune, “nemesis” has come to mean, in our language, an unavoidable doom.

傲慢至今仍被认为是七宗罪中最严重的。根据我们自己的传说，路西法正是因为傲慢而堕落。当我们谈到“嫉妒的神灵”时，仍然保留着古希腊人对傲慢的这种看法，他们认为神灵不会允许任何人过于幸运。这就是为什么我们说“骄傲使人跌倒”，以及为什么我们在谈论自己多么幸运或多么富裕时会敲木头。据说这样做可以避开复仇女神的惩罚。

Pride is still considered the most serious of the seven deadly sins. It was through pride that Lucifer fell, according to our own stories. We still have this old Greek feeling about pride when we speak of “the jealous gods” who won’t allow anyone to be too lucky. That is why we say that “pride goeth before a fall” and why we knock wood when we talk about how fortunate we are, or how well off. That is supposed to keep off Nemesis.

骄傲使人跌倒的神话故事中，阿拉克涅的故事就是一个例子。她是吕底亚王国（位于小亚细亚西部）的一位少女，擅长纺织。她对自己的技艺极为自豪，甚至夸口说，就连包括纺织在内的实用技艺的女神雅典娜也比不上她，并向雅典娜发起了挑战。（这真是狂妄自大。）

An example of such a pride-goeth-before-a-fall myth is the story of Arachne. She was a girl of the kingdom of Lydia (in western Asia Minor) who was very skilled at weaving. She was so proud of her skill that she boasted that even Athena, the goddess of the practical arts, including that of weaving, could do no better, and challenged Athena to a contest. (There was hubris.)

雅典娜接受了挑战，两人都开始织锦。雅典娜在锦绣中织满了关于诸神的荣耀故事，而阿拉克涅则织满了关于诸神的丑恶故事。阿拉克涅的作品精美绝伦，但雅典娜的作品堪称完美。雅典娜对阿拉克涅织锦的内容感到愤怒，将她的织物撕成了碎片。阿拉克涅惊恐万分，自缢身亡。（这就是所谓的复仇。）

Athena accepted the challenge and both wove tapestries. Athena wove into hers all sorts of glorious stories about the gods while Arachne wove into hers unflattering stories about them. Arachne’s work was excellent but Athena’s was perfect. In anger at Arachne’s subject matter, Athena tore Arachne’s weaving to shreds and Arachne, struck with terror, hanged herself. (There was nemesis.)

雅典娜并非残暴的女神，她不愿事态发展到如此地步，于是松开了绳子，将阿拉克涅变成了一只蜘蛛。变成蜘蛛后，阿拉克涅继续吐丝织网，编织美丽的蛛网，她也依然悬挂在一根薄如蝉翼的蛛丝上，仿佛仍在试图自缢。当然，“阿拉克涅”在希腊语中就是“蜘蛛”的意思，这个神话的灵感很可能来源于对蜘蛛活动的观察。但它也蕴含着一个道理：切勿傲慢自大。

Athena, who was not a cruel goddess, didn’t want things to go that far, so she loosened the rope and changed Arachne into a spider. As a spider, Arachne continued spinning threads and weaving beautiful webs, and she also continues to hang from a strand of gossamer as though still trying to hang herself. Of course “arachne” is the Greek word for “spider” and the idea of the myth surely came from watching spiders at work. But it does teach a moral: avoid hubris.

在动物学中，这个女孩的名字得以流传，因为蜘蛛及其近亲被归入“蛛形纲”。

In zoology, the name of the girl lives on, since spiders and their relatives are put in the class “Arachnida.”

此外，任何像蜘蛛网一样轻薄的物质都被称为“蛛网膜”。例如，大脑和脊髓被双层膜包裹以起到保护作用。在双层膜的两层之间还有一层非常薄的膜，这层膜被称为“蛛网膜”。

Furthermore, anything as filmy and delicate as a spider’s web is said to be “arachnoid.” For instance, the brain and spinal cord are enclosed by a double membrane for protection. In between the two parts of the double membrane is a third membrane which is very thin and filmy. This is called the “arachnoid membrane.”

另一个此类神话的例子是法厄同的故事，他是太阳神赫利俄斯的凡人儿子。他为自己是太阳神的儿子而骄傲自满，认为自己可以驾驭太阳（太阳被描绘成一辆由骏马拉着的闪亮战车）横跨天空。他哄骗父亲答应让他这样做。（这真是狂妄自大。）

Another example of this sort of myth is that of Phaethon, the mortal son of Helios. He was so proud of being the son of the sun god, that he felt he could drive the sun (which was pictured as a gleaming chariot drawn by wild, gleaming horses) across the sky. He tricked his father into promising to let him do so. (That was hubris.)

法厄同驾驭着太阳，却发现自己无法控制骏马，它们偏离轨道，俯冲到离地面太近的地方。希腊人认为撒哈拉沙漠灼热的沙丘正是太阳战车掠过时灼烧大地的痕迹，并认为非洲土著的黝黑肤色也是由此造成的。为了拯救大地免遭毁灭，宙斯被迫用雷霆击杀了法厄同。（这就是复仇女神。）

Phaethon drove the sun but found he could not control the horses, which went out of their course and swooped too near the earth. The Greeks supposed the burning sands of the Sahara showed where the swooping sun chariot had scorched the earth and felt that the dark color of the African natives was also the result of it. To save the earth from destruction, Zeus was forced to kill Phaethon with a thunderbolt. (That was nemesis.)

“phaeton”（通常不带“h”，发音为“fay'uh-ten”）一词可以用来形容任何鲁莽的驾驶员。这个词也曾用来指没有顶棚或侧板的马车或汽车。这些车辆很轻便，比普通的重型车辆行驶速度更快。不过，这两种用法现在都已过时了。

The word “phaeton” (usually without the “h” and pronounced “fay’uh-ten”) can be applied to any reckless driver. The word was also applied to a carriage or automobile without top or sides. These were light vehicles, you see, that could be driven more quickly than ordinary heavier ones. Both uses are out of date, now.

傲慢与报应的案例分别降临在父女二人身上。故事始于坦塔罗斯，他是宙斯的凡人儿子。他深受父亲和其他神祇的喜爱，甚至被允许参加众神的宴会，享用神食（ambrosia）和琼浆玉液（nectar）。神食（ambrosia）源自希腊语，意为“不朽”；琼浆玉液（nectar）则源自希腊语，意为“战胜死亡”。据说，食用神食后，神祇的血管中充满的是神血（ichor），而非血液，因此他们拥有永生。

One case of hubris and nemesis struck father and daughter separately. It begins with Tantalus, a mortal son of Zeus. He was a great favorite of his father and the other gods. He was even allowed to join the banquets of the gods and eat ambrosia and drink nectar. This is the food of the gods. “Ambrosia” comes from a Greek word meaning “immortal” and “nectar” from one meaning “death-defeating.” As a result of such food, the veins of gods are filled with ichor instead of blood, so that the gods are immortal.

如今，在更实际的语境中，“花蜜”和“琼浆玉液”被用来形容任何美味的食物。特别是，“花蜜”可以指任何甜的液体。例如，花朵中蜜蜂用来酿蜜的含糖液体就被称为“花蜜”。一种表皮光滑的桃子因其甜美的味道而被命名为“油桃”。

Nowadays, in more practical times, nectar and ambrosia are used to describe any delightful food. Nectar, particularly, can refer to any sweet liquid. The sugary fluid in flowers, used by bees to make honey, is called nectar, for instance. A type of smooth-skinned peach has been named “nectarine” for its sweet taste.

然而，回到坦塔罗斯身上——他因众神的友谊而沾沾自喜，竟将这些食物和饮料当作自己的私物，还带了一些回人间分给朋友们。不仅如此，他还到处炫耀此事。

To return to Tantalus, however—He felt so puffed up at the gods’ friendship that he acted as though this food and drink were his in actual fact and took some back to earth with him to give to his friends. Furthermore, he boasted about this.

复仇女神涅墨西斯紧随其后。他被宙斯杀死，并被囚禁在塔尔塔罗斯，遭受一种特殊的酷刑，这种酷刑与食物和饮水有关。他被迫永远站在齐颈深的水中。然而，每当他弯腰饮水时，水就会下沉并旋转消失。当他站起身时，水又会再次涌到他的脖子。与此同时，美味的水果在他眼前摇曳，但当他伸手去摘时，风却将它们吹走。就这样，尽管食物和饮水近在咫尺，他却永远忍受着饥渴的折磨。

Nemesis followed. He was killed by Zeus and condemned to a special torture in Tartarus, one which involved food and drink. He was forced to stand forever in water up to his neck. Every time he bent to drink, however, the water sank downward and swirled away. When he stood up, the water rose to his neck again. Meanwhile, delicious fruit dangled near his face, but when he reached out for it, the wind blew it out of his reach. Thus, with food and drink continually near, he suffered eternal hunger and thirst.

因此，我们有了“tantalize”（诱人诱惑）这个词，指的是任何旨在给予他人虚假希望，而这种希望却在即将实现之际被夺走的行为。同样，用来存放葡萄酒的玻璃柜被称为“tantalus”（诱人诱惑），因为人们可以看到酒，但没有钥匙就无法触摸。

For this reason, we have the word “tantalize” referring to any action which is designed to give someone false hope which is snatched away just as it seems on the point of coming true. In the same way, a glass cabinet in which bottles of wine may be locked away is called a “tantalus” because the wine can be seen but, without the key, cannot be touched.

1814年，令人着迷的钽被正式列入元素周期表。十二年前，瑞典化学家安德斯·古斯塔夫·埃克贝里发现了一种新金属。强酸无法对其产生影响。它能置身于强酸之中而不被“吸收”——也就是说，它不会与强酸发生反应，也不会消耗掉自身的能量。因此，1814年，瑞典化学家贝采利乌斯认为，这就像钽置身于水中却不被“吸收”一样。他将这种元素命名为“钽”（tantalum），这个名字沿用至今。

In 1814, the tantalized Tantalus entered the list of elements. Twelve years earlier, a Swedish chemist named Anders Gustaf Ekeberg discovered a new metal. Strong acids did not affect it. It could stand in strong acid without “drinking” it; that is, without reacting with it and using it up. In 1814, therefore, the Swedish chemist Berzelius decided that this was like Tantalus standing in water but not drinking. He named the element “tantalum” and that is now its name.

尼俄柏是坦塔罗斯的女儿，但她并没有从父亲的命运中学会谦逊。她有十四个孩子，七个儿子和七个女儿，她为孩子们俊美的容貌和卓越的成就感到无比自豪，认为没有哪个母亲能比她更幸运。她甚至夸耀说，就连勒托也只有一个女儿和一个儿子。

Niobe was the daughter of Tantalus, but she did not learn humility from his fate. She had fourteen children, seven sons and seven daughters, and was so proud of their good looks and accomplishments that she felt no other mother could be as lucky. Even Leto, she boasted, only had one daughter and one son.

当然，这些孩子正是阿波罗和阿尔忒弥斯。当他们听到母亲被嘲笑时，便展开了报复。阿波罗用箭射死了七个儿子，阿尔忒弥斯则用箭射死了七个女儿。

Of course these children were Apollo and Artemis and, on hearing their mother sneered at, they took revenge. Apollo killed the seven sons with his arrows and Artemis killed the seven daughters with hers.

尼俄柏怀中失去了她的小女儿，她为失去的孩子痛哭不已，最终，出于怜悯，众神将她变成了一块石头，从中涌出源源不断的泉水。

Niobe, in whose arms the last daughter died, wept ceaselessly for her lost children and eventually, out of pity, the gods turned her into a stone out of which a spring of water continued to run.

尼俄柏和她的父亲坦塔罗斯一样，也被列入了化学元素的名录。1801年，英国化学家查尔斯·哈切特认为他在一块最初发现于康涅狄格州的岩石中找到了一种新元素。他以哥伦比亚（美国的一个富有诗意的名称）命名了这种新元素为“铌”。

Niobe, like her father, Tantalus, also entered the list of chemical elements. In 1801, an English chemist, Charles Hatchett, thought he had located a new element in a rock that had first been found in Connecticut. He named the new element “columbium” after Columbia, a poetic name for the United States.

然而，关于铌是否与几乎同时发现的钽相同，一直存在争议。直到1846年，德国化学家海因里希·罗斯才证明铌与钽相似，但并非完全相同。为了体现相似性，他建议以钽的近亲——他的女儿尼俄柏的名字来命名。很长一段时间里，美国化学家称这种元素为“columbium”，而欧洲化学家则称之为“niobium”。几年前，美国化学家最终妥协，如今该元素的官方名称均为“niobium”。

However, some argument arose as to whether columbium wasn’t actually identical with tantalum, which was discovered at about the same time. It wasn’t until 1846 that a German chemist, Heinrich Rose, showed that columbium was similar to tantalum but not identical. For the sake of its similarity, however, he suggested that it be named after a close relative of Tantalus; to wit, after his daughter, Niobe. For a long time, American chemists called the element columbium, while the European chemists called it “niobium.” A few years ago, the American chemists gave in. The official name everywhere is now niobium.

“警惕傲慢”并非希腊人唯一能引申的寓意。还有一个广为人知的神话，蕴含着截然不同的寓意。这个故事的主角是迈达斯，他是戈尔迪乌斯的儿子。戈尔迪乌斯是弗里吉亚王国（位于小亚细亚）的第一位国王。

The moral of “beware hubris” was not the only one the Greeks could draw. There is a well-known myth which has a moral of an entirely different sort. This involves Midas, the son of Gordius. Gordius was the first king of Phrygia (a kingdom in Asia Minor).

戈尔迪乌斯乘坐牛车进入都城时，受到了如同国王般的欢迎。据说，神谕曾指示市民们要接纳下一个乘坐这种交通工具进入都城的人。

Gordius was greeted as king when he rode into the capital city in an ox-cart. It seems an oracle had advised the citizens to accept the next man to enter the city on such a vehicle.

戈尔迪乌斯做的第一件事就是将牛车的轭和缰绳打成一个极其复杂的结，以此表明他再也不用使用这辆牛车了。他宣称，谁能解开这个结，谁就能征服整个亚洲。之后，他登上了这座城市的王位，并将这座城市改名为“戈尔迪乌姆”。尽管许多人尝试解开这个结，但无人成功。由此，我们今天仍然有“戈尔迪之结”这个短语，用来形容极其棘手且无法解决的问题。

Gordius’s first act was to tie the yoke of the ox-cart with the reins in a very complicated knot, to show that he would never have to use that ox-cart again. He announced that anyone who untied that knot would conquer all Asia, and then went on to be king in the city, now renamed “Gordium.” Though many tried to untie the knot, none ever succeeded. From that, we still have our phrase “a Gordian knot” to represent a very tangled and insoluble problem.

这个传说虽属神话，但戈尔迪之结却真实存在。至少在公元前333年，当亚历山大大帝途经弗里吉亚时，有人向他展示了这个结。他并没有试图解开它，而是拔剑斩断，之后继续征战四方，征服了亚洲所有他能到达的地区，从未尝败绩。由此便有了“斩断戈尔迪之结”这个短语，意指以直接而出人意料的行动解决复杂问题。

The legend was mythical but the knot actually existed. At least when Alexander the Great passed through Phrygia in 333 B.C. he was shown the Gordian knot. He made no attempt to untie it. Instead, he drew his sword and calmly cut through it, then went on to conquer all those parts of Asia he could reach with his armies, without once being defeated. From this comes the phrase “to cut the Gordian knot,” meaning to solve a complicated problem by direct and unexpected action.

但正是戈尔迪乌斯的儿子迈达斯的行为，揭示了一个特殊的道德教训。他变得非常富有，却一心只想变得更加富有。由于迈达斯曾帮过狄俄尼索斯一个忙，这位神祇答应给他一个愿望，让他可以随心所欲地许愿。迈达斯立刻许愿，让他触摸到的一切都变成黄金。

But it was Midas, the son of Gordius, whose actions pointed a particular moral. He grew quite wealthy but had his mind entirely fixed on the desire to grow still more wealthy. Because of a favor Midas had done for Dionysus, that god offered Midas a free wish for anything he wanted. At once Midas wished that everything he touched should turn to gold.

我们至今仍称之为“点石成金”或“点石成金”，并用它来指代任何在商业上特别成功的人。可以说，他所触及的一切都变成了黄金。

We still speak of this as the “Midas touch” or the “golden touch” and use it to refer to anyone who is particularly successful in business. Everything he touches, so to speak, turns to gold.

然而，尽管大多数人仍然钦佩并羡慕这种能力，希腊人却指出了截然相反的寓意。迈达斯发现，他触摸的每一件物品都变成了黄金，整座宫殿及其所有陈设也随之变成了黄金，但这却让一切都变得单调乏味，丑陋不堪。他尝试吃喝的东西，一碰到也立刻变成了黄金，以至于他面临着饿死的威胁。最后（在神话的后期版本中），他的女儿跑向他，当他拥抱她时，她也变成了黄金。

However, although most people still admire and envy this ability, the Greeks pointed out the opposite moral. Midas found that his entire palace and all its furnishings turned to gold, as he touched object after object, but that made everything rather monotonous and ugly. Everything he tried to eat and drink turned to gold as soon as he touched it so that he was threatened with starvation. Finally (in a late version of the myth) his daughter ran to him, and turned to gold when he embraced her.

他不得不恳求狄俄尼索斯收回礼物，狄俄尼索斯答应了。显然，无论我们信不信，这其中的寓意我们都耳熟能详，那就是“金钱并非万能”。

He had to beg Dionysus to withdraw the gift, and Dionysus did. Apparently the moral is one well known to us, whether we believe it or not. It is that “money isn’t everything.”

如果希腊人对爱情不感兴趣，那他们就不是人类了。因此，他们的许多神话自然而然地就成了我们今天所说的“爱情故事”。其中一些故事非常感人，流传了近三千年之久。

The Greeks would not have been human if they weren’t interested in romance. It is natural, therefore, that many of their myths should have been what we would call today “love stories.” Some of them are very touching and have remained famous for nearly three thousand years.

例如，有关于俄耳甫斯的故事，他是太阳神阿波罗和缪斯女神之首卡利俄珀的儿子。有这样一对父母，俄耳甫斯歌喉动人也就不足为奇了。他的歌声能让岩石都靠近，野兽也会乖乖地趴下来聆听。

There is, for instance, the story of Orpheus, the son of Apollo and Calliope, chief of the Muses. With such parents, it is not surprising that Orpheus should be a sweet singer. At his singing, the very rocks would move closer while wild beasts would lie down tamely to hear.

他在这方面名声大噪，以至于“奥菲恩”至今仍指悦耳动听、令人陶醉的歌声。同样，许多音乐厅为了纪念他而被命名为“奥菲姆”，这个名字后来又被用于歌舞杂耍剧院和电影院。

So famous was he in this respect that “Orphean” still means melodiously enchanting. Similarly, various music halls were called “Orpheum” in his honor, and the name passed on to vaudeville theaters and movie houses.

俄耳甫斯娶了欧律狄刻为妻，但短暂的幸福过后，欧律狄刻被毒蛇咬伤身亡。俄耳甫斯悲痛欲绝，决定前往冥界，将她带回人间。他弹奏着七弦琴，放声歌唱，一路下到冥界。卡戎被他的琴声深深吸引，载着他渡过冥界；地狱犬刻耳柏洛斯也低下头，放俄耳甫斯通过。亡灵们聚集在他周围，在音乐的旋律中，悲伤地回忆起前世的苦难；塔尔塔罗斯的酷刑也随之停止，恶魔们驻足聆听。

Orpheus married Eurydice, but after a short period of happiness, Eurydice was bitten by a snake and died. Orpheus was inconsolable and decided to go down to Hades and get her back. Playing his lyre and singing, he went down to the underworld. So enchanted by his playing was Charon that he took him across; and Cerberus bowed his heads and let Orpheus pass. The shades flocked around, remembering sadly their past lives at the sound of the music, and the tortures in Tartarus stopped while the demons paused to listen.

就连冥王哈迪斯铁石心肠的脸上，在俄耳甫斯的到来下也流下了眼泪。他将甜美的歌者欧律狄刻还给了这位歌者，但有一个条件：在他到达人间之前，永远不要回头看她。

Tears came even to the iron face of Hades at the approach of Orpheus and he gave the sweet singer back his Eurydice, on one condition: that he never look back to see her until he reached the world above.

俄耳甫斯继续向上攀登，弹奏着七弦琴，歌唱着。当他几乎走出冥界，太阳的光芒已隐隐可见时，他再也无法忍受。他转身去看欧律狄刻是否真的跟在他身后。她确实跟了上来，但当他转身的那一刻，她却突然发出一声悲伤的哭喊，无助地张开双臂，飘然离去。

Back upward traveled Orpheus, still playing his lyre, still singing. And when he was almost out of Hades, when the light of the sun could already be dimly seen, he could bear it no longer. He turned to see if Eurydice was really following. She was, but when he turned, she suddenly drifted away with a sad cry, holding out her arms helplessly.

俄耳甫斯急忙往下跑，但现在没人听他的了。卡戎拦住了他，不让他上船。对岸的刻耳柏洛斯发出可怕的咆哮，远处的哈迪斯则冷冷地摇了摇头。

Orpheus rushed back downward but now no one would listen. Charon barred him from the boat. Cerberus on the other side growled terribly, and Hades, in the distance, shook his head stonily.

欧律狄刻失踪了！

Eurydice was lost!

后来，俄耳甫斯成为神秘宗教的中心人物。毕竟，他曾到过冥界，又返回人间。事实上，他的故事是最著名的神秘故事之一，“俄耳甫斯教”至今仍意为“神秘的”。

Orpheus was later made the center of a mystery religion. He had after all been in the underworld and returned. His was one of the most famous mysteries, in fact, and “Orphic” still means “mystical.”

另一个故事是关于一位名叫厄科（Echo，源自希腊语，意为“声音”）的山林仙女。她因喋喋不休而触怒了赫拉，被罚几乎完全沉默。她只能重复别人对她说的最后一句话。

Another story is that of a mountain nymph, called Echo (from a Greek word meaning “sound”). She had offended Hera with her chattering tongue and had been condemned to almost complete silence. She could only repeat the last words anyone said to her.

厄科爱上了一位名叫纳西索斯的英俊青年，但她无法表达自己的感情，因为她只能重复他临终前的话语。纳西索斯对她冷酷无情，轻蔑地对待她。这是一种傲慢，随之而来的是报应。纳西索斯在水中看到了自己的倒影。他从未见过自己的倒影，也不知道那是自己。他爱上了倒影中的自己。

Echo fell in love with a handsome young man named Narcissus, but she could not make her feelings plain because she could only repeat his last words. Narcissus treated her with unfeeling harshness and scorned her. This was a kind of hubris, and nemesis followed. Narcissus saw his own reflection in the water. He had never seen it before and did not know it was himself he saw. He fell in love with the reflection.

当然，这对他毫无益处，轮到他被拒绝了。他日渐消瘦，最终死去，化作一朵花，人们至今仍以他的名字称之为“水仙花”。而我们至今仍然称那些极度自负的人为“自恋者”。

Naturally, that did him no good and it was his turn to be rejected. He pined away and died, turning into a flower which is still called the “narcissus” in his honor. And we still call anyone who is extremely conceited “narcissistic.”

7位英雄

7 THE HEROES

根据神话传说，在远古时期，神与人自由交往。有些孩子父母一方是神，一方是人。这样的孩子有时是半神，有时则只是凡人，但却拥有非凡的力量和勇气。这些人就是英雄。

IN THE EARLY DAYS, according to the myths, gods and men associated freely. Children were born with one parent a god and one human. Such children were demigods occasionally. Sometimes they were merely human, but of unusual strength and bravery. These were heroes.

英雄们生活的时代被称为“英雄时代”。因此，一个国家历史上任何一段时期，只要发生过许多激动人心的事件，涌现出许多勇敢的人物，都被称为“英雄时代”。

The age in which the heroes lived was called the “Heroic Age.” As a result, any period in a nation’s history when there were many stirring events and many brave men is now termed a “Heroic Age.”

希腊人编造了无数关于这些超人——这些人与神之子——的传说。每一位后来的神话创造者都让笔下的英雄比前一位更加英勇无畏。结果，这些英雄传说变成了激动人心的冒险故事，充满了奇幻色彩。（然而，现在这个词已经泛指任何勇敢的人；或者仅仅指故事的主角，即使他并非特别勇敢。）

The Greeks made up innumerable legends about these supermen, these offspring of man and god. Each succeeding mythmaker made the heroes he sang of even more heroic than the mythmaker before had done. The result was that the hero legends became rousing adventure stories with strong elements of fantasy. (Now, however, the word has come to mean any brave man; or just the main character of a story, even if he is not particularly brave.)

例如，其中一个传说讲述的是英雄珀尔修斯的故事，他被描绘成宙斯的儿子。他和母亲达那厄流亡到一位国王的宫廷，这位国王企图强迫达那厄嫁给他。为了救母亲，珀尔修斯提出用可怕的戈耳工美杜莎的头颅来赎回她。

One such legend, for instance, involved the hero Perseus, who was pictured as the son of Zeus. He and his mother, Danae, were refugees at the court of a king who intended to force an unwelcome marriage on Danae. To save his mother Perseus offered to ransom her by bringing back the head of the dreadful Gorgon, Medusa.

由于珀尔修斯是宙斯的儿子，深受众神的喜爱，所以他们都帮助了他。雅典娜送给他一面光洁的盾牌，这样他就能通过反射看到美杜莎，从而避免被石化。赫尔墨斯送给他一把魔法剑，哈迪斯送给他一顶隐形头盔。珀尔修斯还收集到了飞行凉鞋和一个用来装美杜莎头颅的特殊袋子。这些都是他历经种种冒险才从几位仙女那里得到的。

Since Perseus was a son of Zeus and was loved by the gods, they helped him. Athena gave him a polished shield so that he could see Medusa by reflection only and avoid being turned to stone. Hermes gave him a magic sword and Hades gave him a helmet which would make him invisible when worn. Perseus also collected flying sandals and a special bag in which to hold Medusa’s head. These were obtained from certain nymphs whom he only reached after various adventures.

珀尔修斯发现戈耳工们正在沉睡，便穿着飞行凉鞋俯冲而下，头盔让他隐身于空中。他借用雅典娜的盾牌观察，用赫尔墨斯的剑瞄准美杜莎，砍下她的头颅，装进袋子里。美杜莎的姐妹们惊醒后追赶他，发出凄厉的尖叫，但他身手敏捷，飞速逃脱。

Perseus found the Gorgons sleeping, swooped down on his flying sandals, invisible thanks to his helmet. He used Athena’s polished shield to see with, aimed Hermes’ sword at Medusa, cut her head off and put it in the bag. Medusa’s sisters woke and pursued him, shrieking horribly, but he flew swiftly and escaped.

珀尔修斯在返回途中经过阿特拉斯，出于怜悯，他向阿特拉斯展示了美杜莎的头颅。阿特拉斯随即变成了一座巨大的石山，他的惩罚也随之结束。

On his way back, Perseus passed Atlas, whom he showed Medusa’s head out of pity. Atlas was turned into a huge stone mountain and his punishment was ended.

珀尔修斯还经过南方的一个王国，那里正上演着另一场悲剧。这个王国的国王是刻甫斯，王后是卡西奥佩娅。他们有一个美丽的女儿，名叫安德洛墨达。卡西奥佩娅极其虚荣，她吹嘘自己和女儿比海仙女涅瑞伊得斯还要美丽。这是狂妄自大，报应很快就来了。

Perseus also passed a southern kingdom where another drama was taking place. The king of the land was Cepheus and the queen was Cassiopeia. They had a beautiful daughter named Andromeda and Cassiopeia was so vain that she boasted that she and her daughter were more beautiful than the Nereids, the nymphs of the sea. This was hubris, and nemesis followed quickly.

为了维护涅瑞伊得斯的名誉，波塞冬派遣海怪肆虐陆地海岸。神谕说，唯一能让它离开的办法就是将安德洛墨达献祭给它。于是，安德洛墨达被锁在海岸边的一块岩石上，她的父母站在一旁痛哭流涕。

Poseidon, to avenge the honor of the Nereids, sent a sea monster to ravage the coasts of the land. The only way to make it leave, said the oracles, was to sacrifice Andromeda to it. So Andromeda was chained to a rock at the shore, while father and mother stood weeping by.

珀尔修斯飞掠而过，正值海怪逼近安德洛墨达之际。他迅速潜入水中，从袋子里取出美杜莎的头颅，展示给海怪看，海怪顿时变成了石头。珀尔修斯娶了安德洛墨达为妻，带着她回到母亲仍在等候的地方。他随身带着美杜莎的头颅，但当他提起此事时，国王和朝臣们却嘲笑他，认为他的故事不过是吹牛。为了证明自己的清白，他再次从袋子里取出头颅，将他们全部变成了石头。

Perseus flew by just as the sea-monster was approaching Andromeda. Diving rapidly, he took Medusa’s head out of its bag and showed it to the monster, who turned to stone. Perseus married Andromeda and took her back with him to where his mother still waited. He had Medusa’s head with him, but when he said so, the king and courtiers laughed at him and denied his story as a boastful tale. As proof, he took the head out of the bag and turned them all to stone.

珀尔修斯将美杜莎的头颅交给雅典娜，雅典娜将其置于盾牌中央，使其成为完美的防御武器。这面盾牌不仅能抵挡长矛和刀剑，任何靠近的人甚至在攻击发生之前都会被石化。雅典娜的盾牌（最初来自宙斯）名为埃癸斯（Aegis），这个词有时也拼作“egis”，至今仍在英语中用来指代任何强大的保护或防御。

Perseus then gave Medusa’s head to Athena, who placed it in the middle of her shield, making it a perfect protection. Not only did it ward off spears and swords but anyone approaching was turned to stone before an attack could even be made. Athena’s shield (which she originally got from Zeus) was called Aegis and that word, sometimes spelled “egis,” is still used in the language to refer to any strong protection or defense.

珀尔修斯的故事在希腊人中非常流行，他们用星座来代表故事中的各种人物，并将这些星座绘制在天空中。“珀尔修斯”是金牛座附近一个星座的名称。

The story of Perseus was very popular with the Greeks and they filled the sky with constellations representing the various characters in it. “Perseus” is the name given to a constellation near Taurus.

该星座中最引人注目的恒星有时被称为“魔鬼星”。它是该星座中第二亮的恒星，因此也被称为“英仙座β星”。

The most remarkable star in the constellation is one sometimes called the “Demon star.” It is the second brightest star in the constellation and is therefore also called “Beta Persei.”

每隔两天半，大陵五会在五个小时内突然失去大部分亮度，然后再过五个小时恢复亮度。我们现在知道，这是因为大陵五有一颗较暗的伴星，它围绕着大陵五运行，偶尔会遮挡住大陵五的光芒，使其黯然失色。然而，对于对此一无所知的希腊人来说，这种亮度骤减的现象显得异常怪异，违背自然规律，因为几乎没有其他恒星会出现这种情况。因此，将这样一颗奇异的恒星比作美杜莎似乎很贴切。或许正是这颗恒星，促使他们将英仙座归入美杜莎所在的星群。

Every two and a half days, Algol suddenly loses most of its brightness over a period of five hours and then regains it in five more hours. We now know that this is because Algol has a dimmer companion which circles it and occasionally gets in the way of its light, eclipsing it. To the Greeks, however, who knew nothing about this, the loss of light seemed remarkable and against nature, since almost none of the other stars seemed to behave so. It must have seemed fitting then to make such a monstrous star represent Medusa. Perhaps that star was the cause of their placing Perseus in that particular collection of stars.

每年八月，地球运行到太阳附近一片特定的太空区域时，经常会出现流星雨。这片区域聚集着大量的流星体，它们围绕太阳运行。这些流星似乎来自英仙座附近的天空，因此被称为“英仙座流星雨”。

In August, there is often a display of meteors as the earth passes through a section of space in which a large collection of these small pebbles follow a path around the sun. The meteors seem to come out of a portion of the sky in the constellation Perseus, and so they are called the “Perseids.”

紧邻英仙座的是仙女座。而北面则是仙后座和仙王座。

Right next to the constellation Perseus is the constellation Andromeda. And to the north are the constellations of Cassiopeia and Cepheus.

仙王座只有几颗暗淡的星星。正如神话中所述，在星空中，国王的光芒应该被他意志坚定的王后所掩盖，因为仙后座有五颗明亮的星星。然而，希腊人恐怕无法预料到，仙王座中的一颗星星竟会让他意外地名声大噪。

The constellation Cepheus contains only dim stars. It seemed right that in the skies, as in the myth, the king should be outshone by his strong-minded queen, for Cassiopeia contains five bright stars. However, the Greeks could not have foretold that one of the stars in Cepheus was to make him unexpectedly famous.

仙王座右腿上的那颗星叫做“仙王座δ星”。仙王座δ星和大陵五一样，亮度会发生变化。然而，大陵五会被食遮蔽，而仙王座δ星则不会。天文学家发现，仙王座δ星会脉动，它会以规律的运动变亮和变暗。后来​​，天文学家发现许多恒星都以这种方式脉动，亮度忽明忽暗。为了纪念第一颗被发现的这类恒星，所有脉动星都被命名为“造父变星”。

The star in the right leg of Cepheus is called “Delta Cephei.” Delta Cephei, like Algol, changes its brightness. However, Algol is eclipsed, but not so Delta Cephei. Delta Cephei, astronomers found out, pulsates. It gets larger and smaller in a regular motion. Afterward, astronomers found that a number of stars pulsated in this manner, growing dimmer and brighter. In honor of the first such star to be discovered, the whole class of pulsating stars was called “Cepheids.”

1912年，美国天文学家亨丽埃塔·勒维特发现了利用造父变星测量遥远天体距离的方法，而其他方法对此束手无策。因此，造父变星在天文学中变得极其重要，并受到密切研究。有趣的是，如此重要的天体竟然以一个源自一位默默无闻、妻管严的神话国王的词语来命名。

In 1912 an American astronomer, Henrietta Leavitt, discovered how to use the Cepheids to find the distance of far-off objects in space where no other method could be used. As a result, Cepheids have become extremely important in astronomy and are very closely studied. It is amusing to think that objects so important in astronomy are called by a word that traces back to an obscure and henpecked mythical king.

就连即将吞噬仙女座的海怪也能在天空中找到。它就是鲸鱼座（Cetus，可以翻译为“鲸鱼”或“海怪”）。

Even the sea monster that was to devour Andromeda is to be found in the sky. It is there as the constellation Cetus (which can be translated as “whale” or “sea monster”).

珀尔修斯神话还有一部分尚未讲述。当珀尔修斯砍下美杜莎的头颅时，鲜血滴落在地上，从中化作一匹美丽的飞马，名叫珀伽索斯。

One other portion of the Perseus myth remains to be told. When Perseus cut off Medusa’s head, drops of blood fell to the ground and out of them was formed, full grown, a beautiful winged horse called Pegasus.

任何一匹迅捷如飞的骏马都可以被称为“飞马”。对于任何爱马之人来说，飞马这种生物从未真正存在过，无疑令人惋惜。诚然，有一种小型鱼类，身体两侧长着一对形似翅膀的大鳍，如今动物学家也称之为“飞马”。然而，它显然无法与美丽的骏马相提并论。

Any fiery and swift horse can be called a “pegasus.” To any lover of horses, it would seem a shame that something like Pegasus never really existed. To be sure, there is a small fish with large fins sticking out of either side of the body like a pair of wings, and that is nowadays called “Pegasus” by zoologists. However, it is certainly a poor substitute for the beautiful horse.

珀伽索斯曾被另一位英雄贝勒罗丰骑乘。他骑着珀伽索斯斩杀了可怕的奇美拉。（从此以后，骑士骑马屠龙的情节便出现在童话故事中。）

Pegasus was used by another hero, Bellerophon, as a mount. Riding on Pegasus, he slew the horrible Chimaera. (Ever since, knights on horses have been slaying dragons in the fairy tales.)

紧邻仙女座的是飞马座。飞马座中最亮的三颗星，加上仙女座中最亮的那颗星，构成了一个大正方形，被称为“飞马座四边形”。

Right next to the constellation Andromeda is that of Pegasus. The three brightest stars in this constellation, together with the brightest star in Andromeda, form a large square which is called the “Square of Pegasus.”

有时，希腊人会把他们的英雄们聚集在一起，组成一个庞大的队伍，形成一种“神话奇观”，以增加故事的刺激性。例如，猎杀“卡吕冬野猪”的故事就是如此。

Sometimes the Greeks managed to get their heroes into a large group, a kind of “myth-spectacular” for increased excitement. An example was the hunting of the “Calydonian boar.”

故事中的主人公是希腊中部卡吕冬国王的儿子墨勒阿革。墨勒阿革出生时，命运女神告诉他的母亲阿尔忒亚，这个婴儿只能活到火中一根燃烧的木柴（称为“火把”）完全燃尽为止。阿尔忒亚立刻抓起火把，熄灭火焰，并将其藏了起来。

The chief hero involved here was Meleager the son of the king of Calydon in central Greece. When Meleager was born, the Fates told his mother, Althaea, that the baby would live only until a certain burning piece of wood in the fire (a “brand”) was completely burned. Althaea at once seized the brand, put out the flame and hid it.

当墨勒阿革长大成人时，阿尔忒弥斯因不满卡吕冬国王的所作所为，派遣一头巨大的野猪肆虐国土。墨勒阿革组织了一支狩猎队去捕捉并猎杀这头野猪，许多英雄也加入了他们的行列。

When Meleager grew to manhood, Artemis, who was offended at the king of Calydon, sent a large boar to ravage the lands. Meleager organized a hunting expedition to trap and kill the boar and many heroes came to join the party.

例如，卡斯托耳和波吕丢克斯就是其中之一。他们是孪生兄弟，但波吕丢克斯拥有永生，而卡斯托耳却并非如此。这对孪生兄弟被称为狄俄斯库里兄弟，意为“神的儿子”，或者更确切地说是“宙斯的儿子”。后来，罗马人对他们极为尊崇，称波吕丢克斯为波吕克斯。我们今天所熟知的，正是这对孪生兄弟的卡斯托耳和波吕克斯。

Among them, for instance, were Castor and Polydeuces. These were twin brothers, but while Polydeuces was immortal, Castor was not. These twins were called the Dioscuri, which means the “sons of god” or, really, “sons of Zeus.” They were greatly honored later by the Romans, who called Polydeuces Pollux. It is as Castor and Pollux that the twins are best known to us.

事实上，他们的出生与一个著名的神话有关。宙斯为了赢得他们的母亲勒达的芳心，化身为天鹅。最终，勒达生下了两枚蛋。一枚蛋里孕育着波吕丢克斯和一个女孩，海伦；另一枚蛋里孕育着卡斯托耳和一个女孩，克吕泰涅斯特拉。这两个女孩在之后我将提到的神话中都扮演着重要的角色。

Their birth, in fact, involves a famous myth. Zeus wooed Leda, their mother, by turning himself into a swan. Eventually, Leda gave birth to two eggs. In one was Polydeuces and a girl, Helen. In the other was Castor and another girl, Clytemnestra. Both girls were important in myths I shall mention later.

宙斯一如既往地将他化身的动物形象置于星座之中，以此表达对他的敬意。这个星座如今被称为天鹅座，在拉丁语中意为“天鹅”。

Zeus, as usual, honored the animal form he had taken by placing it among the constellations. That constellation is now known as Cygnus, which in Latin means “swan.”

卡斯托耳和波吕丢克斯与另一对双胞胎兄弟伊达斯和林刻俄斯结下了深仇，他们也参加了那场野猪狩猎。伊达斯武艺更高，而林刻俄斯则以敏锐的视力著称，据说他能看穿岩石。猫科动物的视力当然也被认为很好，有一种大型猫科动物至今仍被称为“猞猁”，尽管它当然不可能像其他动物一样看穿岩石。视力极佳的人仍然被形容为“拥有猞猁般的眼睛”。

Castor and Polydeuces carried on a great feud with another pair of twins, Idas and Lynceus, who were also present at the boar hunt. two, Idas was the better fighter, but Lynceus was renowned for his sharp eyesight, being able to see through rocks. Cats, of course, are supposed to have sharp eyes, and one variety of large cat is still called “lynx,” though, of course, it can no more see through rocks than any other creature can. People with very good eyesight are still said to be “lynx-eyed.”

这场世仇的高潮出现在一场混战中：林克修斯被卡斯托耳杀死，卡斯托耳又被伊达斯杀死，伊达斯最终被波吕丢克斯杀死。波吕丢克斯拥有不死之身，无法死去，但他对兄弟的爱如此深切，以至于他向宙斯祈祷，希望宙斯能收回他的永生，除非他能与卡斯托耳共享。最终，宙斯允许他们二人轮流在天堂和冥界度过余生。

The climax of the feud came when, in a battle royal, Lynceus was killed by Castor who was killed by Idas who was killed by Polydeuces. Polydeuces, being immortal, could not die, but such was his love for his brother that he prayed to Zeus to take away his immortality unless he could share it with Castor. As a result, Zeus let both spend their days alternately in heaven and in Hades.

在星座中可以找到北河二（Castor）和波吕丢克斯（Polydeuces）。黄道十二宫的第三个星座是双子座，在拉丁语中意为“双胞胎”。这个星座包含两颗明亮的恒星，一颗名为“北河二”（Castor），另一颗名为“北河三”（Pollux）。北河三比北河二更亮一些，这也很合理，因为北河三（波吕丢克斯）是永生的兄弟。或许正是因为两颗明亮的恒星如此靠近，才让希腊人将它们称为双子座。古罗马人对这对双胞胎的敬意至今仍有迹可循。我们仍然会说“By Jiminy”（以吉米尼的名义发誓）。

Castor and Polydeuces are to be found among the constellations. The third sign of the zodiac is Gemini which, in Latin, means “twins.” The constellation contains two bright stars, one called “Castor” and one “Pollux.” Pollux is somewhat the brighter of the two, as is reasonable since Pollux (Polydeuces) was the immortal brother. Probably the existence of two bright stars so close to each other was what gave the Greeks the notion of making it a twin constellation. A trace of the old Roman respect for the twins remains. We still swear by them when we say “By Jiminy.”

另一位参与猎杀卡吕冬野猪的英雄是雅典人忒修斯。他是雅典神话创造者们的宠儿，他最著名的冒险经历是使雅典摆脱了向米诺斯国王纳贡的义务。

Another hero present at the hunting of the Calydonian boar was Theseus the Athenian. He was the pet of the Athenian mythmakers and his most famous adventure is one in which he freed Athens of the necessity of paying tribute to King Minos.

每年雅典都要派七名青年男子和七名青年女子前往克里特岛，米诺斯会将他们喂给一个拥有人身牛头的怪物。这个怪物就是弥诺陶洛斯，或称“米诺斯之牛”，它被囚禁在迷宫之中。事实上，代达罗斯建造迷宫正是为了囚禁弥诺陶洛斯。

Every year Athens had to send seven young men and seven young women to Crete, where Minos fed them to a monster with a man’s body and a bull’s head. The monster was the Minotaur or “bull of Minos” and it was kept in the Labyrinth. In fact, it was to keep the Minotaur that Daedalus built the Labyrinth.

双方达成的协议是，如果雅典人能够杀死弥诺陶洛斯并走出迷宫，那么进贡就将结束。

The agreement was that if ever the Athenians could kill the Minotaur and then get out of the Labyrinth, the tribute would be ended.

有一年进贡之时，雅典国王埃勾斯的儿子忒修斯已长大成人，他也加入了进贡的队伍。在克里特岛，弥诺斯的女儿阿里阿德涅爱上了忒修斯，并送给他一团线，让他能用这团线找到走出迷宫的路。忒修斯和其他人一起进入迷宫，边走边解开线团，最终杀死了弥诺陶洛斯，然后顺着线团走出了迷宫。进贡就此结束。

When the time of tribute came one year, Theseus, who was the son of Aegeus, king of Athens, and who was now grown to manhood, had himself included as one of the young men. In Crete, Ariadne, daughter of Minos, fell in love with Theseus and gave him a ball of twine which he could use to find his way out of the Labyrinth. Theseus entered with the others, unwound the ball of twine as he walked, met and killed the Minotaur, then followed the twine out of the Labyrinth. The tribute was ended.

此前，载着年轻人前往克里特岛的雅典船只，往返都挂着黑帆，以示哀悼。忒修斯曾向埃勾斯承诺，他一定会打败弥诺陶洛斯，并会带着象征喜悦的白帆凯旋而归。然而，由于当时的兴奋，忒修斯竟忘记更换船帆了。

On previous occasions, the Athenian ship which carried the young people to Crete used black sails, going and returning, out of mourning. Theseus had promised Aegeus that he would defeat the Minotaur and would return with white sails signifying joy. However, Theseus forgot to change the sails, in all the excitement.

埃勾斯站在高高的岩石上眺望船只，看到黑色的船帆，以为忒修斯已经死了，绝望之下纵身跃入大海自尽。因此，希腊和土耳其之间的地中海海域被称为“爱琴海”。

Aegeus, watching for the ship from a high rock, saw the black sails, assumed Theseus was dead and in despair plunged into the sea to his death. As a result the portion of the Mediterranean sea between Greece and Turkey is called the “Aegean Sea.”

据说忒修斯在世时曾游历希腊的雅典地区，斩妖除魔，惩恶扬善（就像我们现代西部电视剧里的英雄一样）。他最著名的遭遇之一，便是与一个名叫普罗克鲁斯忒斯的强盗交锋。普罗克鲁斯忒斯有一张床，他会强迫受害者躺在上面。如果受害者太长，他就砍掉他们腿的一部分；如果太短，他就把他们的腿掰断，拉长。总之，无论用什么方法，他都要让受害者勉强躺在床上。

Theseus, in his time, was supposed to have wandered over the Athenian portion of Greece, destroying monsters and killing brigands (like the heroes on our modern television Westerns). One of his most famous encounters was with a robber called Procrustes. Procrustes had a bed in which he forced his victims to lie. If they were too long for the bed, he cut off portions of their legs. If they were too short he pulled their legs out of joint to stretch them. Either way they were made to fit the bed.

忒修斯杀了他。然而，从那以后，任何试图强迫人们接受某种特定思维或行为方式的做法都被称为“普罗克鲁斯忒斯式的”。

Theseus killed him. Ever since, however, any attempt to force people into a particular way of thinking or behaving has been termed “procrustean.”

忒修斯另一次著名的冒险是远征亚马逊女战士，她们居住在小亚细亚。许多希腊英雄都曾与她们作战，通常英雄们都能取得胜利。忒修斯也不例外。他掳走了亚马逊女王希波吕忒，并娶她为妻。（莎士比亚的戏剧《仲夏夜之梦》的故事就发生在忒修斯和希波吕忒婚礼前的夏日假期期间的雅典。）

Another famous adventure of Theseus involved an expedition against the Amazons, a group of warrior women supposed to live in Asia Minor. Many of the Greek heroes fought against them. Usually the heroes won. Theseus did, too. He carried away the queen of the Amazons, who was named Hippolyta and married her. (Shakespeare’s play, A Midsummer Night’s Dream, is supposed to take place in Athens during the holiday period that preceded the marriage of Theseus and Hippolyta.)

自那时起，体格强壮、肌肉发达的女性，尤其是那些从事男性工作的女性，就被称为“亚马逊女战士”。1541年，一位名叫弗朗西斯科·德·奥雷利亚纳的西班牙探险家，沿一条四十年前被发现、曾被赋予不同名称的南美洲大河逆流而上。途中，他与当地土著居民发生冲突，并发现部落中的女性也与男性并肩作战。因此，他将这条河命名为“亚马逊河”，并沿用至今。

Strong, muscular women, especially those who do a man’s work, have been called “amazons” ever since. In 1541, a Spanish explorer named Francisco de Orellana, was sailing up a large South American river, which had been discovered forty years earlier and which had been called by various names. At one point he had to fight a battle with natives and it seemed to him that the women of the tribe were fighting along with the men. For this reason, he named the river the “Amazon River” and it has kept that name ever since.

然而，回到卡吕冬野猪狩猎，其中最杰出的成员是阿塔兰忒，她本身就像一位亚马逊女战士，因为她是一位专门从事狩猎的女性，并且在体育比赛中可以胜过大多数男性。

However, to return to the Calydonian boar hunt, the most remarkable member was Atalanta, a kind of Amazon herself, for she was a woman who specialized in hunting and could outdo most men in athletic contests.

墨勒阿革爱上了她，但他的两个叔叔（墨勒阿革的母亲阿尔忒亚的兄弟）却不喜欢她，因为他们迷信，认为女人不应该从事男人的职业。

Meleager fell in love with her but his two uncles (the brothers of Meleager’s mother, Althaea) disliked her, being superstitious about allowing a woman to engage in a man’s occupation.

狩猎过程漫长而艰辛，阿塔兰忒率先伤到了野猪，最终由墨勒阿革亲自将其杀死。墨勒阿革剥下野猪的皮，献给阿塔兰忒，以表彰她率先猎杀野猪的功绩。

The hunt was long and hard, but Atalanta was the first to wound the boar, and Meleager himself finally killed it. Meleager skinned the boar and offered the skin as a gesture of honor to Atalanta for drawing first blood.

墨勒阿革的叔叔们对此强烈反对，墨勒阿革怒不可遏，拔剑将他们二人杀死。阿尔忒亚得知此事后，悲痛欲绝，为兄弟们哀悼不已。她愤怒地抓起自墨勒阿革幼年起就珍藏的未燃尽的火把，扔进火中，火把随即燃烧起来。墨勒阿革顿时感到一阵虚弱，随即死去。

Meleager’s uncles objected violently to this and Meleager, growing angry, drew his sword and killed them both. When this news was carried to Althaea, she was overcome with a spasm of grief for her brothers. Angrily, she snatched the unburned brand she had saved ever since Meleager’s babyhood and threw it in the fire where it burned. As it did, Meleager was overcome with sudden weakness and died.

因此，任何关乎一个人生命或名誉，并可能毁灭他的物品，都被称为“阿尔忒亚的烙印”。

Because of this, any object on which a man’s life or reputation depends and by which he can be destroyed, is called an “Althaea’s brand.”

得知墨勒阿革的死讯后，他的姐妹们哀嚎不止，被众神变成了珍珠鸡，从此它们就一直发出凄厉的叫声。事实上，“meleagris”在拉丁语中就是“珍珠鸡”的意思，至今仍被用作珍珠鸡的动物学名称。

At the news of Meleager’s death, his sisters wailed and shrieked continuously and were changed by the gods into guinea hens, which have continued their wild shrieking ever since. In fact, “meleagris” is the Latin word for “guinea fowl” and it is still used today as a zoological name for the creatures.

另一次英雄集结是为了寻找“金羊毛”。金羊毛被保存在科尔基斯境内，科尔基斯位于黑海东端，靠近高加索山脉。

Another gathering of heroes took place on the occasion of the expedition to get the “Golden Fleece.” The Golden Fleece was kept in the land of Colchis which was located at the eastern tip of the Black Sea, near the Caucasus Mountains.

在神话时代的希腊人眼中，高加索山脉几乎就是世界的东端。那是他们不离开地中海所能到达的最东端。他们听说过里海，它位于高加索山脉以东，于是他们误以为里海只是环绕地球的海洋河流的一个海湾。

Now the Caucasus Mountains were just about the eastern end of the world to the Greeks of the mythmaking age. It was as far east as they could go without leaving the Mediterranean. They heard some rumors of the Caspian Sea, which lay to the east of the Caucasus, and were under the impression that the Caspian was just an inlet of the ocean-river that encircled the earth.

希腊人在遥远的东方创造的奇迹几乎与在遥远的西方创造的奇迹一样多。普罗米修斯被锁在高加索山脉；太阳神赫利俄斯本应从科尔喀斯开始他的西行之旅（这只是说科尔喀斯是当时世界上最东边的城市）。

The Greeks put almost as many wonders into this far east, as they put into the far west. Prometheus was chained to the Caucasus; and Helios was supposed to begin his westward journey at Colchis (just a way of saying that Colchis was the most easterly city in the world).

在黑海以北，希腊人想象着一片居住着被称为辛梅里安人的土地。据说他们生活在永恒的黑暗之中（这或许源于希腊人听到的关于北极地区的模糊传闻，据说那里太阳有时会连续数日甚至数月不见天日）。由此便衍生出了“辛梅里安的黑暗”一词，用来形容深不见底的黑暗。克里米亚半岛位于黑海北岸，如今是苏联的一部分，其名称正是“辛梅里安”（Cimmeria）一词的变体。

Just north of the Black Sea, the Greeks imagined a land inhabited by people called Cimmerians. These were supposed to live in eternal darkness (the result, perhaps, of vague rumors the Greeks heard of the Arctic region where the sun actually doesn’t shine for days or even months at a time). From this comes the phrase “Cimmerian darkness” for darkness that is deep indeed. The peninsula of Crimea, on the north shore of the Black Sea and now a part of the Soviet Union, is a version of the word Cimmeria.

总之，在众多奇观中，金羊毛就存在于科尔喀斯。据说，它是宙斯派来拯救年轻男子弗里克斯和他的妹妹赫勒的飞羊的皮毛。当时，他们正面临被恶毒继母杀害的危险。两个年轻人爬上羊背，羊便神奇地飞向科尔喀斯。

In any case, among the other wonders, the Golden Fleece existed at Colchis. It was supposed to be the pelt of a flying ram sent down by Zeus to save the lives of a young man named Phrixus and his sister, Helle, who were in danger of being killed by a wicked stepmother. The two youngsters climbed upon the ram’s back and it flew (in magic fashion) swiftly toward Colchis.

当船只驶过分隔欧亚大陆的第一组狭窄海峡时，赫勒号沉没了。因此，这条海峡被称为“赫勒斯滂海峡”（意为“赫勒之海”）。现代，海峡的名称已经更改，我稍后会提到。然而，位于海峡欧洲一侧的加里波利半岛的尖端，至今仍被称为“赫勒角”。

When passing over the first set of narrow straits that separate Europe and Asia, Helle fell off and drowned. That strait was therefore called “Hellespont” (“sea of Helle”). In modern times the name has been changed, as I shall mention later. However, the tip of the Gallipoli Peninsula, on the European side of the strait, is still called “Cape Helle.”

在科尔喀斯，人们将公羊献祭给宙斯，并将它的金羊毛悬挂在神庙中，由一条不眠之龙守护。公羊也被置于黄道十二宫中，成为白羊座（Aries），“白羊座”一词源于拉丁语。在古代，太阳在春分时进入白羊座，因此白羊座成为黄道十二宫的第一个星座。尽管春分点（其位置每年缓慢变化）如今已不在白羊座，而是在双鱼座，但白羊座仍然被认为是黄道十二宫的第一个星座。

At Colchis, the ram was sacrificed to Zeus and its golden fleece was hung in a temple where it was guarded by an unsleeping dragon. The ram was also placed in the zodiac as the constellation Aries, which is the Latin word for “ram.” In ancient times, the sun entered Aries at the time of the spring equinox and that made Aries the first sign of the zodiac. It still is considered this, even though the spring equinox (which shifts position slowly from year to year) is no longer in Aries but is now in Pisces.

故事接下来转向伊阿宋，他的父亲埃宋曾是希腊北部伊奥尔科斯城的国王。埃宋的兄弟珀利阿斯武力夺取了王位，埃宋带着儿子伊阿宋逃亡。伊阿宋长大成人后，返回伊奥尔科斯，并继承了王位。珀利阿斯为了除掉他，提出用王位换取金羊毛，希望伊阿宋在争夺金羊毛的过程中丧命。

The plot of the story next turns to Jason whose father, Aeson, had been king of the city of Iolcus in northern Greece. Pelias, the brother of Aeson, had taken over the throne by force and Aeson with his son, Jason, had fled. Once grown to manhood, Jason returned to Iolcus and claimed the throne. Pelias, to get rid of him, offered him the throne in exchange for the Golden Fleece, expecting Jason to die in the attempt to get it.

伊阿宋接受了挑战，并召集所有英雄前来助阵。四十九位英雄响应号召（加上伊阿宋，共五十人），他们建造了一艘有五十支桨的船。希腊人认为这是有史以来建造的第一艘大型船只。这艘船以设计者阿尔戈斯的名字命名为阿尔戈号 。（然而，希腊语“argos”意为“快速”，这或许才是船名的由来。）因此，这五十位为寻找金羊毛而扬帆远航的英雄被称为阿尔戈英雄（“阿尔戈号的水手”）。

Jason accepted the challenge and sent out a call to all the heroes to gather and aid him. Forty-nine of them did (with Jason making a total of fifty) and a ship was built with fifty oars. The Greeks supposed this to be the first large ship ever built. It was called the Argo after the designer of the ship, Argus. (However, the Greek word “argos” means “swift” and that could have been the reason for the naming of the ship.) The fifty heroes who sailed for the Golden Fleece are therefore called the Argonauts (“sailors of the Argo”).

（奇怪的是，“argosy”一词指的是满载货物的大型船只，但它与阿尔戈号并无关联。“argosy”一词是名为“拉古萨”（Ragusa）的海港的变体，古代这类船只就是从那里起航的。）

(Oddly enough, the word “argosy” meaning a large ship with a rich cargo has no connection with the Argo. The word “argosy” is a twisted version of the seaport named “Ragusa” from which such ships would set sail in ancient times.)

阿尔戈号是一艘非常著名的船，以至于它被列入了星座之中。它位于遥远的南方，从希腊纬度只能看到它的一部分。它是一个很大的星座，如果不是现在被分成了四个较小的星座，它本应是天空中最大的星座。这四个星座分别是：船帆座（Vela，拉丁语意为“帆”）、船底座（Carina，拉丁语意为“龙骨”）、船尾座（Puppis，拉丁语意为“船尾”）和罗盘座（Pyxis，拉丁语意为“罗盘”）。

The Argo was such a famous ship that it was placed among the constellations. It is located far to the south and only parts of it can be seen from the latitude of Greece. It is a large constellation, and would be the largest in the sky except that it is now broken up into four smaller constellations. These are Vela, which means “sail” in Latin, Carina, which means “keel,” Puppis, which means “stern,” and Pyxis, which means “compass.”

至于“阿尔戈英雄”这个词，它在动物学中留下了痕迹。有一种海洋生物，是章鱼的近亲，雌性会形成一个精致美丽的贝壳。古人认为这种生物用贝壳作帆，因此称它为“鹦鹉螺”（nautilus），在希腊语中意为“水手”。

As for the word “Argonaut,” that leaves a trace in zoology. There is a certain sea creature, a relative of the octopus, the female of which forms a delicate and beautiful shell. The ancients supposed the creature used the shell as a sail and so the creature was called “nautilus” which, in Greek, means “sailor.”

现代人称其为“纸鹦鹉螺”，是因为它的贝壳非常脆弱，而且为了将其与其他也被称为“鹦鹉螺”的生物区分开来。伟大的动植物分类学家林奈将纸鹦鹉螺归入“船蛸目”（Argonauta）。纸鹦鹉螺的学名是“Argonauta argo”。然而，尽管名字里有“鹦鹉螺”，但由于它并不用贝壳作帆，所以实际上它并不是真正的“水手”。

In modern times, it was named the “paper nautilus,” because of the delicacy of the shell and to distinguish it from other creatures also called “nautilus.” Linnaeus, the great classifier of plants and animals, put the paper nautilus in the order “Argonauta.” The scientific name of the paper nautilus is “Argonauta argo.” However, since it does not use the shell as a sail, the creature is not really a sailor despite its name.

卡吕冬野猪狩猎中的一些英雄也出现在阿尔戈英雄之中。卡斯托耳、波吕丢克斯、伊达斯、林刻俄斯、阿塔兰忒和墨勒阿革都在其中。（墨勒阿革在狩猎中与阿塔兰忒相遇后不久便去世，因此后两位为何也在场，这一点没有解释，但有时神话创造者之间似乎并不在意。）俄耳甫斯也在场，当时他尚未与欧律狄刻结婚。

Some of the heroes of the Calydonian boar hunt were also present among the Argonauts. Castor, Polydeuces, Idas, Lynceus, Atalanta, and Meleager were among the group. (How the last two could be there when Meleager supposedly met Atalanta at the hunt and died shortly after is not explained, but then one mythmaker doesn’t worry about the next, sometimes.) Present also was Orpheus, who had not yet married Eurydice.

阿尔戈英雄们横渡爱琴海，登陆各个岛屿，与友好的岛民欢聚一堂，或与心怀不轨之人浴血奋战。他们还曾击退过骚扰盲王的哈耳庇厄。

The Argonauts traveled across the Aegean Sea, landing on islands where they feasted with friendly inhabitants or battled suspicious ones. At one point they drove off the Harpies who were carrying on a campaign of annoyance against a blind king.

为了进入黑海，他们必须穿过两块名为辛普勒加德斯（Symplegades）的移动岩石，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“相互撞击”。这两块岩石时不时会猛烈碰撞，试图通过的船只都会被压碎。阿尔戈英雄们放出一只鸽子，鸽子飞入岩石之间。它们相互碰撞，在岩石向后移动的过程中，阿尔戈英雄们驾驶着阿尔戈号全速穿过。在岩石再次碰撞之前，阿尔戈号安全通过，辛普勒加德斯也从此固定不动，再也没有移动过。

To enter the Black Sea, they had to pass between two moving rocks called the Symplegades, which comes from Greek words meaning “striking together.” These clashed together forcefully every once in a while and ships trying to pass through were crushed. The Argonauts let loose a dove which flew between the rocks. They clashed and, as they moved back, the Argonauts drove the Argo through at top speed. Before the rocks could come together again, the Argo had passed safely and the Symplegades were fixed, never to move again.

最终，在与一群投掷金属羽毛如矛的鸟类战斗之后，他们抵达了科尔喀斯。科尔喀斯国王的女儿美狄亚爱上了伊阿宋，就像阿里阿德涅爱上忒修斯一样。美狄亚给了伊阿宋一种特殊的药膏，使他能够抵御巨龙（厄喀德娜之子）的喷火，从而帮助他杀死了守护金羊毛的巨龙。

Eventually, after a battle with birds which dropped metal feathers like spears, they reached Colchis. The daughter of the king of Colchis was Medea, who fell in love with Jason, just as Ariadne had fallen in love with Theseus. Medea helped him kill the dragon (a child of Echidna) that guarded the Fleece by giving him a special lotion that protected him against its breath of fire.

得到金羊毛后，美狄亚帮助伊阿宋带着它逃走，她运用魔法和计谋击退了追击的船只。他们只有一次真正陷入危险，那就是经过塞壬女妖的巢穴时。然而，俄耳甫斯及时出现，救了他们。他用歌声盖过了塞壬女妖。所有人都被俄耳甫斯的歌声吸引，塞壬的歌声无人理会。

Once they had the Golden Fleece, Medea helped Jason escape with it, using spells and stratagems to fight off the pursuing ships. Only once were they in real danger and that was when they passed the Sirens. Here, however, Orpheus came to the rescue. He outsang them. All ears were for Orpheus and the Sirens went unheard.

回到伊奥尔科斯后，伊阿宋娶了美狄亚，并重夺王位。在希腊人眼中，美狄亚是女巫或魔法师的典范，他们编造了许多关于她的恐怖故事。然而，她精通各种草药的疗愈功效却是不争的事实。例如，神话中就记载了她如何让伊阿宋年迈的父亲喝下特制的药水，从而恢复青春。

Back in Iolcus, Jason married Medea and regained his throne. Medea, to the Greeks, was the prize example of a female magician, or witch. They kept making up horrible stories about her. However, what seems basic is that she knew the healing properties of various herbs. For instance, there is one passage in the myth which tells how she restored youth to Jason’s old father by having him drink a special potion.

在现代医学出现之前，会用草药治病的女性往往令人畏惧。人们担心，如果有人得罪了她们，她们会不会利用草药知识来杀人灭口呢？即使在近代，那些过于精通植物疗法的女性仍然会因此惹上麻烦。

Now before the days of modern medicine, women who could use herbs to cure disease were looked upon with fear. Might they not use their knowledge to poison as well as cure if they were crossed? Even in quite modern times, women who were too acquainted with plant remedies were in trouble over it.

然而，美狄亚的疗法或许留下了一个重要的痕迹，这并非完全不可能。我们今天所说的“medicine”（医学）一词可能就源于她。这仅仅是一种推测，因为没有人能确切地说出这个词的起源，但它源于美狄亚的说法却是一个有趣的理论，我个人很喜欢。

And yet, it is just barely possible that Medea’s treatments may have left one important trace. Our word “medicine” may trace back to her. It is only a suggestion, for no one is quite sure how this word actually arose, but that it arose from Medea is an interesting theory, and I rather like it.

希腊最伟大的英雄是赫拉克勒斯，他更为人熟知的名字是拉丁语版本的赫拉克勒斯。赫拉克勒斯身材高大、强壮有力，肌肉发达，人们总是把他描绘成身披狮皮、手持巨棒的形象。

The greatest of the Greek heroes was Heracles, who is better known by the Latin version of his name, Hercules. Heracles was a tall, strong, muscular man, always pictured as dressed in a lion’s skin and carrying a gigantic club.

他拥有超人的力量，完成了超人的任务。希腊人乐于编造更多关于他的故事。最终，几乎所有的神话都离不开赫拉克勒斯，甚至出现了一句谚语：“没有赫拉克勒斯，什么都不是。”

He had superhuman strength and accomplished superhuman tasks. The Greeks delighted in making up more and more stories about him. Eventually, scarcely a myth could be told without dragging in Heracles somehow, until a proverb arose, “Nothing without Heracles.”

他堪称古代世界的保罗·班扬，以至于“大力神”一词既指超乎常人的困难，也指超乎常人的力量。本书不可能详尽介绍所有关于赫拉克勒斯的传说，即使是简要提及也不行，所以我只会提及那些在语言中留下痕迹的传说。

He was a kind of Paul Bunyan of the ancient world, so that the word “herculean” means something either of superhuman difficulty or of superhuman strength. It would be impossible to run through all the myths about Heracles in this book, even briefly, so I will just mention those that have left traces in the language.

首先，赫拉克勒斯在群星中以“武仙座”命名。武仙座内有一个球状星团，是北半球最亮的星团。1934年，该星团中的一颗恒星爆炸，亮度之高甚至肉眼可见。这种爆炸的恒星被称为“新星”。这颗近年来最亮的恒星被命名为“武仙座新星”。

To begin with, Heracles is honored among the stars by the constellation “Hercules.” It contains a globular cluster of stars which is the brightest to be seen in the northern hemisphere. In 1934, a star in this cluster exploded and grew so bright it could be seen with the naked eye. Such an exploding star is called a “nova.” This particular one, the brightest in recent years, was called “Nova Herculis.”

赫拉克勒斯是宙斯和凡人女子阿尔克墨涅的儿子。赫拉自然心生嫉妒。她用计让宙斯发誓，底比斯王室下一个出生的孩子将成为国王。宙斯明知赫拉克勒斯即将出生，却还是发誓了。赫拉随后故意推迟赫拉克勒斯的出生，让同属王室的另一位王子欧律斯透斯先于他出生。就这样，赫拉克勒斯被迫服侍欧律斯透斯，直到完成十二项伟大的任务。

Heracles was a son of Zeus and a mortal woman, Alcmena. Of course, Hera was jealous. Through trickery, she got Zeus to swear that the next child born in the royal house of the city of Thebes was to be king. Zeus did so, knowing that Heracles was about to be born. Hera then delayed Heracles’ birth just long enough for another prince of the same house, Eurystheus, to be born first. In this way, Heracles was condemned to serve Eurystheus until he had achieved twelve great tasks.

另一种解释是，赫拉克勒斯像神话中偶尔发生的那样，陷入了疯狂。疯狂之时，他无法控制自己的力量，杀死了所有他能看到的人。为了赎罪，他承担了十二项任务。

Another explanation is that Heracles went mad as he did every once in a while in the myths. When he was mad, he could not control his own strength but killed everyone in sight. As atonement for such killings, he undertook the twelve labors.

第三种解释则是一种非常注重道德的解释，出现的时间比其他解释晚得多。据说赫拉克勒斯曾受到“快乐”和“美德”的感召。

Still a third explanation is a highly moral one made up considerably later than the others. Heracles was supposed to have been approached by “Pleasure” and “Virtue.”

享乐让他过上轻松愉悦的生活，而美德则让他过上辛勤劳作的生活，最终获得永生。赫拉克勒斯选择了后者，经过无数的劳作，他成为了神。选择美德而非享乐的道路，有时被称为“赫拉克勒斯的选择”。

Pleasure offered him a life of ease and delight. Virtue offered him a life of toil, with immortality at the end. Heracles chose the latter and, after many labors, became a god. To choose the path of virtue over pleasure is sometimes called “the choice of Heracles.”
