至于赫拉克勒斯的十二项功绩，无论是出于赫拉的恶意、他自己的疯狂，还是出于自愿，第一项便是杀死涅墨亚狮。这头狮子体型巨大，力大无穷，肆虐涅墨亚城周围的地区。据说它是厄喀德娜的孩子之一，它的皮毛坚韧无比，刀剑箭矢都无法穿透，就连赫拉克勒斯的棍棒也无法将其击断。最终，赫拉克勒斯徒手勒死了它。他剥下狮皮，终生佩戴，以此作为抵御武器的最佳护身符。

As for the labors that Heracles underwent, whether out of Hera’s spite, or his own madness, or his free choice, the first was to kill the Nemean lion. This was a lion of huge size and strength that was ravaging the district about the city of Nemea. It was supposed to be one of the children of Echidna and its hide was so tough that neither sword nor arrow could penetrate and even Heracles’ club broke against it. Eventually, Heracles killed it by strangling it with his bare hands. He stripped off its skin and wore it the rest of his life as the best protection against weapons.

尼米亚狮在星空中占据着黄道十二宫第五宫——狮子座（拉丁语意为“狮子”）的位置。狮子座中有一颗明亮的恒星，位于狮子的前爪处。天文学家称之为“狮子座α星”（Alpha Leonis），但“轩辕十四”（Regulus）才是它更为人熟知的名字。轩辕十四意为“小国王”，对于一颗位于百兽之王——狮子座中的恒星来说，这个名字可谓恰如其分。

The Nemean lion finds its place in the stars as the fifth sign of the zodiac, Leo (the Latin word for “lion”). The constellation contains a bright star in the forepaw of the lion. This is called “Alpha Leonis” by astronomers but “Regulus” is its more familiar name. This means “little King,” an appropriate name for a star that is found in the king of beasts.

作为他的第二项任务，赫拉克勒斯消灭了厄喀德娜的另一个孩子——九头蛇许德拉。它生活在勒尔内地区的沼泽地里，因此被称为“勒尔内许德拉”。起初，赫拉克勒斯砍掉许德拉的头（你可能还记得它有九个头），新的头却不断长出来。于是，赫拉克勒斯让他的一个侄子用燃烧的木头烧灼每个头的脖子，这样新的头就无法再长出来了。许德拉的第九个头是不死的，赫拉克勒斯不得不把它埋在一块巨石下，它仍然会不停地折断。

As his second labor, Heracles destroyed another of the children of Echidna, the Hydra. It lived in a marsh in a district called Lerne and was therefore called the “Lernean Hydra.” At first, as Heracles cut the heads off the Hydra (it had nine, you may remember) new heads kept growing back. Heracles therefore had a nephew of his use a burning log to sear each neck as a head was lopped off. In this way new heads could not grow back. The ninth head of the Hydra was immortal and Heracles had to bury it, still snapping away, under a huge rock.

赫拉克勒斯在执行这项任务时，嫉妒心极强的赫拉派了一只螃蟹去咬他的脚后跟。赫拉克勒斯把它踩死了，但赫拉为了感谢螃蟹的功劳，把它放逐到了星空之中。如今，它就是黄道十二宫的第四个星座——巨蟹座（拉丁语中“螃蟹”的意思）。狮子座和巨蟹座都位于黄道十二宫中与赫拉克勒斯星座相对的一侧，仿佛它们都害怕再次与这位英雄交锋。

While Heracles was performing this labor, Hera (always jealous) sent a crab to nip at his heels. Heracles crushed it but Hera, in gratitude for the efforts of the creature, set it among the stars. It is now the fourth sign of the zodiac, Cancer (the Latin word for “crab”). Both Leo and Cancer are on the side of the zodiac opposite the constellation Hercules, as though both feared to tangle with the hero again.

6月21日，太阳运行到最北端，在古代，它位于巨蟹座。此时，太阳的光芒直射一条穿过墨西哥中部、埃及南部、印度和中国南部的直线。因此，这条直线被称为“北回归线”。

On June 21, when the sun is as far north as it ever gets, it was in the constellation of Cancer during ancient times. It then shines directly over a line that passes through central Mexico, southern Egypt, India, and southern China. This is called the “Tropic of Cancer,” in consequence.

赫拉克勒斯捕获了一头速度超乎常人的鹿和一头力大无比的野猪，并且都活捉了它们。之后，他开始了第五项任务：清理国王奥革阿斯的牛棚。他必须在一天之内完成这项任务。由于牛棚已经三十年没有清理过，而且里面饲养了三千头牛，因此污垢堆积如山。从此以后，任何极其肮脏或腐败的地方都被称为“奥革阿斯牛棚”。

After capturing a deer of superhuman speed and a boar of superhuman strength, taking both alive, Heracles undertook his fifth labor. This was to clean stables that belonged to a king named Augeias. He was required to do this in one day. Since the stables hadn’t been cleaned in thirty years and since three thousand cattle had been kept in it, the accumulation of filth was tremendous. Ever since, anything that is extremely filthy or corrupt has been referred to as an “Augean stable.”

赫拉克勒斯通过改道两条河流，让河水流经牛棚，将其冲洗干净，从而完成了这项任务。因此，以强有力的突然行动来根除犯罪或腐败，就如同“清洗奥吉亚斯的牛棚”。

Heracles accomplished this task by diverting two rivers which sent their currents through the stables, washing them clean. And so, to end crime or corruption by strong and sudden action is “to cleanse the Augean stables.”

接下来，赫拉克勒斯必须征服长着金属羽毛的鸟、喷火公牛、食人马以及整个亚马逊女战士大军。然而，他的第十项任务却是前往遥远的西方，那里充满了奇观。他要去取回属于一个名叫革律翁的怪物的牛群，革律翁住在西洋的一个岛屿上。革律翁有三个头和六条手臂，而守护这些牛群的还有一条名叫奥特鲁斯的双头犬，它是厄喀德娜的另一个孩子。

Heracles next had to conquer birds with metallic feathers, a fire-breathing bull, man-eating horses and the entire Amazon army. For his tenth labor, though, he had to take a long trip to the far west where the marvels were. He had to fetch cattle that belonged to a monster called Geryon who lived on an island in the western Ocean. Geryon had three heads and six arms and the cattle were further guarded by a two-headed dog named Orthrus, another child of Echidna.

当然，赫拉克勒斯完成了这项壮举。但他在穿越地中海西端时，将西班牙与非洲分隔开来，而此前它们显然是连在一起的。因此，古人将海峡两侧的两座悬崖称为“赫拉克勒斯之柱”。这个短语至今有时仍被用作直布罗陀海峡（这个名称直到中世纪才出现）的诗意别称。

Heracles accomplished this, of course. But in passing the western end of the Mediterranean, he split Spain from Africa where, apparently, they had previously been joined together. The two cliffs on either side of the strait were called by the ancients “the pillars of Heracles” for that reason. This phrase is still sometimes used as a poetic synonym for the Strait of Gibraltar (a name which arose only in the Middle Ages).

在第十一项任务中，赫拉克勒斯必须原路返回，回到遥远的西方去取回赫斯珀里得斯的金苹果。（你可能还记得，赫斯珀里得斯是阿特拉斯的女儿。）这些金苹果和金羊毛一样，都由一条巨龙守护着。

In the eleventh labor, Heracles had to retrace his steps and go back to the far west to fetch the golden apples of the Hesperides. (The Hesperides, as you may remember, were daughters of Atlas.) These were guarded by a dragon just as the Golden Fleece was.

在其中一个版本的神话中，赫拉克勒斯用计骗取了它们。而在另一个版本中，他亲自出手，并在过程中斩杀了巨龙。赫拉对赫拉克勒斯的敌人总是心存感激，她将巨龙升入天空，化作天龙座（Draco），拉丁语中意为“龙”。天龙座蜿蜒于大熊座和小熊座之间，其头部则位于大熊座、小熊座和武仙座之间。

In one version of the myth, Heracles is supposed to have tricked Atlas into getting them. In another, he himself did the job, slaying the dragon in the process. Hera, always grateful to any enemy of Heracles, put the dragon in the heavens as the constellation Draco, which is Latin for “dragon.” Draco winds its starry way between Ursa Major and Ursa Minor, and its head lies between those two constellations and the constellation of Hercules.

在星座图中，赫拉克勒斯手持永恒的棍棒，显然，他似乎正举起棍棒再次杀死巨龙。

Heracles who, in the constellation, is pictured with his eternal club, of course, seems to be raising it to kill the dragon once again.

赫拉克勒斯完成这项任务后返回途中，途经非洲，经历了一系列奇遇，其中包括遇到一群小矮人，并与他们展开了一段滑稽的插曲，有点像格列佛与小人国的故事。

On his way back from the accomplishment of this task, Heracles passed through Africa and, among other adventures, met a race of tiny people, with whom he had a comic interlude, something like that of Gulliver and the Lilliputians.

希腊人坚信在埃及以南的非洲某处生活着一群身材矮小的人。他们称他们为“俾格米人”（Pygmies），这个词源于希腊语，意为“拳头”。这意味着这些人只有正常成年人紧握拳头，从肘部到指关节的距离，大约只有十四英寸高。当然，神话的创造者们不断地缩小他们的身高，直到他们只有拳头那么高，也就是四英寸。从此以后，任何比同类生物通常身高矮小的生物都被称为“俾格米人”。后来，到了十九世纪，当人们在非洲真正发现了一个身材矮小的黑人种族时，他们也被称作“俾格米人”，尽管他们的平均身高被证实大约是四英尺半，而不是四英寸甚至十四英寸。

The Greeks firmly believed that there were tiny people living in Africa somewhere south of Egypt. They called them Pygmies from a Greek word meaning “fist.” This meant that the people were only as tall as the distance from the elbow to the knuckles of the clenched fist of a normal man. This would make them about fourteen inches high. Naturally, the mythmakers kept making them smaller until they were only as big as a fist, or four inches high. Ever since, any creature smaller than the usual run of its type has been called “pygmy.” Then, when in the nineteenth century, a race of small Negroes was actually found in Africa, these were called Pygmies, though their average height proved to be four and a half feet or so, and not four inches or even fourteen inches.

赫拉克勒斯的第十二项也是最后一项任务，是将地狱犬刻耳柏洛斯从冥界带回人间。除此之外，赫拉克勒斯还陪伴阿尔戈英雄们走了一段路，攻占了许多城市，参与了许多决斗，并救出了许多落难少女。

For his twelfth and last labor, Heracles brought Cerberus up from Hades. In addition to all this, Heracles accompanied the Argonauts part of the way, captured many cities, fought many duels and rescued many damsels in distress.

他的死终究到来，如同他艰辛的一生一样悲惨。晚年，他娶了一位名叫德伊阿涅拉的美丽女子。有一次，他们渡过一条洪水泛滥的河流时，一个名叫涅索斯的半人马提出要帮助德伊阿涅拉过河。然而，他却想掳走她，德伊阿涅拉惊叫着向赫拉克勒斯求救。赫拉克勒斯立刻用涂有毒液的箭射中了涅索斯。（他使用的毒液来自九头蛇许德拉，没有比这更致命、更难解药的毒药了。）

When his death finally came, it was as tragic as his life had been laborious. Late in life, he married a beautiful woman named Deianeira. At one time, while crossing a river in flood, a Centaur named Nessus offered to help Deianeira across. He tried to make off with her, however, and Deianeira shrieked to Heracles for help. Instantly, Heracles shot Nessus with his poisoned arrows. (The poison he used had been obtained from the Hydra, and no poison was deadlier or more incurable.)

垂死的涅索斯为了报复，嘱咐黛安娜拉把他身上那件浸透了他鲜血的衣服收下。如果她觉得赫拉克勒斯对她失去了兴趣，就把这件衣服给他穿上，他的爱意就会重新燃起。

The dying Nessus, in revenge, told Deianeira to take the garment he wore, which had been soaked in his blood, and keep it. If ever she thought Heracles was losing interest in her, she should give him the garment to wear and his love would be renewed.

果然，几年后，黛安娜怀疑赫拉克勒斯不再爱她了。她把半人马的衣服给了他，并劝他穿上。赫拉克勒斯不知道这件衣服的来历，便穿上了，九头蛇的毒液瞬间渗入了他的身体。

Sure enough, some years later, Deianeira suspected that Heracles no longer loved her. She gave him the Centaur’s garment and urged Heracles to wear it. Not knowing where it had come from, Heracles put it on, and instantly the Hydra poison penetrated his body.

赫拉克勒斯感到剧痛难忍，几乎要发疯，他知道如果真的发疯，他会杀死所有在他视线范围内的人。他立即命人燃起一堆篝火，纵身跃入火中自尽，而黛安娜拉则悲痛欲绝，自刎而死。

So fierce was the pain that Heracles felt himself going mad and knew that if this happened, he would kill everyone in sight. He quickly ordered a fire to be built and leaped into it to his death, while Deianeira killed herself in grief.

从此以后，一件致命的礼物就被称为“涅索斯的衬衫”。

Ever since, a fatal gift has been termed “a shirt of Nessus.”

然而，希腊人为这个故事安排了一个圆满的结局。赫拉克勒斯被提升到天界，赫拉最终原谅了他。他与青春女神赫柏结为夫妻，结束了漫长而艰辛的一生，最终回到了他应得的永恒幸福之中。

However, the Greeks provided a happy ending to the story. Heracles was raised to heaven, where Hera finally forgave him. He was married to Hebe, the goddess of youth, and after his long, laborious life, he settled back to the eternity of bliss which he had earned.

8 特洛伊围城战

8 THE SIEGE OF TROY

在所有希腊英雄神话中，最著名的要数希腊人与特洛伊城之间的战争了。

THE MOST FAMOUS of all the Greek hero myths were those that concerned a war fought by the Greeks against the city of Troy.

这些英雄事迹因公元前850年左右由传奇诗人荷马创作的两首长诗而名垂青史。这两首诗被古希腊人视为最伟大的文学作品，时至今日，我们仍然认为它们位列最伟大的文学作品之列。荷马的诗歌仅讲述了特洛伊神话的一小部分，其余部分则由其他一些名气稍逊的诗人补充完善。

These were made famous for all time by two long poems written about those heroes around 850 B.C. by a legendary poet named Homer. These were considered the greatest of all works of literature by the ancient Greeks and we still consider them among the greatest today. The poems of Homer dealt with only a small portion of the Troy myths but other, lesser poets filled in the rest.

荷马因其对伟大事件的描写而闻名，“荷马式的”一词常被用来指“崇高”或“雄伟”。然而，在诗篇的某一部分，荷马描写众神看到跛脚的赫菲斯托斯四处奔走时，竟忍不住大笑起来。因此，这种长时间的、无法控制的笑声，尽管与崇高或雄伟毫不相干，却被称为“荷马式笑声”。

So famous is Homer for dealing with great actions that “Homeric” is often used to mean “sublime” or “majestic.” However, in one part of the poems Homer describes the gods as laughing without being able to stop at the sight of lame Hephaestus bustling about. For that reason, long fits of uncontrolled laughter, though anything but sublime or majestic, are called “Homeric laughter.”

在特洛伊神话中，希腊军队隶属于迈锡尼城邦的国王，迈锡尼被描绘成希腊最伟大的城市，而梯林斯则是另一座重要的城市。特洛伊城据说位于小亚细亚，靠近赫勒斯滂海峡，统治着从地中海通往黑海的海峡沿岸地区。

In the Troy myths, the Greek forces were under the king of the city of Mycenae, which was pictured as the greatest city in Greece, with Tiryns, another important city. Troy was supposed to be in Asia Minor near the Hellespont and to rule the territory about the straits leading from the Mediterranean to the Black Sea.

在近代的大部分时间里，历史学家都认为特洛伊战争完全是虚构的。迈锡尼和梯林斯只不过是一些小小的废墟村庄，至于特洛伊，他们甚至怀疑这座城市是否真的存在过。

During most of modern times, historians believed the Trojan War to be a complete legend. Mycenae and Tiryns were but tiny ruined villages and as for Troy, they doubted that any such town ever existed.

然而，1868年，一位名叫海因里希·施利曼的德国商人开始在他认为特洛伊可能存在的地方进行挖掘。他热爱荷马的诗歌，并深信其真实性，因此愿意将自己的财富投入到挖掘工作中。令全世界震惊的是，他在特洛伊遗址上发现了古代城市的遗迹。

In 1868, however, a German businessman named Heinrich Schliemann began to dig at the place where he thought Troy might exist. He loved the poems of Homer and believed they were true, so he was willing to put his own fortune into the digging. To the amazement of the whole world, he found traces of ancient cities on the site of Troy.

19世纪70年代，他前往希腊，在迈锡尼和梯林斯遗址进行挖掘，发现了比普通希腊城市古老得多的城市遗迹。之后，埃文斯又发现了克里特岛的古代文明，至此，特洛伊战争和特洛伊城都已存在。

Then, in the 1870’s, he went to Greece and dug at the sites of Mycenae and Tiryns and discovered the ruins of cities that were far older than the ordinary Greek cities. After Evans went on to discover the ancient civilization of Crete, that settled the matter. There had been a Troy and a Trojan war.

事情的经过大致是这样的：第一批希腊人在公元前2000年之前进入希腊。他们受到了当时已在克里特岛和希腊周边岛屿建立的米诺斯文明的影响。迈锡尼和梯林斯等城市也随之建立起来。

Apparently, what happened was this: The first Greeks entered Greece before 2000 B.C. They came under the influence of the Minoan civilization which was already established in Crete and the islands about Greece. The cities of Mycenae and Tiryns were established.

公元前1400年左右，新一波希腊人入侵希腊，他们被称为阿开亚人。他们摧毁了克里特人的统治，并征服了希腊世界。（忒修斯和弥诺陶洛斯的神话可能就是对这段历史的纪念。）在阿开亚人的统治下，迈锡尼成为希腊最强大的城市，四周环绕着巨大的“独眼巨人”城墙。这被称为“迈锡尼时代”。

About 1400 B.C. a new wave of Greeks invaded Greece, these being called the Achaeans. They helped destroy the Cretan rule and took over the Greek world. (The myth of Theseus and the Minotaur may be a memory of this.) Under the Achaeans, Mycenae became the most powerful city in Greece, surrounded by huge “cyclopean” walls. This was called the “Mycenean Age.”

阿开亚人开始与黑海沿岸地区进行贸易。希腊本身是一个多山的国家，农业发展困难，但黑海周边的城市却盛产粮食。（阿尔戈英雄的远征就是这段贸易开端的见证。）

The Achaeans reached out for trade with districts in the Black Sea. Greece itself is a mountainous country where agriculture is difficult, but the cities around the Black Sea grew grain in plenty. (The expedition of the Argonauts is a memory of this beginning of trade.)

位于黑海入口处的强大城市特洛伊控制着贸易，因为他们可以决定船只是否通行。他们收取高额通行费，因此变得富甲一方。大约在公元前1200年，日益强大的亚该亚人认为没有理由放弃这些通行费。他们入侵小亚细亚，围攻并摧毁了特洛伊城。

The powerful city of Troy situated at the entrance of the Black Sea controlled the trade since they could either let the ships pass or not. They collected stiff fees for passage and grew rich. So about 1200 B.C. the Achaeans, who were growing increasingly strong, saw no reason they should not save the fees. They invaded Asia Minor, laid siege to Troy and destroyed it.

关于这场围城战，吟游诗人编造故事，添油加醋地加入神祇的奇幻细节，并加以戏剧化的润色，最终形成了我们今天所知的特洛伊传说。荷马（或者我们现在统称为荷马的几位诗人）在公元前850年收集了这些碎片，并以此创作了流传至今的诗歌。

About this siege, the minstrels made up stories, added fantastic details about gods, and piled on dramatic improvements until we now have the Troy legend. Homer (or various poets whom we now lump under his name) collected the pieces in 850 B.C. and made the poems out of it that still survive today.

尽管传说中充满了奇幻色彩，但它仍然相当准确地描绘了迈锡尼时代的生活。例如，英雄们使用青铜盔甲和剑，因为当时铁器制造武器的技术尚未发展成熟。

Despite all the fantasy contained in the legend, it still depicts the life of the Mycenean Age fairly accurately. For instance, the heroes used bronze armor and swords, since the use of iron for weapons had not yet developed.

但我们还是来谈谈特洛伊的传说，而不是真实的历史吧。正是这个传说丰富了我们的语言，留下了痕迹；而对于真实的历史，我们至今仍然知之甚少。

But let’s turn to the legend of Troy, rather than to the real history. It is the legend that has added words and left traces in our language; while as for the real history, about that we still know very little.

传说始于一位名叫忒提斯的美丽海仙女。（她在天上占有一席之地，因为第十七颗被发现的小行星就被命名为“忒提斯”。）

The legend begins with a beautiful Nereid named Thetis. (She has a place in the heavens, since the seventeenth planetoid to be discovered was named “Thetis.”)

她美艳绝伦，宙斯和波塞冬都为之倾倒。然而，命运女神（或许是睿智的泰坦普罗米修斯）警告宙斯，忒提斯注定会生下一个比他父亲强大得多的孩子。这意味着任何神都不能娶她，因为她的孩子将会推翻宙斯，就像宙斯推翻克洛诺斯，克洛诺斯推翻乌拉诺斯一样。为了阻止这一切发生，众神决定让忒提斯嫁给一个凡人。

She was so beautiful that both Zeus and Poseidon were in love with her. However, the Fates (or perhaps the wise Titan, Prometheus) warned Zeus that Thetis was fated to have a child far more powerful than his father. This meant that no god could marry her since her child would then overthrow Zeus as Zeus had overthrown Cronus and Cronus had overthrown Uranus. In order to prevent that from happening, it was decided to marry Thetis to an ordinary mortal.

被选中的凡人是珀琉斯，他曾是阿尔戈英雄之一。珀琉斯是埃阿科斯的儿子，埃阿科斯是宙斯的儿子。嫉妒的赫拉憎恨埃阿科斯，降下瘟疫到埃阿科斯的王国，几乎毁灭了那里所有的人类。宙斯出于怜悯，将蚂蚁变成了人类，重建了这座城市。因此，埃阿科斯和他儿子珀琉斯的臣民被称为米尔米顿人，这个名字源于希腊语，意为“蚂蚁”。

The mortal chosen was Peleus, who had been one of the Argonauts. Peleus was the son of Aeacus who was the son of Zeus. The jealous Hera hated Aeacus and sent plagues to Aeacus’ kingdom that destroyed almost every man there. Zeus, in pity, changed ants into men that repopulated the city. For that reason, the subjects of Aeacus and of his son, Peleus, were called Myrmidons, from a Greek word meaning “ant.”

米尔米顿人是优秀的士兵，曾参加特洛伊战争。他们像蚂蚁一样，保持着高度的服从和纪律。即使在今天，我们仍然称那些为任何付钱的人而无情战斗的职业士兵为“米尔米顿人”。

The Myrmidons were excellent soldiers and fought at the Trojan War. They kept antlike qualities of obedience and discipline. Even now, we still call professional soldiers who will fight ruthlessly for anyone who will pay them “myrmidons.”

珀琉斯和忒提斯的婚礼盛大而欢乐，众神齐聚一堂，唯独不和女神厄里斯被意外地遗漏了。她突然出现，将一个苹果扔进人群，苹果滚落到赫拉、雅典娜和阿芙洛狄忒正在交谈的地方。

The occasion of the wedding between Peleus and Thetis was a time of great celebration and merrymaking. All the gods attended except Eris, the goddess of discord, who had accidentally been overlooked. She appeared suddenly and tossed an apple into the crowd which rolled to where Hera, Athena and Aphrodite were standing in conversation.

苹果上用金字刻着“献给最美的女神”，自然而然地，谁才是最美的女神这个问题就出现了。赫拉、雅典娜和阿芙洛狄忒都声称拥有这颗苹果，谁也不肯让给其他两位。也没有哪位神敢于在这件事上做出决定。（由于这个传说，任何引发人们或群体争吵的物品都可以被称为“纷争之苹果”。）

On the apple was inscribed in golden letters “For the Fairest,” and of course the question arose as to which goddess was most beautiful. Hera, Athena and Aphrodite all claimed the apple and not one would allow either of the other two to have it. Nor was any god brave enough to make a decision in the matter. (Because of this portion of the legend, any object over which people or groups of people quarrel can be called an “apple of discord.”)

唯一的办法就是将此事交由一位凡人来裁决，而这位凡人的裁决必须得到女神们的认可。最终，一位名叫帕里斯的年轻牧羊人脱颖而出，特洛伊城也由此进入了故事。

The only thing to do was to put it up to some mortal whose judgment the goddesses would agree to accept. The choice fell on a young shepherd named Paris, and with him, the city of Troy enters the story.

传说中，特洛伊所在的亚细亚地区最初是由一群来自克里特岛的人殖民的。这很可能是正确的，因为特洛伊最初很可能处于米诺斯文明的统治之下。

According to legend, the region of Asia Minor in which Troy was located was originally colonized by a group of men from Crete. This is probably correct, since Troy, most likely, was under Minoan rule to begin with.

早期国王之一是达达努斯，这片土地因此得名达达尼亚，他还建立了一座名为达达努斯的城市。这座城市在海岸边一直存在到很久以后，后来被称为赫勒斯滂海峡的现代名称“达达尼尔海峡”也由此而来。

One of the early kings was Dardanus, from whom the land was named Dardania and who founded a city named Dardanus. This city existed on the shore through much later times and from it came the modern name “Dardanelles” for the strait which had been earlier called the Hellespont.

（达达诺斯是昴星团成员之一厄勒克特拉的儿子，有时人们认为他就是失踪的昴星团成员。据说她为了不目睹特洛伊的毁灭而黯淡了自己的光芒。）

(Dardanus was a son of Electra, one of the Pleiades, and this is sometimes said to be the Lost Pleiad. She is supposed to have dimmed her light rather than witness the destruction of Troy.)

后来的国王包括特洛斯（他的儿子之一是伽倪墨得斯，宙斯将他带到了天界）和伊洛斯。他们都建立了城镇，最终合并成了传说中的那座城市。这座城市因特洛斯而被称为“特洛伊”，因伊洛斯而被称为“伊利昂”。

Later kings included Tros (one of whose sons was Ganymede, whom Zeus carried to heaven) and Ilus. Both founded towns which eventually joined to form the city of the legend. From the former, the city was called “Troy” and from the latter “Ilium.”

这两个名字在传说中都有出现！例如，荷马关于特洛伊围城的史诗名为《伊利亚特》 ，意为“关于伊利昂”。由于《伊利亚特》中充满了苦难和流血，这个词后来被幽默地（但很少见）用来指代任何漫长而悲惨的故事。

Both names are used in the legend! For instance, Homer’s poem about the siege of Troy is called the Iliad meaning “about Ilium.” Since the Iliad has a great deal of suffering and bloodshed in it, the word has come to be used humorously (but only rarely) to mean any long hard-luck story.

伊洛斯的儿子是拉俄墨冬，他是赫拉克勒斯时代的特洛伊国王。赫拉克勒斯前往特洛伊，从海怪手中救下了拉俄墨冬的女儿赫西俄涅，就像珀尔修斯救了安德洛墨达一样。拉俄墨冬背信弃义，拒绝支付他承诺的赏赐，于是赫拉克勒斯率领部下攻占了特洛伊，杀死了拉俄墨冬，拥立他的儿子普里阿摩斯为王。此外，他们还掳走了赫西俄涅。希腊人围攻特洛伊时，统治特洛伊的正是普里阿摩斯。

The son of Ilus was Laomedon, who was king of Troy in the time of Heracles. Heracles, on a trip to Troy, saved Hesione, the daughter of Laomedon, from a sea monster, as Perseus had saved Andromeda. When Laomedon went back on his word and refused the reward he had promised, Heracles and the men with him attacked Troy, captured it, killed Laomedon and put Laomedon’s son, Priam, on the throne. Moreover, they carried off Hesione a captive. It was Priam who was king of Troy at the time of the Greek siege.

普里阿摩斯娶了赫卡柏和其他女子为妻，共育有五十个儿子和十二个女儿。赫卡柏更为人熟知的名字是她的拉丁语版本：赫卡柏（Hecuba）。

Priam married Hecabe and other women and had a total of fifty sons and twelve daughters. Hecabe is better known under the Latin version of her name, Hecuba.

普里阿摩斯的长子是赫克托耳，他是特洛伊一方的首席战士。他始终处于劣势，却以沉着冷静的勇气对抗更强大的敌军。事实上，特洛伊人因能坚守十年之久而备受敬仰。“像特洛伊人一样工作”或“像特洛伊人一样战斗”意味着勤奋努力、坚韧不拔地工作或战斗。

Priam’s oldest son was Hector, who was the chief warrior on the Trojan side. He was the underdog at all times, fighting a stronger army with steady bravery. In fact, the Trojans as a whole were greatly admired for having held off stronger forces for ten years. The phrase “to work like a Trojan” or “to fight like a Trojan” means to work or fight with great industry and endurance.

尽管赫克托耳一直被视为爱国者的典范，但他的名字却逐渐有了“欺凌”或“威胁”的含义。诚然，赫克托耳在《伊利亚特》中发表了许多夸夸其谈的演说，但所有英雄都如此。这在当时是他们的惯例。

Yet although Hector has always been considered a model patriot, his name has come to mean “to bully” or “to threaten.” To be sure, Hector makes boastful speeches in the Iliad, but so did all the heroes. It was customary for them to do so.

普里阿摩斯的另一个儿子是帕里斯。帕里斯出生前，赫卡柏梦见自己即将出生的孩子变成了一根燃烧的火把。神谕告诉她，这预示着这个孩子将会是特洛伊城焚毁的罪魁祸首。于是，普里阿摩斯和她决定除掉这个孩子。他们把这项任务委托给一个牧羊人，但牧羊人不忍心杀死这个漂亮的孩子。他把孩子丢在山坡上，任其自生自灭。然而，帕里斯被牧羊人发现，并被他们抚养长大。

Another of Priam’s sons was Paris. Before Paris was born, Hecabe dreamed that the child she was going to have turned out to be a burning brand. An oracle assured her this meant that the child would be the cause of the burning and ruin of Troy. Consequently, Priam and she decided to have the child killed. They entrusted the task to a herdsman, who couldn’t bring himself to kill the pretty child. Instead, he left him exposed on a mountainside expecting him to die. However, Paris was found by shepherds and raised as one.

众女神来到巴黎，决定谁将获得纷争之苹果。

It was to this Paris that the goddesses came for a decision as to which was to get the apple of discord.

三位女神都试图贿赂他。赫拉许诺给他财富，雅典娜许诺给他英勇的名声。而阿芙洛狄忒则许诺给他世上最美丽的女人做他的妻子。阿芙洛狄忒作为美的女神，在任何公平的比较中都是三位女神中最美的，所以当帕里斯把金苹果给她时，这无疑是一个明智的选择。然而，赫拉和雅典娜却因此感到极大的冒犯，从此她们与帕里斯和特洛伊结下了不共戴天的仇。

The goddesses each tried to bribe him. Hera offered him riches and Athena offered him fame as a warrior. Aphrodite, however, offered him the most beautiful woman in the world as his wife. Now Aphrodite, as the goddess of beauty, was the most beautiful of the three in any fair competition, so that when Paris gave her the apple it was a good judgment. However, Hera and Athena were bitterly offended, and from then on they were deadly enemies of Paris and of Troy.

之后，帕里斯访问了特洛伊，并在各项体育赛事中屡获殊荣。人们通过他随身携带的拨浪鼓认出了他就是普里阿摩斯失散多年的儿子，而这拨浪鼓正是他童年时珍藏的纪念品。

After that, Paris visited Troy where he was victorious in various athletic events. He was recognized as the long-lost son of Priam by a rattle that had been exposed with him and that he still kept as a souvenir of his childhood.

然而，普里阿摩斯有个女儿名叫卡珊德拉，阿波罗赐予她预言的能力。后来，阿波罗对她心生不满，便暗中破坏了这份天赋，让她虽然预言准确，却无人相信。当帕里斯被带回普里阿摩斯家后，卡珊德拉曾疯狂预言他会给特洛伊带来毁灭，但依然无人相信。

However, Priam had a daughter named Cassandra to whom Apollo had given the gift of prophecy. Later, Apollo grew displeased with her, and spoiled the gift by arranging it so that although she always prophesied the truth, no one would ever believe her. When Paris was taken back into Priam’s family, she prophesied wildly that he would bring about the ruin of Troy, but no one believed her.

如今，任何预言末日和邪恶的人都被称为“卡珊德拉”，尤其当他的警告显得夸张且不被相信时。现代最著名的卡珊德拉当属温斯顿·丘吉尔。在20世纪30年代，他不断警告人们警惕纳粹德国的危险，但却无人相信。

Nowadays, any person who prophesies doom and evil is called a “Cassandra,” particularly if his warnings seem exaggerated and are not believed. The most famous Cassandra of modern times was Winston Churchill. During the 1930’s he constantly warned against the dangers of Nazi Germany and was not believed.

与此同时，阿芙洛狄忒必须履行她对帕里斯的承诺。当时，世界上最美丽的女人是斯巴达的海伦，她是勒达的女儿，卡斯托耳和波吕丢克斯的妹妹。海伦到了出嫁的年纪，希腊的每一位英雄都渴望娶她为妻，因为她的美貌令人叹为观止。

Meanwhile, Aphrodite had to fulfill her promise to Paris. At the time, the most beautiful woman in the world was Helen of Sparta, the daughter of Leda and the sister of Castor and Polydeuces. When the time came for Helen to marry, every hero in Greece was anxious to take her as wife, so great was her beauty.

她的父亲，斯巴达国王廷达瑞俄斯，害怕把她嫁给他们中的任何一个，以免其他人为了报复而向他发动战争。因此，他召集了英雄们，让他们同意让海伦自己选择，并承诺此后每个人都会保护被选中的人，抵御一切敌人。

Her father, Tyndareus, King of Sparta, was afraid to give her to any one of them, lest the rest make war on him in revenge. He therefore called the heroes together and got them to agree to let Helen make her own choice and to promise that thereafter each would defend whoever was chosen against all enemies.

海伦选择了墨涅拉俄斯，他是迈锡尼国王阿伽门农的弟弟，也是当时希腊最有权势的统治者。墨涅拉俄斯与海伦一同继承了斯巴达王位，成为斯巴达国王。而阿伽门农则娶了海伦的妹妹克吕泰涅斯特拉为妻。

Helen chose Menelaus, who was the younger brother of Agamemnon, the king of Mycenae and the most powerful ruler in Greece. Menelaus, along with Helen, received the throne of Sparta and became king. As for Agamemnon, he married Clytemnestra, the sister of Helen.

于是，阿芙洛狄忒决定将海伦许配给帕里斯为妻。帕里斯以王子的身份来到斯巴达。（卡珊德拉曾警告说这将是一次致命的旅程，但无人听从。）帕里斯受到了盛情款待，受到了无微不至的礼遇。然而，阿芙洛狄忒却让海伦爱上了他，并促使她在他前往特洛伊时与他同行。

It was Helen, then, whom Aphrodite decided to give to Paris for a wife. Paris arrived at Sparta as a visiting prince. (Cassandra warned this would be a fatal voyage but no one listened to her.) Paris was feasted and treated with every courtesy. However, Aphrodite caused Helen to fall in love with him and to join him when he left for Troy.

战争期间她一直待在那里，从此被称为“特洛伊的海伦”。和阿芙洛狄忒一样，她从此成为美的化身。

There she stayed throughout the war and has ever since been called “Helen of Troy.” Like Aphrodite, she has been the model of beauty ever since.

海伦被誉为史上最美的女子，这从她至今仍是一个流行的名字便可见一斑。在古代，人们常常从神话中汲取灵感，给孩子取名。然而，基督教兴起后，人们开始从《圣经》和圣徒的生平事迹中寻找名字。希腊神话则被认为不适合用来给孩子取名。

The memory of Helen as the most beautiful woman who ever lived shows itself in the fact that it is still a popular name. In ancient times, names taken from the myths were very commonly given to youngsters. Once Christianity took over, however, names were chosen from the Bible instead and from the saints. The Greek myths weren’t considered proper to use in naming children.

只有少数源自神话的名字在时尚潮流的转变中得以保留，而且这些名字大多是女孩的名字。例如艾琳（Irene）、辛西娅（Cynthia）、菲比（Phoebe）、黛安娜（Diana）、弗洛拉（Flora）、艾瑞斯（Iris）和格蕾丝（Grace）。杰森（Jason）则是源自神话的男孩名字的一个例子。

Only a few names from the myths survived this change in fashion and those were mostly girl’s names. Examples are Irene, Cynthia, Phoebe, Diana, Flora, Iris, and Grace. An example of a boy’s name that comes from the myths is Jason.

但自古希腊时代以来，在欧美地区始终广受欢迎的希腊神话名字非海伦莫属。它衍生出各种变体，例如海伦娜、艾伦、埃琳娜、伊莱恩、埃莉诺、埃莉诺拉、艾拉，更不用说像内莉和内尔这样的昵称了。毕竟，人们很难忘记这位史上最美丽的女人，以及——我们很快就会看到——引发史上最著名战争的罪魁祸首。

But the only name from Greek mythology that has always remained extremely popular through all of Europe and America ever since Greek times has been Helen. It has taken different forms such as Helena, Ellen, Elena, Elaine, Eleanor, Eleanora, Ella, to say nothing of pet versions like Nellie and Nell. It is not easy, after all, to forget the most beautiful woman who ever lived and the one, as we shall soon see, who caused the most famous war of all time.

发现妻子失踪后，墨涅拉俄斯派遣使者前往希腊各地，提醒各位英雄履行诺言，并请求他们与他一同远征特洛伊，夺回海伦。首先，使者前往特洛伊，要求归还海伦。普里阿摩斯拒绝了，理由是希腊人仍然扣押着他的妹妹赫西俄涅，赫西俄涅是被赫拉克勒斯掳走的。

Finding his wife gone, Menelaus sent messengers to all parts of Greece reminding the various heroes of their promise and requesting all of them to join him in an expedition against Troy to get Helen back. First, ambassadors were sent to Troy demanding the return of Helen. Priam refused on the grounds that the Greeks still kept Hesione, his sister, whom Heracles had carried off.

这意味着战争。

That meant war.

并非所有英雄都愿意远行。其中一位极不情愿，便是伊萨卡岛的国王奥德修斯。他更为人熟知的名字是拉丁语版的尤利西斯。奥德修斯娶了海伦的表妹佩涅洛佩为妻，并刚刚生下儿子忒勒马科斯。他自然不愿离开家人。

Not all the heroes were willing to go. One, who was very reluctant to do so, was Odysseus, King of the island of Ithaca. He is better known by the Latin version of his name, Ulysses. Odysseus had married Penelope, a cousin of Helen, and had just had a little son named Telemachus. Naturally, he did not want to leave his family.

然而，奥德修斯以其智慧和机敏闻名，希腊人觉得他们必须得到他。他假装疯癫，但他们识破了他的伪装，迫使他遵守誓言。

However, Odysseus was famous for his intelligence and shrewdness and the Greeks felt they had to have him. He feigned madness but they were able to penetrate the pretense and forced him to stick to his oath.

预言家们也曾预言，希腊人若没有珀琉斯和忒提斯的儿子阿喀琉斯的帮助，就无法赢得战争。（这一切似乎都始于珀琉斯和忒提斯的婚礼，而且他们当时已经有一个到了参战年龄的儿子。更奇怪的是，特洛伊战争结束前，阿喀琉斯的儿子也已经到了参战年龄。然而，神话的创造者们似乎从未仔细计算过时间。）

It was also foretold by soothsayers that the Greeks could not win unless they had the help of Achilles, the son of Peleus and Thetis. (It seems strange that all this started at the wedding of Peleus and Thetis and that already they had a son of fighting age. What is worse is that before the Trojan War was over, a son of Achilles had reached fighting age. However, the mythmakers never counted the years too closely.)

作为忒提斯的儿子，阿喀琉斯注定要比他的父亲强大得多。事实也的确如此，因为除了赫拉克勒斯之外，他是希腊英雄中最著名的一位。此外，赫拉克勒斯的胜利是凭借超人的力量取得的。阿喀琉斯虽然力大无穷，但他被描绘成一位技艺高超的战士，而不是一个只会挥舞棍棒的闲散英雄。

It was Achilles who, as the son of Thetis, was destined to be far more powerful than his father. And he was, for next to Heracles he was the most famous of all Greek heroes. Moreover, the victories of Heracles were achieved through sheer superhuman strength. Achilles, while exceedingly strong, was pictured as a skillful fighter, not someone who simply laid about with a club.

阿喀琉斯出生时，忒提斯试图让他获得永生，便将他浸入冥河斯堤克斯。她抓住他的一只脚后跟，却忘记再次将他浸入水中，使脚后跟也沾湿。因此，她抓住他脚后跟的地方没有被冥河水浸透，那部分仍然会死。结果，在特洛伊战争中，他最终被一支射中脚后跟的箭射死，因为那是他唯一能被致命一击的地方。

When Achilles was born, Thetis tried to make him immortal by dipping him in the river Styx. She held him by one heel as she did this and forgot to dip him a second time in order to get the heel wet, too. The place where she held him therefore remained untouched by the water of the Styx and that part remained mortal. As a result, during the Trojan War, he was finally killed by an arrow in the heel, the one place where he could be mortally wounded.

因此，任何看似稳固的防守体系中的薄弱环节都被称为“阿喀琉斯之踵”。此外，连接小腿肌肉和跟骨的强韧肌腱被称为“阿喀琉斯之腱”。

For this reason, any weak point in an otherwise solid defense is called an “Achilles heel.” Furthermore, the strong tendon that connects the muscles of the calf of the leg with the heel bone is called the “Achilles tendon.”

忒提斯知道阿喀琉斯面临两种命运：要么留在家中，过着平静而漫长的生活；要么加入希腊军队，英年早逝，却能名垂青史。忒提斯希望他能长寿，于是她将他装扮成女孩，和其他女孩一起藏在斯基罗斯岛上。

Thetis knew that Achilles had a choice of two fates. He could either stay at home and lead a long, quiet life, or he could join the Greek forces and die young, but gain eternal fame. Thetis would rather he lived long so she dressed him as a girl and hid him on the island of Scyros with other girls.

听到传闻，奥德修斯率领希腊使团前往斯基罗斯岛，奥德修斯的机智开始发挥作用。搜查姑娘们有失体统，于是奥德修斯带去了衣物和珠宝作为礼物，其中还夹带了一把剑。姑娘们纷纷争相领取这些精美的礼物，但其中一位姑娘却忍不住拿起剑，用力挥舞，想试试它的平衡。当然，这位姑娘并非姑娘，而是阿喀琉斯，于是他加入了希腊军队。与他同行的还有他的挚友帕特罗克罗斯，以及珀琉斯麾下的一支军队——米尔米顿人。

Hearing rumors of this, Odysseus and a Greek delegation traveled to Scyros and here the wit of Odysseus began to pay dividends. It would have been unmannerly to search the girls. Instead, Odysseus brought presents of clothes and jewelry for the women but included a sword as well. The various girls seized upon the pretty presents, but one girl couldn’t resist seizing the sword and swinging it forcefully to test its balance. That girl, of course, was no girl at all, but Achilles, and so he joined the Greek forces. With him went his best friend, Patroclus, plus a group of Peleus’ army, the Myrmidons.

阿喀琉斯被证明是希腊最伟大的战士，如今任何伟大的战士都可以被称为阿喀琉斯。例如，在滑铁卢战役中击败拿破仑的威灵顿公爵，一度被称为“英格兰的阿喀琉斯”。同样，任何足智多谋的人都可以被称为“奥德修斯”。

Achilles proved to be the greatest of the Greek warriors and any great warrior may now be called an Achilles. For instance, the Duke of Wellington, who defeated Napoleon at Waterloo, was at one time called the “Achilles of England.” Similarly, any crafty man may be called an “Odysseus.”

许多加入军队的希腊英雄都是阿尔戈英雄的儿子。阿喀琉斯是阿尔戈英雄珀琉斯的儿子，奥德修斯是阿尔戈英雄拉厄耳忒斯的儿子。此外，还有两位名叫埃阿斯的英雄也加入了军队。（“埃阿斯”实际上是这个名字的罗马拼写，希腊语版本是Aias，发音为“ay'as”。）

Many of the Greek heroes who joined the forces were sons of Argonauts. Achilles was the son of the Argonaut Peleus, and Odysseus was the son of the Argonaut Laertes. In addition, two heroes named Ajax joined. (“Ajax” is actually the Roman spelling of the name, the Greek version being Aias, pronounced “ay’as.”)

其中一位身材魁梧，力大无穷，是阿尔戈英雄忒拉蒙之子，通常被称为大埃阿斯。另一位是阿尔戈英雄俄琉斯之子，通常被称为小埃阿斯。阿尔戈英雄提丢斯之子狄俄墨得斯也加入了队伍，并成为主要英雄之一。

One was a huge, strong man, the son of the Argonaut Telamon and usually called Ajax the Greater. The other was the son of the Argonaut Oileus and was usually called Ajax the Lesser. Diomedes, the son of the Argonaut Tydeus, also joined and proved one of the major heroes.

即使是克里特岛也依然强大到足以派遣一位重要的英雄伊多梅纽斯。

Even Crete was still powerful enough to send an important hero, Idomeneus.

其中一位英雄实际上是卡吕冬野猪狩猎的老兵。他就是皮洛斯城的国王涅斯托尔。特洛伊战争爆发时他已是耄耋之年（大概六十岁，在那个年代，普通男子三十岁就夭折了，这已是极高的年纪）。他不仅在战争中幸存下来，而且战后至少又活了十年，因此他去世时至少有八十多岁。传说中，他被描绘成一位智慧过人、阅历丰富的长者，他的建议总是中肯可取，因此“涅斯托尔”一词后来也用来指代任何一位睿智的老人。

One of the heroes was actually a veteran of the hunt of the Calydonian boar. This was Nestor, king of the city of Pylus. He was an old man at the time of the Trojan War (probably sixty, which was extremely old in an age when the average man was dead at thirty). He survived the war too and lived on at least ten years beyond it, so that he must have been at least in his eighties when he died. He is pictured in the legends as a man of wisdom and experience whose advice was always good, so that a “Nestor” has come to mean any wise old man.

总的来说，特洛伊传说中只提到了英雄人物，但也有少数次要人物在语言中留下了痕迹。荷马曾提到斯滕托尔，他是一位传令官，因其嗓音洪亮，足以震慑五十人，因此在鼓舞士气方面发挥了重要作用。

On the whole, only heroes are mentioned in the Troy legend but one or two minor characters have left traces in the language. At one point, Homer mentions Stentor as a herald who was useful at rallying the army because he had a voice as loud as fifty men.

因此，任何响亮的声音都被称为“洪亮的”，而吼猴的洪亮吼声在一英里甚至更远的地方都能听到，有时也被称为“喇叭猴”。当然，某些单细胞生物也被称为“喇叭猴”，它们完全不会发出任何声音，但形状却像扩音器，而扩音器自然与噪音联系在一起。

For this reason, anything loud is said to be “stentorian” and the howling monkey whose resonant bellow can be heard at a distance of a mile or more is sometimes called “Stentor.” The same name is given to certain one-celled creatures which make no noise at all, you may be sure, but which are shaped like megaphones which, of course, are associated with noise.

荷马再次将《伊利亚特》中提到的唯一平民特尔西忒斯描绘 成一个丑陋、懦弱的煽动者，总是对英雄们破口大骂。事实上，荷马赋予他的那段独白充满了智慧，但由于这些诗歌通常是唱给贵族听众听的，所以特尔西忒斯成了人们嘲讽的对象。除了荷马之外的其他诗人也把特尔西忒斯塑造成一个带有侮辱性的恶毒之人，如今“thersitical”一词也演变成了“大嗓门”或“下流”的意思。

Again, Homer pictures Thersites, the one commoner mentioned in the Iliad, as being an ugly, cowardly agitator always spewing curses at the heroes. Actually, the one speech Homer gives him is full of good sense, but the poems were usually sung to an aristocratic audience, so Thersites was made fun of. Poets other than Homer introduced Thersites as an offensive insulter and now “thersitical” has come to mean “loud-mouthed” or “scurrilous.”

特洛伊战争双方的众多英雄，至少其中一些，都可以在天空中找到。1906年，德国天文学家马克斯·沃尔夫发现了一颗距离太阳异常遥远的行星。它的运行轨道并非位于火星和木星之间，而是几乎与木星的轨道完全重合。这种行星的运行轨道非同寻常，正如我之前解释过的，这类行星通常会被赋予男性化的名字。沃尔夫将其命名为“阿喀琉斯”。

The various heroes, or at least some of them, on both sides of the Trojan War, are to be found in the heavens. In 1906, the German astronomer Max Wolf discovered a planetoid that was unusually far from the sun. The path it followed did not lie between that of Mars and Jupiter but seemed to be nearly identical with the path of Jupiter. This was an unusual orbit for a planetoid and such planetoids, as I explained before, received masculine names. Wolf called it “Achilles.”

如果从阿基里斯星到木星，再到太阳，最后回到阿基里斯星画一条线，这条线会构成一个等边三角形（即三边都相等的三角形）。天文学家知道这种情况是稳定的。也就是说，一个较小的天体与两个较大的天体距离相等，它就会始终以保持与这两个天体距离相等的方式运动。当木星绕太阳运行时，阿基里斯星也随之运动，几乎保持着相同的步长。

If a line is drawn from Achilles to Jupiter, then to the sun, then back to Achilles, it forms an equilateral triangle (that is, a triangle in which the three sides are all equal). Astronomers know that such a situation is stable. That is, a small body equally distant from two large ones would always move in such a way as to remain equally distant from them. As Jupiter moves about the sun, Achilles moves with it, keeping almost exact step.

同年，人们在木星轨道上发现了另一颗小行星，1907年又发现了两颗。之后又发现了更多。其中一些小行星与阿喀琉斯小行星聚集在一起，另一些则位于木星的另一侧，与木星和太阳构成第二个等边三角形。“阿喀琉斯”开创了先例，其余的小行星都以特洛伊战争的英雄命名。第二个被发现的小行星是位于木星另一侧的“帕特罗克罗斯”。之后，木星两侧又陆续发现了其他小行星。与阿喀琉斯一同被发现的还有“安提洛科斯”、“狄俄墨得斯”、“奥德修斯”、“阿伽门农”、“墨涅拉俄斯”、“涅斯托尔”、“埃阿斯”和“赫克托耳”。由于除赫克托耳之外的这些小行星都是希腊人，因此它们被称为“希腊阵营”。与帕特罗克罗斯一同被发现的还有“安喀塞斯”、“特洛伊罗斯”、“埃涅阿斯”和“普里阿摩斯”。除了帕特罗克罗斯之外，这些人都是特洛伊人，所以这里是“特洛伊营地”。

That same year another planetoid was discovered in Jupiter’s orbit, and in 1907 two more were discovered. Then still others. Some of them were discovered in a cluster with Achilles and some were discovered on the other side of Jupiter, forming a second equilateral triangle with Jupiter and the sun. “Achilles” set the precedent and the rest of these planetoids were named after heroes of the Trojan War. The second to be discovered was “Patroclus,” on the other side. Others followed on both sides. Along with Achilles are “Antilochus,” “Diomedes,” “Odysseus,” “Agamemnon,” “Menelaus,” “Nestor,” “Ajax,” and “Hector.” Since all these but Hector are Greeks, these are referred to as the “Greek camp.” Along with Patroclus are “Anchises,” “Troilus,” “Aeneas,” and “Priamus.” Since all of these but Patroclus are Trojans, this is the “Trojan camp.”

这两个阵营的成员统称为“特洛伊小行星”。此外，小天体与两个大天体等距的情况现在被称为“特洛伊情形”。迄今为止，除了这些小行星之外，还没有发现其他类似情况。

As a group, the members of both camps are called the “Trojan planetoids.” Furthermore, the situation where a small body is equidistant from two larger bodies is now called the “Trojan situation.” So far, no case is known other than the one involving these planetoids.

希腊军队在底比斯附近的海港奥利斯集结，准备起航。然而，事情一开始就不顺利，因为没有风。

The Greek army assembled at Aulis, a seaport near Thebes, and prepared to sail. However, matters began badly. There was no wind.

一位预言家告诉阿伽门农，只有将他最美丽的女儿献祭给阿尔忒弥斯，风才会刮起来。于是，阿伽门农派人给妻子克吕泰涅斯特拉送去一个谎言，让她把女儿伊菲革涅亚送来，以便他能把她嫁给阿喀琉斯。女儿到达后，他把她献祭了，风果然刮了起来。

A soothsayer told Agamemnon that the wind would rise only if he sacrificed his most beautiful daughter to Artemis. Agamemnon therefore sent a lying message to his wife, Clytemnestra, asking her to send him his daughter Iphigeneia in order that he might marry her to Achilles. When his daughter arrived, he sacrificed her and the wind rose promptly.

然而，从那一刻起，克吕泰涅斯特拉就对她的丈夫恨之入骨。

However, Clytemnestra hated her husband bitterly from that moment.

当希腊军队最终抵达特洛伊时，他们开始了漫长的围城战。特洛伊人坚守在坚固的城墙之后，城墙难以攻破。希腊人沿海岸线不断劫掠，试图用饥荒耗尽特洛伊人的兵力。双方僵持了九年之久。

When the Greek army finally reached Troy, they settled down to a long siege. The Trojans stayed behind their walls, which were too strong to be assaulted. The Greeks raided up and down the coasts, trying to starve out the Trojans. For nine years, there was a stalemate.

荷马史诗《伊利亚特》的著名故事始于围城战的第九年。剧情的关键事件是阿喀琉斯和阿伽门农之间因战利品分配而发生的争吵。

Homer’s Iliad begins its famous story in the ninth year of the siege. The key event in the plot was a quarrel between Achilles and Agamemnon, over the division of some of the spoils of war.

争吵过后，阿喀琉斯怒不可遏，与帕特罗克罗斯和密尔米顿人一起退出了战争。他待在帐篷里，拒绝战斗。他还请求母亲忒提斯劝说宙斯让希腊人战败，迫使他们前来求助，以此平息他的怒火。

As a result of the quarrel, Achilles in anger withdrew himself, together with Patroclus and the Myrmidons, from the war. He remained in his tent and refused to fight. Moreover he asked his mother, Thetis, to persuade Zeus to bring defeat upon the Greeks so that they would have to come begging to him for his help and thus soothe his ill-temper.

此后，特洛伊人走出城池，在赫克托耳的率领下开始击退希腊人，而阿喀琉斯则面带微笑。因此，任何人因感情受挫而放弃战斗，令战友失望，都会被认为“像阿喀琉斯一样在帐篷里生闷气”。

Thereafter, the Trojans emerged from their city and under the leadership of Hector began to drive the Greeks back, while Achilles smiled. As a result, anyone who abandons a fight because of hurt feelings, and lets his comrades down, is said to “sulk like Achilles in his tent.”

希腊人最终派人去恳求阿喀琉斯重返战场，但他拒绝了。希腊人被击退到船上，面临彻底的失败。这时，阿喀琉斯的朋友帕特罗克罗斯心生怜悯，恳求阿喀琉斯允许他穿上阿喀琉斯的盔甲代替他作战。阿喀琉斯同意了，帕特罗克罗斯便率领米尔米顿人投入战斗。

The Greeks eventually sent to Achilles to beg him to return to the fight, but he refused. The Greeks were driven back to their very ships and were faced with utter defeat. Then Patroclus, Achilles’ friend, filled with pity, begged permission of Achilles to put on Achilles’ armor and fight in his place. Achilles gave the permission and Patroclus led the Myrmidons out to battle.

帕特罗克罗斯一度击退了特洛伊人，特洛伊人起初以为是阿喀琉斯前来作战。然而，阿喀琉斯曾警告帕特罗克罗斯，他只能将特洛伊人赶回船上，绝不能攻击赫克托耳。帕特罗克罗斯被胜利冲昏了头脑，认为就连赫克托耳也无法与他匹敌。（他又一次狂妄自大了。）他向赫克托耳发起冲锋，结果被赫克托耳杀死（当然，这是报应）。

For a while, Patroclus drove back the Trojans who at first thought it was Achilles coming back to fight. Achilles had, however, warned Patroclus to do no more than drive the Trojans back from the ships and on no account to attack Hector. Patroclus, enchanted by his own success, felt that not even Hector could stand against him. (There was hubris again.) He charged Hector and was killed (nemesis, of course).

阿喀琉斯顿时怒火中烧，绝望不已。他本想立刻冲锋陷阵，却苦于没有盔甲。他迅速与阿伽门农结盟，从忒提斯那里得到了一套新的盔甲（由赫菲斯托斯亲自打造），然后才投身战场。

At once, Achilles was filled with rage and despair. He would have charged immediately into the fight but he had no armor. He made friends with Agamemnon rapidly, got a new set of arms (forged by Hephaestus himself) from Thetis, and then leaped into battle.

他像驱赶绵羊一样驱赶着特洛伊人，他们全都逃进了特洛伊城墙之内。只有赫克托耳留在城外，他羞于逃亡，决心与阿喀琉斯决一死战，即便为此牺牲也在所不惜。两人展开决斗，这场决斗（据荷马史诗所述）是文学史上最伟大的场景之一，赫克托耳最终战死沙场。

He drove the Trojans before him like sheep and all of them fled behind the walls of Troy. Only Hector remained outside, ashamed to flee, and determined to fight Achilles and die, if necessary. The two met in a duel which (as told by Homer) is one of the greatest scenes in all literature, and Hector was killed.

不久之后，阿喀琉斯被帕里斯用弓射出的箭射中脚后跟而死。

Shortly thereafter, Achilles was killed by an arrow in the heel from the bow of Paris.

两位希腊英雄争夺已故阿喀琉斯的盔甲，他们分别是奥德修斯和埃阿斯大帝。两人都声称自己为希腊事业做出了更大的贡献。最终，希腊人投票将盔甲授予奥德修斯，而埃阿斯大帝则绝望自尽。

Two of the Greek heroes competed for the honor of possessing the armor of the dead Achilles. These were Odysseus and Ajax the Greater, each of whom claimed to have done more for the Greek cause. The Greeks voted the armor to Odysseus, and Ajax the Greater killed himself in despair.

即使失去了两位最伟大的英雄，希腊人依然奋战不息。特洛伊人方面，赫克托耳已阵亡，帕里斯也很快死于菲罗克忒忒斯的箭下。菲罗克忒忒斯继承了赫拉克勒斯的毒箭。帕里斯此前因伤被留在希腊军队中，直到阿喀琉斯死后才重返战场。

Even with their two greatest champions dead, the Greeks fought on. As for the Trojans, Hector was dead, and Paris soon died as the result of an arrow from the bow of Philoctetes who had inherited Heracles’ poisoned arrows. He had been left behind when the Greeks sailed for Troy because of a wound he suffered, but he rejoined the Greek forces after Achilles died.

即使帕里斯死了，战争也没有结束。海伦只是嫁给了普里阿摩斯的另一个儿子德伊福布斯，战争继续进行。

But even with Paris dead, the war did not end. Helen was simply married to another son of Priam, Deiphobus, and the war went on.

神谕预言，只要帕拉斯神像（帕拉斯的雕像或半身像）留在城内，特洛伊城就不会陷落。（我在本书前面提到过帕拉斯神像。）狄俄墨得斯和奥德修斯乔装潜入特洛伊，偷走了帕拉斯神像，但特洛伊城依然屹立不倒。阿喀琉斯的儿子尼奥普托勒摩斯，又名皮洛士，此时加入了军队。希腊人深受鼓舞，因为他与阿喀琉斯如此相像，让他们感觉伟大的英雄复活了。

An oracle then suggested that Troy could not be captured as long as the Palladium (a statue or bust of Pallas) remained within its walls. (I’ve mentioned the Palladium earlier in the book.) Diomedes and Odysseus entered Troy in disguise and stole the Palladium, but still Troy did not fall. Achilles’ son, Neoptolemus, who was also called Pyrrhus, now joined the forces and the Greeks were much heartened, for he seemed so like Achilles that they felt their great champion had come back to life.

但特洛伊城仍然没有陷落。

But still Troy did not fall.

就在那时，奥德修斯灵光一闪，想出了发动战争的绝妙主意。他建议希腊人建造一匹巨大的空心马，能够容纳全副武装的士兵。然后，希腊人将这匹马留在特洛伊城外的平原上，把士兵们塞进马里，而其余的希腊战士则登上船只，驶向下一个海岬，在那里隐蔽起来。

It was then that Odysseus had the great inspiration of the war. He suggested that the Greeks build a huge, hollow horse, capable of holding armed men inside it. The Greeks were then to leave this horse standing on the field outside Troy and to fill it with armed men, while all the remaining Greek warriors were to get into their ships and sail away just beyond the next cape where they would lie hidden.

他们照做了。特洛伊人看到希腊人离开，对那匹马感到困惑。一个名叫西农的希腊人被留下来，假装是希腊军队的逃兵。他说希腊人绝望了，认为他们永远无法攻克特洛伊，已经扬帆回国了。那匹马是献给雅典娜的神像，如果特洛伊人把它带进城里，雅典娜就会保佑这座城市永远不会落入任何敌人之手。

This they did. The Trojans saw the Greeks leave and were puzzled by the horse. A Greek named Sinon, left behind, pretended to be a deserter from the Greek forces. He said the Greeks, in despair, had decided they would never conquer Troy and had sailed for home. The horse was an image dedicated to Athena and if the Trojans took it into the city, Athena would see to it that the city would never fall to any enemy.

特洛伊人欣喜若狂，开始将木马牵进城里。他们甚至拆毁了一部分城墙，以便开辟一个足够大的入口。

The Trojans gave themselves up to wild joy and set about bringing the horse into the city. They even broke down a portion of the walls to make an opening large enough.

特洛伊阿波罗祭司拉奥孔是唯一一个发出警告的特洛伊人。他说：“我惧怕希腊人，即便他们带着礼物而来。”（这句话后来成了一句谚语，意思是即使宿敌突然变得友好，也不要轻易相信他。）然而，站在希腊人一边的海神波塞冬派出一条巨蛇从海里杀死了他，因此特洛伊人确信拉奥孔错了。

A Trojan priest of Apollo, named Laocoon, was the only Trojan to warn against this. He said, “I fear the Greeks, even when they come bearing gifts.” (This has become a proverbial phrase meaning that one must not trust a long-time enemy even when he suddenly begins to seem friendly.) However, Poseidon, who was on the side of the Greeks, sent a serpent out of the sea to kill him and the Trojans therefore felt sure that Laocoon was wrong.

他们把马牵进了城里。当晚，特洛伊人狂欢了一天后正酣然入睡，城门上的活板门突然打开，希腊战士走了出来。他们放火焚城，开始屠杀，而一直藏在城外不远处的希腊舰队也返回了。

They brought the horse into the city and that night when the Trojans were sleeping after a day of gaiety, the trap door opened and the Greek warriors came out. They set fire to the city and began a slaughter, while the Greek fleet which had been hiding just out of sight returned.

尼奥普托勒摩斯杀死了普里阿摩斯最小的儿子，然后又杀死了普里阿摩斯本人。墨涅拉俄斯原本打算杀死海伦，但当他看到她的美貌时，便下不了手，把她带了回来。唯一逃脱的特洛伊重要人物是普里阿摩斯的亲戚，名叫埃涅阿斯。根据后来的罗马传说，他的后裔建立了罗马城。

Neoptolemus killed the youngest surviving son of Priam and then Priam himself. Menelaus was going to kill Helen, but when he saw her beauty he could not do it and took her back. The only important Trojan to escape was a relative of Priam’s family, named Aeneas. According to later Roman legends, his descendants founded the city of Rome.

受特洛伊传说的影响，任何潜入敌国腹地并等待战略时机发动攻击的组织都被称为“特洛伊木马”。近期最著名的例子是二战期间西方国家内部的某些叛徒。

As a result of this part of the Troy legend, any group that worms its way into the heart of enemy country and waits for a strategic moment to strike is called a “Trojan horse.” The most famous recent example was that of certain internal traitors in Western countries during World War II.

特洛伊战争结束后，幸存的希腊战士们踏上了返乡之路。他们大多历经艰辛，遭遇海难和种种不幸。阿伽门农回到迈锡尼的第一天就被妻子克吕泰涅斯特拉下令处死，因为克吕泰涅斯特拉始终无法原谅他献祭伊菲革涅亚的罪行。

When the Trojan War was over, the various Greek warriors who survived made their way home. For the most part they did so only with great difficulty, suffering shipwrecks and misfortunes. Agamemnon was killed the first day he arrived back in Mycenae by order of his wife, Clytemnestra, who had not forgiven him the sacrifice of Iphigeneia.

至于墨涅拉俄斯，他和海伦漂泊了七年才回到斯巴达。他们大部分时间都在埃及度过，等待顺风将他们带回希腊。在那里，他的舵手卡诺普斯去世了，他的名字被赋予了“船底座”星座中最亮的那颗星。因此，舵手在船上的位置可谓恰如其分。

As for Menelaus, he and Helen wandered for seven years before finding their way back to Sparta. Much of the time was spent in Egypt waiting for a favorable wind to carry them back to Greece. While there, his steersman, Canopus, died and his name was given to the brightest star in the constellation “Argo.” Thus a steersman was fittingly placed in a ship.

老人星是夜空中第二亮的恒星，但它位于天空的极南位置，因此在希腊（或者纽约）永远无法观测到。不过，在埃及，它位于南方地平线的远处，勉强可见。因此，希腊人很可能认为它代表着一位死于埃及而非希腊的神话人物。（事实上，古埃及确实有一座名为卡诺珀斯的城市，这颗星的名字可能就来源于此。）

The star Canopus is the second brightest star in the sky but is located so far south in the heavens that it is never visible in Greece (or in New York for that matter). It is just visible in Egypt, however, far down on the southern horizon so that the Greeks might well imagine it to represent a mythical character who died in Egypt rather than in Greece. (Actually, there was an ancient Egyptian city named Canopus and the star may be named for that.)

最终，墨涅拉俄斯抓住了海神普罗透斯。据说普罗透斯能告诉他如何返回希腊。普罗透斯拥有变形的能力，为了逃脱，他一会儿变成狮子，一会儿变成蛇，一会儿变成豹子，一会儿变成野猪，一会儿变成流水，一会儿变成树。然而，墨涅拉俄斯始终没有放弃，普罗透斯最终被迫说出了能够带来顺风的祭祀仪式。墨涅拉俄斯遵照指示，最终回到了斯巴达，从此他和海伦在那里和平地统治着斯巴达。

At last, Menelaus captured a sea god named Proteus who, he was told, could tell him how to get back to Greece. Proteus had the power of changing shapes and in an effort to escape changed into a lion, a serpent, a panther, a boar, running water and a tree. However, Menelaus held fast throughout and Proteus was finally forced to explain the exact sacrifices that would bring on a fair wind. Menelaus followed orders and finally got back to Sparta, where he and Helen ruled peacefully thereafter.

“变形虫”一词现在被用来指代任何能够改变形状的生物，例如单细胞动物变形虫。任何能够轻易改变主意或观点以适应环境的人，都被称为“善变的”。

“Proteus” is a name now applied to anything that changes its shape such as the one-celled animal called the amoeba. Anyone who changes his mind or his opinions easily, to suit circumstances, is said to be “protean.”

然而，在所有英雄中，返乡之旅最长、最著名的当属奥德修斯。荷马的第二部长诗就描述了这段旅程。

Of all the heroes, however, the one with the longest and most famous homeward journey is Odysseus. Homer’s second long poem describes it.

这首史诗几乎与《伊利亚特》一样伟大（甚至更受欢迎），它就是荷马的《奥德赛》。如今，任何漫长而复杂的旅程都被称为“奥德赛”。

The poem, almost as great as the Iliad (and even more popular), is Homer’s Odyssey. Any long and complicated journey is now called an “odyssey.”

奥德修斯在地中海长达十年的漂泊充满了奇幻色彩和童话故事。他最初停留的地方之一，是一片以莲花为食的土地。据说，凡是吃了莲花的人，都会忘记一切，只沉浸在莲花带来的愉悦之中，一心只想永远留在那里。奥德修斯的一些船员吃了莲花，结果被其他人强行拉回了船上。因此，那些生活安逸奢华、漠视责任的人，就被称为“食莲者”。当然，莲花本身完全是神话传说，但它的名字却被用来指代各种睡莲。

Odysseus’ ten-year wanderings all over the Mediterranean are filled with fantasies and fairy tales. One of his first stops was at a land where the inhabitants ate a fruit named the lotus. Anyone eating it forgot everything but the pleasure of eating it and wished only to stay in that land forever. Some of Odysseus’ men ate of the lotus and had to be forced back to the ships by the rest. For that reason, anyone who lives in ease and luxury and gives no thought to duty is called a “lotus-eater.” The plant itself is completely mythical, of course, but its name has been given to various water lilies.

奥德修斯和他的手下随后来到一座岛屿，岛上居住着巨大的独眼巨人。其中一个名叫波吕斐摩斯的独眼巨人是个食人怪物，他抓住了奥德修斯和他的十二名手下，把他们带到自己的洞穴里，开始吃他们，每次吃两个人。奥德修斯哄骗独眼巨人喝了酒，喝醉了。趁着独眼巨人睡着的时候，奥德修斯挖出了他的一只眼睛。然后，他把手下绑在波吕斐摩斯羊群的底部。瞎眼的独眼巨人放羊过去，自己则守在洞口等着奥德修斯和他的手下，他们就这样逃了出来。

Odysseus and his men next came to an island on which dwelt huge Cyclops. One of them, a man-eating monster named Polyphemus, captured Odysseus and twelve of his men, brought them to his cave and began to eat them, two at a meal. Odysseus tricked the Cyclops into drinking wine and getting drunk. While the Cyclops was sleeping, Odysseus succeeded in putting out his eye. He then tied his men to the bottom of the sheep of Polyphemus’ flock. The blind Cyclops let the sheep pass while waiting at the cave opening for the men, and so they escaped.

由于这个故事的名声，“polypheme”有时被用来表示巨人，一种非常大的蛾子也被命名为“波吕斐摩斯蛾”。

Because of the fame of this story, a “polypheme” is sometimes used to mean a giant and one very large moth has been given the name of “Polyphemus moth.”

波吕斐摩斯是海神波塞冬的儿子，此后，波塞冬不断降下风暴追赶奥德修斯。最终，奥德修斯抵达一座岛屿，岛上居住着风神埃俄罗斯。埃俄罗斯给了他一个装满各种风的袋子，唯独没有西风。如果奥德修斯将袋子封好，就只会吹西风，而西风就能带他回到伊萨卡岛。

Polyphemus was a son of Poseidon and after that, Poseidon was always sending storms after Odysseus. Finally, Odysseus reached an island on which lived Aeolus, god of the winds. Aeolus gave him a bag filled with all the winds but the west wind. If Odysseus kept the bag tightly closed, only the west wind would blow and that would bring him home to Ithaca.

他们已经能看到伊萨卡岛了，甚至还能看到岸边燃烧的篝火冒出的缕缕青烟。疲惫不堪的奥德修斯睡着了。他的船员们也同样疲惫，他们觉得那个神秘的袋子（他们实在无法相信里面除了风什么也没有）或许装的是酒。他们打开袋子，风立刻全都飞了出去。随之而来的暴风雨将船只吹向茫茫大海，他们彻底迷失了方向。

They were within sight of Ithaca and could even see the smoke of burning fires on the shore when the exhausted Odysseus fell asleep. His men, also tired, thought the mysterious bag (which somehow they could not believe contained nothing but wind) might contain wine. They opened it and, at once, all the winds flew out and in the storm that followed, the ships were driven far off to sea, hopelessly lost.

文中多处提及风神埃俄罗斯。例如，“风琴”指的是一种琴弦乐器，风吹过时会发出柔和的嗡嗡声。风吹来的沙子被称为“风沙”。甚至还有一个词“aeolistic”（充满空话的）来形容夸夸其谈。

There is reference to Aeolus in several words. An “aeolian harp,” for instance, is an arrangement of strings which will hum with soft tones when the wind blows through it. Sand carried by the wind is called “aeolian sand.” There is even a word “aeolistic” which is used to describe talk that is full of “hot air.”

埃俄罗斯被认为是风神之父，每一种风都对应着一位不同的半神。玻瑞阿斯是狂暴的北风，因此我们今天所说的“boreal”（北方的）一词就源于此。对希腊人来说，最温和的风是西风。它既不带来北方的寒冷，也不带来南方沙漠的酷热，而是轻柔地吹拂。希腊语中西风的名字是泽菲罗斯（Zephyrus），如今任何轻柔的微风都被称为“zephyr”（西风）。

Aeolus was considered the father of the winds, each of which was a different demigod. Boreas was the rough north wind and our word “boreal” means “north” in consequence. The kindest of the winds was the west wind to the Greeks. It did not bring cold from the north or heat from the southern deserts but blew gently and mildly. The Greek name for the west wind was Zephyrus and now any gentle, mild breeze is called a “zephyr.”

接下来，奥德修斯遭遇了另一群食人巨人——莱斯特律戈涅斯人，他们摧毁了奥德修斯除一艘船外的所有船只。奥德修斯和他仅存的一艘船来到一座名为埃埃亚岛的岛屿，岛上住着一位名叫喀耳刻的美丽女巫，她是太阳神赫利俄斯的女儿。她能把人变成动物，显然是为了取乐，她把奥德修斯的船员变成了猪。（因此，任何看似令人愉悦但却能使人像野兽一样行事的东西，例如酒，都被称为“喀耳刻之物”。）

Odysseus next underwent a disastrous meeting with another set of man-eating giants, the Laestrygones, who destroyed all but one of Odysseus’ ships. Odysseus and his one remaining ship came to an island called Aeaea on which lived a beautiful enchantress named Circe who was the daughter of Helios. She could change men into animals, apparently for amusement, and changed Odysseus’ men into pigs. (As a result, anything which seems pleasant but which has the power to make men behave like beasts, as liquor will, for instance, is called “Circean.”)

奥德修斯本人之所以能免疫喀耳刻的魔法，是因为赫尔墨斯给他带来了一种名为“莫利”（moly）的花，闻到这种花的气味就能得到保护。这种植物完全是神话传说中的，但它的名字却被用来指代某些蒜科植物。（的确，大蒜的气味或许能让美女们打消对你施咒的念头。）

Odysseus himself was immune to Circe’s spells because Hermes had brought him a flower called “moly” which would give him protection when he smelled it. The plant is entirely mythical but the name has been given to certain plants of the garlic family. (It is true that a garlic odor may keep beautiful women from trying to enchant you.)

奥德修斯强迫喀耳刻将他的船员们变回原形，然后在岛上待了一段时间，作为航行之余的愉快休息。

Odysseus forced Circe to change his men back into their natural shape and then stayed a period of time on the island, as a pleasant rest from his voyages.

最终，他觉得必须想办法回家，于是听从了喀耳刻的建议，前往冥界，向一位已故的预言家寻求回家的最佳方法。（神话创造者们喜欢让他们的英雄造访冥界。忒修斯、俄耳甫斯和赫拉克勒斯都曾到访过冥界。）

Eventually, he felt he had to try to get home again and, on Circe’s advice, he traveled to Hades to get the advice of a dead soothsayer on how best to do this. (It was a favorite device of the mythmakers to have their heroes visit Hades. Theseus, Orpheus and Heracles had all visited Hades.)

成功完成任务后，奥德修斯驶过塞壬的领地。他让手下用蜡塞住耳朵，以免他们听到塞壬的歌声。他自己虽然听见了歌声，但先让手下把他绑在桅杆上，并让他们发誓无论他如何哀求都不要放了他。尽管他苦苦哀求，他们还是没有放了他。

After accomplishing that successfully, Odysseus sailed past the Sirens. He protected his men by having their ears filled with wax so that they could not hear the Siren song. He himself listened to it but had his men tie him to the mast first and made them promise not to release him however hard he begged. And though he did beg, they did not release him.

塞壬女妖之一帕耳忒诺珀，因未能诱捕足智多谋的奥德修斯而悲愤不已，纵身跃入大海自尽。她的遗体被海浪冲到那不勒斯城，因此古代诗人有时将那不勒斯称为“帕耳忒诺珀”。这个名字在近代再次出现。1799年，法军攻占那不勒斯，建立了一个共和国。这个共和国只存在了几个月，但在它存在期间，它被称为“帕耳忒诺珀共和国”。

One of the Sirens, named Parthenope, was so upset at this failure to trap the shrewd Odysseus that she threw herself into the sea to her death. She was washed ashore at the city of Naples, which was therefore sometimes called “Parthenope” by ancient poets. The name cropped up again in modern times. In 1799, French troops captured Naples and established a republic. It only lasted a few months, but while it did it was called the “Parthenopean Republic.”

离开塞壬之后，奥德修斯接下来要穿过两座悬崖。其中一座悬崖上潜伏着一个名叫斯库拉的怪物，她是厄喀德娜的女儿之一。她有着人类的躯体，却长着六个长长的脖子，每个脖子的末端都是一个狂吠的狗头。另一座悬崖下则是盖亚的女儿，名叫卡律布狄斯的怪物。她每天三次吸入海水，形成可怕的漩涡，任何被困其中的东西都会被摧毁。

Having left the Sirens, Odysseus had next to pass between two cliffs. On one cliff, there lurked a monster called Scylla, another of the children of Echidna. She had a human body out of which grew six long necks at the end of each of which was a yapping dog’s head. Below the other cliff was a monstrous daughter of Gaea called Charybdis. Three times a day she sucked in water, making a terrible whirlpool that would destroy anything trapped in it.

奥德修斯当时必须决定从哪座悬崖边经过。从此以后，任何需要在两个同样可怕的危险之间做出的选择，都被称为“进退两难”。（人们认为斯库拉和卡律布狄斯位于分隔西西里岛和意大利的狭窄的墨西拿海峡。当然，那里并没有什么怪物，虽然确实存在某种漩涡。即便如此，意大利一侧的一块岩石至今仍被称为“斯库拉”，而西西里一侧的一块岩石则被称为“卡律布狄斯”。）

Odysseus had to decide near which cliff to pass. Ever since, any choice that must be made between two dangers equally bad is said to put someone “between Scylla and Charybdis.” (Scylla and Charybdis are assumed to have been located in the narrow Strait of Messina that separates Sicily and Italy. Of course no monsters exist there, though there is a whirlpool of a sort. Still, a rock on the Italian side is called “Scylla” even now, while one on the Sicilian side is called “Charybdis.”)

回到奥德修斯的故事——他决定与其冒着整艘船被卡律布狄斯吞噬的风险，不如冒险穿越斯库拉的魔爪。结果，他失去了六名船员，每人都被这头犬头怪物的利齿所吞噬。

Getting back to Odysseus—He decided that rather than risk losing the entire ship to Charybdis, he would run the gantlet of Scylla. In doing so, he lost six men, one to each set of jaws of the dog-headed monster.

随后，他登陆了太阳岛，太阳神赫利俄斯在那里饲养着成群的牛（“太阳之牛”）。喀耳刻曾警告他不要吃这些牛，但由于海面平静，时间流逝，船员们饥饿难耐，不顾奥德修斯的命令，宰杀并食用了这些牛。结果，当船只最终再次起航时，海神波塞冬得以掀起一场风暴，摧毁了最后一艘船。只有奥德修斯一人幸存，因为他没有吃过这些牛。

He then landed on the island of the sun, where Helios kept herds of cattle (the “cattle of the sun”). Circe had warned him not to eat these, but as they were becalmed and time passed, the crew grew desperate with hunger and against Odysseus’ orders killed and ate. Consequently, when the ship finally set sail again, Poseidon had full permission to raise a storm that destroyed the last ship. Only Odysseus himself, who had not eaten of the cattle, survived.

孤身一人漂流的奥德修斯被冲到奥吉吉亚岛，仙女卡吕普索将他囚禁了七年。然而，他实在太想家了，卡吕普索最终同意放他走。他造了一艘木筏，乘船前往斐阿刻亚人的土地。斐阿刻亚人都是技艺精湛的水手，最终将他带回了伊萨卡。

Alone and adrift, Odysseus was cast ashore on the island of Ogygia where a nymph, Calypso, kept him prisoner for seven years. He was so homesick, however, that finally Calypso agreed to let him go. He built a raft which carried him to the land of the Phaeacians. These were skilled sailors and they brought him back to Ithaca at last.

在伊萨卡登陆时，他是所有二十年前和他一起前往特洛伊的人，以及十年前和他一起离开特洛伊回家的人中唯一的幸存者。

On Ithaca he landed as the only survivor of all those who had left with him for Troy twenty years before and had left Troy for home with him ten years before.

在奥德修斯归来之前的这段时间里，他已去世的谣言自然在伊萨卡岛传开。因此，岛上及周边地区的一百多位贵族试图说服佩涅洛佩嫁给他们中的一位，以便让此人成为新的国王。

During the lapse of time before Odysseus’ return, the rumor had naturally spread in Ithaca that he was dead. Over a hundred noblemen of the island and surrounding territories therefore tried to persuade Penelope to marry one of them, in order that that one might become the new king.

佩涅洛佩拒绝了他们的请求，因为她忠于奥德修斯，她确信他仍然活着，总有一天会回来。（如今，她的名字被用来象征贤良忠贞的妻子，正如克吕泰涅斯特拉的名字被用来象征不忠的妻子一样。）

Penelope put them off because she was faithful to Odysseus who, she was certain, was still alive and would someday return. (Her name is used today as the model of a modest and faithful wife, just as Clytemnestra’s name is used as the model of an unfaithful one.)

佩涅洛佩不得不耍些小伎俩。例如，她谎称自己必须先为奥德修斯年迈的父亲拉厄耳忒斯织好寿衣，才能嫁人。她开始织寿衣，但每天晚上都会把白天织好的部分全部拆掉。因此，任何永远也织不完的活儿都被称为“佩涅洛佩之网”。（“网”的意思当然是“织成的东西”。）

Penelope had to resort to tricks. For instance, she explained she would have to weave a shroud for Odysseus’ old father, Laertes, before she could marry anyone. This job she began, but each night she would unravel whatever she had done during the day. For that reason any job which seems never to get done is called a “web of Penelope.” (“Web” of course, means “something that is woven.”)

最终，一个不忠的侍女向求婚者们揭露了奥德修斯的阴谋，他们变得咄咄逼人。奥德修斯的幼子忒勒马科斯当然是王位的真正继承人，但他却无能为力。他唯一能依靠的人是奥德修斯留下的一位老顾问。这位老顾问名叫门托尔（Mentor），如今“门托尔”一词指任何顾问或咨询师。“监视者”（Monitor）一词也指同样含义的人，或者指负责预警危险的人。

Finally, the trick was revealed to the suitors by a disloyal maid and they grew threatening indeed. Odysseus’ young son, Telemachus, was, of course, the actual heir to the throne, but he was powerless. The only one he had to help him was an old adviser left behind by Odysseus. This old adviser was named Mentor and “mentor” now means any adviser or counselor. “Monitor,” meaning the same, or someone whose job it is to warn of approaching danger, is another form of the word.

忒勒马科斯担心自己会被杀害，而且除非奥德修斯回家，否则他看不到任何摆脱困境的办法。于是，他听从门托尔的建议，决定去拜访父亲的一些老朋友，看看他们是否有奥德修斯的消息。他去了斯巴达，在那里遇到了墨涅拉俄斯和海伦，他们已经平安地结束了自己的漂泊之旅。他还拜访了涅斯托尔，涅斯托尔虽然比以前老了很多，但仍然健在，他是所有希腊人中唯一一个平安归来的人。

Telemachus, fearing he would be killed and seeing no way out of his troubles unless Odysseus came home, decided, on the advice of Mentor, to visit some of his father’s old friends to see if they had news of him. He visited Sparta, where he met Menelaus and Helen, now safely back from their own wanderings. He also visited Nestor, who was still alive, though older than ever, and who was the only one of all the Greeks who had returned home without trouble.

然而，奥德修斯杳无音讯，他垂头丧气地返回了家乡。不过，这次旅行的目的正是为了让他远离求婚者们密谋杀害他的阴谋，等他回来时，奥德修斯已经登陆了。

However, there was no news of Odysseus, and he returned home downhearted. Yet the trip had served the purpose of keeping him away during a period when the suitors planned to murder him and by the time he was back, Odysseus had landed.

奥德修斯受到了心地善良的养猪人欧迈俄斯的款待，欧迈俄斯并没有认出他。奥德修斯装扮成乞丐，混入求婚者们宴饮的大厅里的仆人中，以便了解情况。

Odysseus was fed and treated well by Eumaeus, a goodhearted swineherd who did not recognize him. Odysseus dressed as a beggar and mingled with the servants in the halls where the suitors feasted in order to see how matters stood.

此时，佩涅洛佩的计谋已经用尽，只好答应嫁给任何一位能够拉开奥德修斯旧弓并准确射中目标的求婚者。（她只希望没有一位求婚者能匹敌奥德修斯的英雄力量。）

By this time, Penelope had been forced to the end of her trickery, so she agreed to marry whichever suitor could draw Odysseus’ old bow and shoot an arrow accurately at a mark with it. (She could only hope that none of the suitors could match Odysseus’ heroic strength.)

诚然，每个求婚者都没能拉开弓。那个假扮乞丐的人（此时他已经向欧迈俄斯和忒勒马科斯表明了自己的身份）请求试一试，欧迈俄斯和忒勒马科斯开玩笑地让他试了试。他轻松地拉开了弓，于是他和忒勒马科斯，以及欧迈俄斯和其他几个忠诚的仆人，出其不意地袭击了求婚者，将他们全部杀死。

To be sure, each suitor failed to pull the bow. The supposed beggar (who had by now revealed himself to Eumaeus and Telemachus) asked the chance to try and, as a joke, was allowed to do so. He drew the bow easily, whereupon he and Telemachus, together with Eumeaus and a few other faithful servants, caught the suitors by surprise and killed every one of them.

奥德修斯和佩涅洛佩终于重逢。奥德修斯年迈的父亲拉厄耳忒斯帮助他与求婚者的父亲们达成和解，《 奥德赛》的故事就此结束。

Odysseus and Penelope were reunited at last. Odysseus’ old father, Laertes, helped make peace with the fathers of the suitors and the Odyssey comes to an end.

结语

EPILOGUE

特洛伊战争结束后，流传着许多关于战争英雄之子们的传说。其中最著名的故事是关于阿伽门农之子俄瑞斯忒斯的。他为父报仇，杀死了自己的母亲克吕泰涅斯特拉。为此，他遭到复仇女神厄里倪厄斯的追杀，直到在雅典得到净化。

THERE ARE SOME LEGENDS concerning the days after the Trojan War and of the sons of the heroes of that war. The most famous involve Orestes, the son of Agamemnon, who avenged his father by killing his mother, Clytemnestra. For this crime, he was hounded by the Erinnyes (or Furies) until he was purified in Athens.

赫米奥娜是墨涅拉俄斯和海伦的女儿，也是他们的独生女。她先嫁给了阿喀琉斯的儿子尼奥普托勒摩斯，尼奥普托勒摩斯死后，她又嫁给了俄瑞斯忒斯。最后，奥德修斯的儿子忒勒马科斯娶了喀耳刻，也就是他父亲曾经认识的那位女巫。

Hermione, the daughter and only child of Menelaus and Helen, first married Neoptolemus, the son of Achilles, and, after his death, she married Orestes. To round things off, Telemachus, the son of Odysseus, married Circe, the enchantress his father once knew.

然而，阿开亚王国在特洛伊战争结束后不到一个世纪就灭亡了，当时一群未开化的希腊人入侵了希腊，他们此前一直居住在希腊北部。

The Achaean kingdoms, however, came to an end within a century of the Trojan War, when there was a new invasion by an uncivilized set of Greeks who till then had lived to the north of Greece.

这次新的入侵是由一群被称为多利安人的希腊人发起的。大约在公元前1100年，他们摧毁了迈锡尼和梯林斯，并在希腊各地永久定居下来。多利安人拥有铁制武器，而阿开亚人只有青铜武器，这些武器既不坚固也不有力，根本无法与之抗衡。

This new invasion was by a group of Greeks called the Dorians who, about 1100 B.C., destroyed Mycenae and Tiryns and established themselves permanently in various parts of Greece. The Dorians had iron weapons, and the Achaeans, with bronze weapons only, which were neither as tough nor as strong, could not stand up against them.

在希腊传说中，多利安人是在赫拉克勒斯后裔的带领下进入希腊的。赫拉克勒斯的儿子们（“赫拉克勒斯人”）曾被逐出希腊，但他们的孙子们返回故土，击败了俄瑞斯忒斯的儿子，并征服了他们声称是赫拉克勒斯合法拥有的所有土地。

In the legends, the Greeks described the Dorians as coming into Greece under the leadership of the descendants of Heracles. The sons of Heracles (“Heraclids”) had been driven out of Greece, but their grandsons returned, defeated the son of Orestes and conquered all the lands they claimed were Heracles’ by right of conquest.

为了解释多利安人是如何占领他们所占领的所有城市的，后来的神话创造者编造了赫拉克勒斯征服众多不同城市的故事。

In order to explain how the Dorians came to take over all the cities they did take over, later mythmakers made up tales which had Heracles conquering a large number of different cities.

一些亚该亚人留了下来，其中最著名的是雅典人。其他人则逃往小亚细亚，在那里建立了许多希腊城邦。在接下来的几个世纪里，这些城邦比希腊本土更加文明。当时的希腊正处于多利安人的统治下，经历了一段野蛮时期。

Some Achaeans remained, notably the Athenians. Others fled to Asia Minor, where they established many Greek cities which for a couple of centuries were more civilized than Greece itself which, under the Dorians, passed through a barbarous period.

然而，自从多利安人征服希腊之后，传说时代就真正结束了。幻想和童话、神祇、英雄和怪兽的金色光芒逐渐消逝，平淡的历史的白昼缓缓降临。

However, once the Dorians conquered Greece, the age of legend was really over. The golden blaze of fantasy and fairy tales, of gods and heroes and monsters gradually died away and the flat daylight of ordinary history slowly dawned.

然而，青铜时代并未完全消亡，因为它留下了永不磨灭的故事遗产，这些故事至今仍是我们文学和生活的一部分。即便有一天无人阅读这些故事，我们的英语语言中依然会蕴藏着对那个神祇与英雄时代的记忆。

And yet the Bronze Age did not die altogether for it left behind it a never-to-be-forgotten heritage of stories, which remain part of our literature and life. Even if it ever happened that no one read the stories, our English language would still contain within its words the memory of those days of gods and heroes.

希腊人对此有一个专门的词……

THE GREEKS HAD A WORD FOR IT…

希腊神话中的天空之神是乌拉诺斯（Ouranos）。罗马人后来称他为天王星（Uranus）。十八世纪，一位天文学家以这位强大的神祇的名字命名了一颗新发现的行星……而一位化学家则以这颗行星的名字命名了一种新发现的金属，他称之为铀（uranium）。

The Greek god of the sky was Ouranos. The Romans later called him Uranus. In the eighteenth century, an astronomer named a new planet after this mighty deity… and a chemist, in turn, named a newly discovered metal after the planet. He called it uranium.

正如作家艾萨克·阿西莫夫所指出的那样，“最古老的希腊神祇仍然生活在一个与最新、最可怕的科学武器相关的世界里。”

As author Isaac Asimov points out, “the oldest of the Greek gods still lives in a word connected with the newest and most dreadful of scientific weapons.”
